Change-Log: (The markers like "2026-03-08" are dates, so DON'T count them up but always use TODAY as the correct marker and always add the latest chang to the TOP of the file )
===========

2026-08-01 (8)
- Fixed: `/admin Execute Nightly Maintenance` shared the same 90-minute migration budget
  (`CONFIG.history_migration_time_budget_minutes`) as the fire-and-forget scheduled 03:00 UTC task,
  via the common `run_nightly_maintenance_routine()`. Wrong for `/admin` specifically — it's an
  interactive, user-awaited command whose job is the maintenance steps (checkpoint/VACUUM/REINDEX/
  ANALYZE), not migration (the opportunistic per-cycle chunk already carries that automatically),
  and a long migration wait risks the ~15-min Discord interaction token expiring before the reply
  sends. `run_nightly_maintenance_routine()` gained an optional `migration_time_budget_seconds`
  param (defaults to the scheduled task's existing 90-min value, unaffected); `/admin` now passes
  a new, separate `CONFIG.history_migration_admin_budget_minutes` (env
  `HISTORY_MIGRATION_ADMIN_BUDGET_MINUTES`, default 1 min).
  Files: QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py, qapbot/config.py, README.md, qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  1474 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (7)
- Added: opportunistic per-cycle history migration chunking, on top of today's other migration
  fixes. Operator's idea — the update cycle already sleeps for the unused remainder of
  SLEEP_INTERVAL after each cycle (~4-4.5 min idle out of 5 min by default); instead of leaving
  that idle, spend it on the migration too when there's still backlog, using the exact same
  time-budget machinery already built today. Replaced the old rare "standalone safety-net" branch
  in `periodic_main()` with one that fires every cycle (not just once/process/month), bounded to
  the new `CONFIG.history_migration_cycle_chunk_minutes` (env `HISTORY_MIGRATION_CYCLE_CHUNK_MINUTES`,
  default 4) via the same asyncio.create_task()/db_maintenance_idle_event pattern already in use —
  a strict superset of what the old safety net covered.
  Trade-off (documented, not hidden): while real backlog remains, Discord commands are blocked
  roughly chunk_minutes out of every SLEEP_INTERVAL cycle instead of once nightly — but total
  backlog completion time drops by roughly an order of magnitude in exchange, and it's
  self-limiting (costs nothing once the migration reports done, which routine future months should
  hit within the first cycle or two). Set the env var to 0 to disable and keep only the once-a-night
  cadence.
  Also documented (no code change): a stale `bot_metadata` "done" stamp written by the pre-fix code
  earlier tonight was blocking every automatic path from re-triggering even after today's fixes
  were deployed — cleared manually via `DELETE FROM bot_metadata WHERE key = 'last_history_migration';`.
  Files: QapBot.py, qapbot/config.py, README.md, qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  1474 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (6)
- Fixed: `CacheManager.save_war_object()` (`qapbot/cache_manager.py`) wrote temp war JSON files
  directly via `open(target_file, "w")`, which truncates to 0 bytes before any new content is
  written — a disk-full or crash mid-write destroyed the previously-good file with nothing valid
  left in its place. Confirmed as the cause of several corrupted temp war files found post-incident
  (`[TEMP-WAR-LOAD] Failed to read ...` — some 0-byte, some truncated mid-JSON). Now writes to a
  sibling `.tmp` file and `os.replace()`s it into place atomically, matching the existing pattern in
  `persist_cwl_recovery_file()` (`QBhelperfunctions.py`) — a failed write now leaves the existing
  good file untouched instead of corrupting it. `manage_war_files()`'s existing Rule 3 (archive any
  file that still fails to parse) is unchanged and remains the safety net for genuine corruption.
  Files: qapbot/cache_manager.py, qapbot/docs/WAR_FILE_MANAGEMENT_RULES.md. 1474 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (5)
- Fixed: a continuously-running bot would NOT automatically advance a chunked history migration one
  night at a time as expected — it would only ever auto-fire the migration ONCE per calendar month
  (whenever first claimed, typically immediately via the restart safety-net), then silently do
  nothing further until day 1 of the following month, no matter how much backlog remained. Root
  cause: the scheduled 03:00 UTC nightly task already runs every night (no day restriction), but its
  migration-due check used the default `is_monthly_migration_due()`, gated on `day == 1` AND an
  in-memory once-per-process claim. Found by directly answering an operator's question during the
  ongoing 2026-08-01 incident recovery ("will it just do one chunk per night with no intervention?").
  Fix: the scheduled path now calls `is_monthly_migration_due(ignore_in_process_claim=True)` (added
  earlier today for `/admin`) — safe because the surrounding block already throttles to once/night
  via `_maint_due`. The rare standalone safety-net fallback deliberately keeps the default (claiming)
  check, since it has no other throttle and bypassing it too would make it re-fire every ~5-minute
  cycle instead.
  Net effect: a bot left running now genuinely completes a large migration backlog one bounded chunk
  per night, fully unattended, on top of the `/admin`-triggered manual option from earlier today.
  Files: QapBot.py. 1474 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (4)
- Fixed: `/admin Execute Nightly Maintenance` couldn't actually be used to advance a chunked
  migration on demand, despite being wired to call it. `is_monthly_migration_due()` claims the
  month in-memory the moment it returns True, and only fires once per bot-process lifetime — so if
  the automatic safety-net had already claimed it earlier in the same process (very likely, it
  fires within the first cycle after any restart while backlog remains), a later `/admin` invocation
  would silently skip the migration step with no error, only running the VACUUM/REINDEX/ANALYZE
  part. Found while answering an operator question during the ongoing 2026-08-01 (1)-(3) incident
  recovery about using `/admin` instead of restarting the bot between chunks.
  Added `ignore_in_process_claim: bool = False` to `is_monthly_migration_due()` — bypasses both the
  `day == 1` gate and the in-memory claim, re-deriving purely from persisted `bot_metadata`.
  `QBdiscordcmds.py`'s `/admin` handler now passes `ignore_in_process_claim=True`; the two automatic
  paths (nightly window, standalone safety-net) are unchanged, so they still only auto-fire once per
  process per month. Operators can now repeatedly trigger `/admin` to push a large migration forward
  in bounded chunks while the bot keeps running normally in between.
  Files: QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py, tests/unit/test_is_monthly_migration_due.py (new, 4 tests).
  1474 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (3)
- Added: `monthly_history_migration()` can now be time-budgeted and chunked instead of having to
  finish in one sitting. Live incident recovery (2026-08-01 (1)/(2)) hit a second blocker after the
  disk-full fix: the real backlog (~22.9M+ rows remaining) projected to ~10h40m even at healthy SSD
  throughput — far too long to keep a live bot's Discord commands blocked. New `time_budget_seconds`
  param (threaded through `_migrate_table_batch_by_date`/`_migrate_cwl_table_by_season` as a
  `deadline`) stops cleanly, not as an error, once the budget is spent, returning a
  "[HIST-MIGRATE] PARTIAL" result; the "mark done" gate is now a real `fully_completed` boolean
  built from each table's `(moved, completed)` return, not a string-prefix check (the old
  `not result.startswith("ERROR")` approach would have wrongly treated PARTIAL as success). Also
  added `batch_size`/`checkpoint_every_batches` as CLI-tunable knobs on
  `run_history_migration_now.py` for a faster one-off recovery run.
  New `CONFIG.history_migration_time_budget_minutes` (env `HISTORY_MIGRATION_TIME_BUDGET_MINUTES`,
  default 90) bounds the *automatic* trigger paths in QapBot.py too — without this, simply
  restarting the bot mid-backlog would re-trigger an unbounded run via the standalone safety-net
  task within the first cycle.
  Also fixed a test-authoring gotcha found while covering this: monkeypatching `_time.monotonic`
  (where `_time` is `import time as _time`, the same object as the real `time` module) also hijacks
  asyncio's own event-loop clock, corrupting the test. Added an isolated `_monotonic` module-level
  reference used only by the deadline checks.
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/scripts/run_history_migration_now.py, qapbot/config.py,
  QapBot.py, README.md, qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md,
  tests/unit/test_history_migration_time_budget.py (new). 1470 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (2)
- Fixed: `run_history_migration_now.py` and `run_db_maintenance_now.py` ran silently with zero
  progress output — neither script (nor anything they import) configures a logging handler, so
  every `logging.info()` call inside `monthly_history_migration()`/`nightly_db_maintenance()`
  (including the per-batch "[HIST-MIGRATE] moved batch of 5000 rows" progress lines) was silently
  dropped. Found live during tonight's incident recovery (2026-08-01 (1)) — a multi-hour resumed
  migration run produced no visible output, making it impossible to tell slow-but-working apart
  from stuck. Both scripts now call `logging.basicConfig(level=INFO, ...)` with the same
  `%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s` format QapBot.py's own logging uses.
  Files: qapbot/scripts/run_history_migration_now.py, qapbot/scripts/run_db_maintenance_now.py.
  1467 tests pass.

2026-08-01 (1)
- Fixed: prod disk-full incident during tonight's first-ever `monthly_history_migration()` run
  (8.3M+ row backlog). Root cause: the migration disables `wal_autocheckpoint` on both DB schemas
  for its entire duration and only checkpoints once at the very end — fine for a short run, but
  this first run took ~4h45m and never checkpointed once, growing both WAL files unbounded until
  the volume hit 0 bytes free (287.9 GB + 103.8 GB of WAL). `_migrate_table_batch_by_date()` now
  runs a non-blocking `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)` every 20 batches (~100K rows) to bound WAL
  growth regardless of total migration size.
  Also fixed a second bug that blocked automatic recovery: `monthly_history_migration()` persisted
  `last_history_migration` as done even when the run errored out partway, which would have
  silently suppressed any retry for the rest of the month. That write is now gated on actual
  success — the migration was already naturally resumable, so this just stops the false "done"
  marker from hiding that.
  Restored `qapbot/scripts/run_history_migration_now.py` (an on-demand CLI to force-run just the
  migration, bypassing the day==1 gate) to resume tonight's interrupted run once disk space is
  freed — also corrected a stale claim in CLAN_AND_WAR_CYCLE_ARCHITECTURE.md that
  `run_db_maintenance_now.py` already covered this (it never did; `nightly_db_maintenance()` has no
  migration step).
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/scripts/run_history_migration_now.py (new),
  qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md, qapbot/docs/CLAN_AND_WAR_CYCLE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  1467 tests pass.

2026-07-30 (6)
- Fixed (final): reworked bot startup ordering instead of special-casing the composite-index
  build again. (5) below deferred `idx_wa_player_tag_date` creation to `nightly_db_maintenance()`
  to avoid the "database is locked" storm from (4) — technically correct, but it meant a fresh
  deploy wouldn't get the leaderboard perf benefit until the next nightly run (or a manual
  `/admin Optimize DB`), and split "ensure schema is correct" across two places for one index.
  Root insight: the *only* reason building this index inline was ever unsafe was that other code
  was already writing to the DB concurrently (PHASE-1 clan fetches) by the time it ran. Fix that
  at the source: sequence DB init strictly BEFORE anything else can touch the DB, not around it.
  - Added `QapBot.initialize_database()`: calls `WarHistoryDB.initialize()` under its own generous
    30-minute `asyncio.wait_for` (not the 60s CoC-login one) and sets
    `QBcore.db_maintenance_mode=True` for the duration (same flag `nightly_db_maintenance()` uses
    — blocks Discord commands with the existing friendly message; Discord's gateway connection is
    already up by `on_ready()`, so the bot still shows online).
  - `on_ready()` now calls this as a new "Step 1.5", strictly before Step 2 (CoC login) and before
    `periodic_main()` can start — so nothing else in the app can be writing to the DB while a rare
    first-run schema migration builds. `startup_login()` no longer does DB init at all (split
    cleanly into auth-only).
  - `idx_wa_player_tag_date` moved back inline into `_create_schema()`/`_create_history_schema()`
    (main + history) — same as any other index, now safe because of the ordering above, not
    because of any special-casing. Every restart after the first is a cheap IF-NOT-EXISTS check;
    logs the build time only when it exceeds 5s so a normal restart stays quiet.
  - Removed `nightly_db_maintenance()`'s "Step 1.5" create-if-missing block from (5) — redundant
    now that `initialize()` always ensures it exists; maintenance's existing REINDEX-if-exists
    loop (which already includes `idx_wa_player_tag_date` in `major_indexes`) is enough.
  - `_create_history_schema_sync()`'s `build_expensive_indexes` flag stays (defensive: pool-fill
    runs synchronously on the event-loop thread and must never attempt this build itself, even
    though in the current ordering `_create_schema()`/`_create_history_schema()` always beat it to it).
  Files: QapBot.py, qapbot/db_manager.py.
  Docs: qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  Tests: 1467 passed. pyright: 0 errors (QapBot.py, qapbot/db_manager.py).

2026-07-30 (5) — SUPERSEDED by (6) above, kept for the incident trail
- Fixed: the background-task fix in (4) below solved the startup timeout but caused a worse,
  live incident — right after the fixed bot came up, PHASE-1's clan-fetch writes started failing
  with "database is locked" (`[DB-WRITE-THROUGH] Failed to persist clan ...`), because
  `CREATE INDEX` holds SQLite's single writer lock for its *entire* build duration regardless of
  which thread runs it. `asyncio.to_thread` only kept the build off the event loop — it did
  nothing to stop it from blocking every other write in the app for however long the build took.
  There is no way to make a large `CREATE INDEX` non-blocking for other writers in SQLite; the
  only real question is whether that lock window is coordinated or not.
  Fix: removed the fire-and-forget background task entirely.
  `initialize()` now only does a cheap read-only existence check and logs a warning if the index
  is missing (`_warn_if_composite_index_missing()`) — never a write. The actual `CREATE INDEX` was
  moved into `nightly_db_maintenance()`'s existing Step 2 (new "Step 1.5"), which already
  `self._pool.drain()`s (waits out in-flight writers, blocks new ones with a clean
  `RuntimeError` instead of a raw `OperationalError`) and sets `QBcore.db_maintenance_mode=True`
  (blocks Discord commands) before touching anything — the one place in this codebase a
  multi-minute exclusive write is already coordinated and safe. Runs automatically on the next
  scheduled nightly maintenance, or immediately via `/admin Optimize DB` /
  `qapbot/scripts/run_db_maintenance_now.py` if you don't want to wait.
  Removed `WarHistoryDB._build_composite_indexes_background()` and the now-unused
  `_composite_index_build_task` attribute from (4).
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py.
  Docs: qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  Tests: 1467 passed (up from 1464; +3 net new — replaced the background-task test with one
  proving initialize() builds nothing, plus one proving nightly_db_maintenance() does).
  pyright: 0 errors.

2026-07-30 (4) — SUPERSEDED by (5) above, kept for the incident trail
- Fixed: bot failed to start on prod ("Startup login timed out after 60 seconds") — a direct
  regression from (3) below. `idx_wa_player_tag_date` was created inline inside
  `_create_schema()`/`_create_history_schema()`, awaited directly inside `initialize()`, which
  itself runs inside `startup_login()`'s 60s login timeout. Its FIRST build on the multi-
  million-row prod `history.war_attacks` table is a full table scan + sort that takes minutes,
  not seconds — every restart after the first would have been instant (`IF NOT EXISTS` is a
  cheap check), but that first build alone blew the login budget and the bot never came up.
  Fix: stopped building this index inline on the awaited startup path entirely. Added
  `WarHistoryDB._build_composite_indexes_background()`, scheduled via `asyncio.create_task`
  (fire-and-forget, NOT awaited) at the end of `initialize()` — builds the index on a dedicated
  `sqlite3` connection via `asyncio.to_thread`, using the same server-machine-safe pragmas as
  `run_nightly_maintenance()` (temp_store=FILE, 128 MB cache — avoids the OOM-kill risk of the
  hot-path pragmas' 8 GB mmap during a large external sort). Also added a
  `build_expensive_indexes` flag to `_create_history_schema_sync()` (the sync/pool-fill helper),
  defaulting True for tests/migrations but passed False from `_SyncConnectionPool._create_conn()`
  — pool-fill runs synchronously on the event-loop thread, so it must never attempt this build
  itself (that would freeze the whole process, not just time out a coroutine). Until the
  background build finishes, scope="all" leaderboard queries simply fall back to the slower
  `idx_wa_player_tag` single-column index — a temporary perf cost, never a correctness issue.
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py.
  Docs: qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  Tests: 1466 passed (up from 1464; +2 new). pyright: 0 errors.

2026-07-30 (3)
- Fixed: `/leaderboard` with the new `scope="all"` default took minutes to respond on prod
  instead of the sub-second DB query expected. Two causes, both fixed:
  1. `get_player_attack_history_sync()`'s `player_tag IN (...) AND date BETWEEN ...` query only
     had a single-column `idx_wa_player_tag` index to work with, so SQLite had to rowid-fetch
     every historical row for each player (across all time) just to filter down to one month.
     Added composite index `idx_wa_player_tag_date(player_tag, date)` (kept the old single-column
     index rather than dropping it — DROP INDEX on the ~5.6M-row war_attacks table is nightly-
     maintenance-only, never safe to run unconditionally at every connection open/startup).
  2. The real dominant cost: `QBdiscordcmds.leaderboard()` resolved scope="all" rosters by
     `await`ing `CACHE.coc_clan_cache.get_clan()` one clan at a time in a for-loop. With no
     clan/family filter (the common case — a channel's full set of subscribed clans/families),
     a cache miss/expiry means a live CoC API call per clan, sequentially — that's what actually
     produced the multi-minute delay. Switched to a single `asyncio.gather(...,
     return_exceptions=True)` batch across every distinct constituent clan (same pattern already
     used by ui_clan_management.py's guild-clan refresh).
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py (composite index ×3 schema sites + major_indexes REINDEX list),
  QBdiscordcmds.py (parallel roster gather).
  Docs: qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.
  Tests: 1464 passed (up from 1462; +2 new). pyright: 0 errors.

2026-07-30 (2)
- Added: `/leaderboard` now auto-highlights the invoking user's own registered player(s) in the
  rendered table — their row is bolded/colored via ANSI escape codes inside a ` ```ansi ` Discord
  code block instead of a plain ` ``` ` block. Plain code blocks ignore all markdown/ANSI, and
  `**bold**` markdown doesn't work inside a code block at all, so this is the only way to
  highlight a row without breaking the table's monospace column alignment. Caveat: some older
  mobile Discord clients don't render the ANSI codes and show the raw escape sequence as text
  instead — there's no way to detect the client from the bot side, so this is an accepted
  trade-off rather than something worth working around.
  `render_leaderboard()` gained a `highlight_player_ids` param (formatting.py); wraps the
  matching row(s) via `_ANSI_HIGHLIGHT`/`_ANSI_RESET`, restricted to style="discord" only.
  `generate_leaderboard_text()` threads it through; QBdiscordcmds.leaderboard() resolves the
  caller's player_tag(s) from `CACHE.user_accounts`. All leaderboard message fences in
  `_split_and_post_leaderboard_helper()` switched from ` ``` ` to ` ```ansi ` (harmless no-op
  when nothing is highlighted).
  Files: qapbot/formatting.py, QBhelperfunctions.py, QBdiscordcmds.py.
  Docs: qapbot/docs/CODE_STRUCTURE.md.
  Tests: 1462 passed (up from 1457; +5 new tests for the highlight rendering). pyright: 0 errors.

2026-07-30 (1)
- Added: `/leaderboard` new `scope` option ("all" default / "own") — "all" credits a player
  currently rostered in a target clan for wars fought while registered to a clan that is no
  longer tracked/subscribed (e.g. they switched clans mid-month); "own" restores the previous
  per-clan-only behavior. New `get_player_attack_history_sync()` in db_manager.py queries
  war_attacks by player_tag across all clan_tags (uses the existing idx_wa_player_tag index —
  cost scales with roster size, not total history volume, so this shouldn't meaningfully slow
  leaderboard generation even for large families).
- Changed: `/leaderboard`'s `month` argument now accepts a range ("6-7"), a semicolon list
  ("1;3;5"), or a trailing count ("-2" = last 2 months) in addition to a single month number.
  The trailing-count form can cross a year boundary (e.g. "-2" in January covers December of
  the previous year + January) — `generate_leaderboard_text()` / `calculate_leaderboard()` now
  aggregate over explicit (month, year) pairs instead of a month list + one shared year, so this
  is computed correctly instead of just being unsupported.
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py (get_player_attack_history_sync, get_war_summaries_sync clan_tag
  now optional), QBhelperfunctions.py (calculate_leaderboard/generate_leaderboard_text scope +
  period-pairs support, new parse_month_argument/_load_history_rows/_format_periods_label
  helpers), QBdiscordcmds.py (/leaderboard command wiring), qapbot/translations/{en,de}.json.
  Docs: qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md, qapbot/docs/CODE_STRUCTURE.md.
  Tests: 1457 passed (up from 1425; +32 new tests for month parsing and scope="all"). pyright: 0 errors.

2026-07-26 (19)
- Fixed: `reconstruct --refresh-missing` never persisted what it fetched. `_refresh_groups_live`
  called get_clan() live for each "no fresh evidence" group's safe-rank candidate but only used
  the result in-memory for that run's decision — clans.war_league / last_checked_via_api in the
  DB were never updated. Symptom the project owner hit on prod: running --refresh-missing twice
  in a row re-fetched the exact same 597 clans live both times (confirmed correct — 0 corrections
  needed either time, the math checked out exactly: 590 of 597 confirmed already-correct, 7
  revealed genuine safe-rank disagreement), silently wasting the second run's entire API budget
  since nothing about those clans could have changed between the two calls seconds apart. Fixed:
  every successful live fetch now also UPDATEs clans.war_league + last_checked_via_api, so a
  repeat run (or the live bot's own routine polling) doesn't pay for the same fetch again.
  Also fixed a separate UX gap this exposed: `reconstruct` returned completely silently when
  `would_fix` was empty — correct behavior (nothing to write) looked identical to a run doing
  nothing at all. Added an explicit "No corrections needed" message for that case.
  Tests: 1425 passed (unchanged). pyright: 0 errors.
  Files changed: qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py (_refresh_groups_live persistence,
  reconstruct's empty-would_fix message).

2026-07-26 (18)
- Fixed: investigated all 19 of (17)'s "disagreement" Champion League II groups by hand —
  100% cleanly matched the newly-discovered Champion League I (4, 1) pattern (ranks 1-4
  promoted, 5-7 unchanged, rank 8 demoted). Root cause confirmed exactly: these groups
  actually played Champion League I; the old bug picked the one *demoted* (rank 8) clan as
  its wrongful "representative", writing that clan's post-season Champion League II onto the
  whole group. Corrected all 19 (152 rows) via `fix`.
  Updated `reconstruct`'s safe-rank band from a single conservative universal (3, 2) to
  per-league where we have empirically-measured evidence: new `_TRUSTED_LEAGUE_PROMO_RULES`
  dict (Gold League I, Crystal League III/I, Master League III/II/I, Champion League III/II/I
  — exactly the leagues `evaluate-promo-rules` actually saw real data for) with the
  conservative band retained as fallback for everything else, including Titan/Legend
  (deliberately excluded — still inconclusive). Re-ran `reconstruct` with the tightened band:
  disagreement dropped 274 -> 208, and it caught 8 *more* corrections the old blanket band had
  missed entirely (7 more "wrong demoted representative" cases, one tier further up the ladder
  — Champion League I mislabeled as the true Titan League III, confirmed by the identical
  rank-pattern check; 1 Master League II/III case). Applied all 8. Confirmed stable
  (`would be fixed: 0` on re-run); disagreement count changed slightly (208 -> 215) purely
  because 7 of the 8 newly-corrected groups landed on Titan League III, which isn't in the
  trusted table, so they fall back to the imprecise conservative band on re-evaluation — the
  DB values are correct, the script just can't self-confirm Titan-tier yet (consistent with
  (17)'s finding).
  Also: gave the corrected prod `fix` command as a single line instead of a multi-line
  `for...do...done` with backslash continuations — the latter can't be reliably pasted into
  an interactive SSH terminal (many clients send each line as its own command as soon as a
  newline is seen, breaking a continued statement).
  Tests: 1425 passed (unchanged). Files changed: qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py
  (_TRUSTED_LEAGUE_PROMO_RULES, _safe_rank_band, reconstruct's docstring).

2026-07-26 (17)
- Added: `evaluate-promo-rules` subcommand to `qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py` —
  empirically measures real CWL promotion/demotion counts per league (Supercell has never
  published these). For every fully-ranked, complete-data 8-clan group in a season whose
  league_rank is already known-correct, compares each clan's final rank against its CURRENT
  live league (fetched right after that season's promotions applied), aggregated per
  (league, rank) across the whole season for real sample sizes instead of 1-2 examples.
  Findings from season 2026-07 (26,467 groups; see `changelog.txt` (12)-(15) for why
  league_rank is trustworthy ground truth for this season specifically):
  - Gold League I through Champion League III: huge samples (hundreds to 5,000+ clans per
    league) cleanly confirm the existing (2, 2) assumption at ~100% consistency.
  - **Corrected** `_CWL_PROMO_RULES_FROM_2026_05["Champion League I"]` from (2, 2) to (4, 1)
    — n=66, unambiguous: ranks 1-4 100% promoted, ranks 5-7 ~89% unchanged, only rank 8
    (~89%) demoted, not the bottom two. Matches the two "disagreement" examples investigated
    with the project owner just before this.
  - **Left unchanged, genuinely inconclusive** (not a code fix — needs more data over time):
    Titan League I/II/III and Legend League show a much messier signal than every other
    league — even top-ranked clans show only a ~35/65 split between promoted and unchanged
    (every other league is ~100/0 at its rank boundary). Initially read this as "doesn't
    follow a rank-based model at all" (e.g. a broader trophy/medal-pool mechanic like Legend
    League's own global system) — walked that back after the project owner pointed out a
    simpler explanation and cross-checking confirmed it's plausible: 97.8% of these groups'
    recorded league_rank was never independently verified by `reconstruct` (all fell into
    "disagreement" under the old, too-conservative safe-rank band) — but that alone doesn't
    prove it, since Champion League I *also* had a 0% confirmation rate yet still gave a
    clean (4, 1) signal. Titan/Legend is a small, rarely-independently-verified population,
    so pre-fix historical corruption has had the most opportunity to accumulate there
    uncorrected — the likelier explanation, but not provable from today's data alone. Left
    at the old placeholder values; revisit with evaluate-promo-rules once league_rank
    populated entirely under the (15)-era only-at-group-creation logic has accumulated
    for a season or two.
  - **Separately flagged, NOT a rule question**: ~5% of Champion League II groups (19 of 371
    sampled) show 6-7 of 8 members jumping to a much higher league, including two-tier jumps
    (Champion II -> Titan III) a single normal promotion can't produce. This looks like
    leftover corrupted league_rank data the (12)/(15) fixes didn't catch — i.e. these 19
    groups' recorded "Champion League II" is itself probably wrong — not a promotion-rule
    quirk. Needs manual spot-checking of specific clans' in-game CWL history, not a code fix.
  Tests: 1425 passed (unchanged — no promo-rule value was asserted by any existing test).
  pyright: 0 errors.
  Files changed: qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py (evaluate_promo_rules, LEAGUE_LADDER),
  QBhelperfunctions.py (_CWL_PROMO_RULES_FROM_2026_05: Champion League I corrected + extensive
  comment documenting the empirical basis and the two open questions).

2026-07-26 (16)
- Fixed: clan-select dropdown in `/clan management`'s War Notifications and Registration
  Management screens was pre-selecting the wrong clan. Root cause: switching mode carried over
  `self.clan_tag` unconditionally, which on the very first switch from Basic Configuration (the
  landing mode, which has no clan dropdown) still held `get_most_active_clan_for_guild`'s pick —
  an arbitrary clan by subscription count, not related to the dropdown's alphabetical order — so
  it could land anywhere, including the last option, looking like a stuck/wrong default.
  Fix: in `ClanManagementView._on_mode_select`, when entering "registrations" or "notifications"
  from a mode without a clan selector (config/roles/families), recompute the default as the
  alphabetically-first clan (same sort `_add_clan_select` uses) instead of reusing the carried
  `clan_tag`. Switching directly between the two clan-scoped screens still preserves whatever
  clan the user had selected, since that transition skips the reset.
  Files changed: qapbot/ui_clan_management.py (_on_mode_select).

2026-07-26 (15)
- Fixed: root-cause fix for the CWL league_rank bug (7)/(11)/(12)/(13)/(14), replacing the
  `.state`-based gate with a correct-by-construction one. Prompted by the project owner asking
  why this happened in the first place and pointing out the actual fix: a league group's
  members are, by definition, all in the same league the moment the group is first discovered
  — so league_rank only ever needs deriving once, at that moment, using whichever member's
  cache is already fresh or (cheaply) one targeted get_clan() call if none are.
  The real defect: `cache_manager._process_league_group_response` had no notion of "first time
  seeing this group" vs. "re-processing an already-recorded one" — the same "trust any member's
  current league" logic ran on *every* fresh get_league_group() response, gated only on whether
  the API's live `.state` currently reported the season as active. That gate worked but treated
  a symptom, not the disease, and needed increasingly complex machinery (staleness windows,
  season-boundary arithmetic) to stay correct.
  Fix: added `db_manager.cwl_group_exists(group_id, cwl_season)` — a cheap indexed lookup,
  checked BEFORE `upsert_cwl_league_data` so it reflects pre-insert state. league_rank
  population (raw_data check, then fresh clan_name_cache entry, then one targeted get_clan()
  call) now only ever runs when this reports the group is genuinely new. An already-known
  group's league_rank is never touched again by this function afterwards, regardless of API
  state — update_cwl_group_stats's self-heal ((13)/(14)) remains the only other path that can
  correct it later, and only via a verified safe-rank cross-check.
  Kept the 8-day cache-staleness check on the fallback (a cached value can be stale from long
  before this brand-new group even without any season-timing concern — that's a general
  cache-freshness question, unrelated to the season-divergence bug this whole fix line was
  chasing). Removed entirely: the `.state` inspection, the season-window arithmetic, and the
  "season already ended, skipping" log line — none of it is reachable anymore since population
  never runs on an already-known group regardless of what state the API reports.
  Replaced `tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py` (tested the now-removed `.state`
  gate; 6 tests) with `tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_new_group_gate.py` (9 tests: new-group
  population from cache/API/raw_data, existing-group never-repopulates including the exact
  divergent-members production shape, and cwl_group_exists checked before the upsert).
  Tests: 1425 passed, 5 deselected. pyright: 0 errors.
  Files changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (_process_league_group_response),
  qapbot/db_manager.py (cwl_group_exists new), tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_new_group_gate.py
  (new, replaces test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py), qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.

2026-07-26 (14)
- Fixed: two follow-up gaps found while verifying (13)'s self-heal against real prod logs.
  1. Self-heal never got a chance to run for a group whose league_rank was *never* populated
     during its active season (still empty/NULL) — it only lived in update_cwl_group_stats'
     cwl_ended=1 short-circuit, not the initial "compute fresh" branch that first sets
     cwl_ended=1. Since the background auto-post loop (QapBot.py) skips calling
     update_cwl_group_stats again once cwl_ended is observed True, an actively-subscribed
     clan reached only through that loop would never get a second chance — league_rank would
     stay empty forever unless someone happened to run /leaderboard cwlinfo/cwlgroup manually
     for that exact clan. Fixed by factoring the self-heal application (log + persist with
     force=True + reflect in rows) into a shared `_apply_cwl_self_heal` helper and calling it
     from both branches — the short-circuit (as before) and the initial computation, right
     after all_ended is determined for the first time.
  2. `_process_league_group_response`'s "league_rank still unknown" log line (added in (7))
     was misleading: it fires purely based on THIS call's local derivation attempt coming up
     empty, regardless of whether the DB already has a correct value from earlier in the
     season (the common, harmless case) or is genuinely still empty (the rarer, real gap —
     now closed by fix 1 above). Observed on prod: "group 9457c3866f02bfcc ... league_rank
     still unknown" for a group whose league_rank was in fact already correctly "Crystal
     League III" in the DB — alarming-looking but harmless, since the guard only prevents
     *this call* from writing, it never touched the existing value. Reworded the message to
     be accurate (doesn't claim the DB value is unknown) and downgraded it from INFO to DEBUG
     since it's an expected, routine occurrence for any ended group being reprocessed, not a
     problem signal on its own.
  Added 2 tests to tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_self_heal.py: self-heal firing on the very
  first all_ended computation (reproduces the exact gap), and confirming it's NOT attempted
  while all_ended is still False (group_rank isn't final yet, so "safe rank" doesn't apply).
  Tests: 1422 passed, 5 deselected. pyright: 0 errors.
  Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (_apply_cwl_self_heal new, update_cwl_group_stats),
  qapbot/cache_manager.py (_process_league_group_response log message),
  tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_self_heal.py.

2026-07-26 (13)
- Added: permanent self-heal for cwl_league_groups.league_rank, closing the gap the (7)/(11)/
  (12) fixes and one-time repair left open — a frozen (cwl_ended=1) row's league_rank was never
  re-examined again, so any row corrupted before the write-time fixes landed would stay wrong
  forever without the manual `reconstruct` script.
  `QBhelperfunctions._cwl_self_heal_league_rank(rows, cwl_season, recorded_league, now=None)`
  (new): reuses the same league-independent "safe middle rank" reasoning validated in (12)'s
  `reconstruct` command — a clan ranked outside the top-3/bottom-2 band of its group (no
  promotion/demotion rule version seen in _CWL_PROMO_RULES_* moves anyone further) is
  guaranteed to have stayed in the same league the following season. If such a clan's current
  live league in clan_name_cache disagrees with the group's frozen league_rank, and the cache
  entry was refreshed within a bounded window after the season ended (roughly day 12 of the
  season's month through day 12 of the *next* month — past that, "current" league may already
  reflect a LATER season, not this one), the frozen value is corrected. In-memory only, no API
  or extra DB calls beyond the one write when a correction actually fires.
  Wired into `update_cwl_group_stats`'s existing `cwl_ended=1` short-circuit (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  — runs every time an ended group's standings are served (i.e. every /leaderboard cwlinfo /
  cwlgroup render), which is also the only point league_rank is ever shown to a user.
  `db_manager.update_cwl_league_rank` gained a `force: bool = False` parameter — the self-heal
  calls it with `force=True` to bypass the `cwl_ended` freeze guard for this one, already-
  verified corrective path; the original derivation path (_process_league_group_response) is
  unaffected and still frozen by the guard as before.
  Added `tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_self_heal.py` (11 tests): the pure self-heal function's
  window/band/disagreement/staleness logic, plus its wiring into update_cwl_group_stats
  (correction persisted with force=True; no-op leaves league_rank untouched).
  Tests: 1420 passed, 5 deselected. pyright: 0 errors on all 4 changed/added files.
  Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (_cwl_self_heal_league_rank, update_cwl_group_stats),
  qapbot/db_manager.py (update_cwl_league_rank), tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_self_heal.py
  (new), qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.

2026-07-26 (12)
- Added: `reconstruct` subcommand to `qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py` — bulk repair
  for cwl_league_groups.league_rank across a whole CWL season, without the ~200,000 live API
  calls a blanket clan refresh would cost.
  Context: found that season 2026-07 has 211,688 cwl_league_groups rows / 26,467 distinct
  groups, but only 6 groups had ever had group_rank computed (that only happens when a clan
  is actively queried via /leaderboard cwlinfo/cwlgroup — the other ~26,461 groups' league_rank
  values, however wrong, have never been shown to any user). The (7)/(11) fixes only stop new
  corruption; they don't repair what's already wrong.
  Approach (a clan finishing a "safe" middle rank — conservatively outside the top 3 / bottom 2
  of its group, a league-independent bound derived from every promotion/demotion count seen in
  QBhelperfunctions._CWL_PROMO_RULES_* — is guaranteed to have stayed in the same league the
  next season):
    1. Compute group_rank/total_stars for every group in the season directly from war_summary
       (already fully populated regardless of who queries a group) — zero API calls.
    2. For a safe-rank clan, cross-check its CURRENT clans.war_league against the recorded
       league_rank, but only trust it if last_checked_via_api is on/after a caller-supplied
       --fresh-after cutoff (the date this season's promotions/demotions applied) — otherwise
       the "current" league could itself reflect a LATER season's outcome.
    3. Where the fresh, safe-rank evidence disagrees with what's stored, correct it.
  Actively-subscribed clans refresh every cycle and passively-tracked Master III+ clans refresh
  every ~12-22h (see fetch_clan_war_data's TTL gate + CLAN_WAR_TRACKING.md's tier model), so
  clans.war_league is very likely already fresh for exactly the leagues this bug hit — meaning
  most of the season was resolvable with NO new API calls at all.
  Added `--refresh-missing`: for the remaining groups with no fresh cached evidence, fetches
  ONE safe-rank clan per group live (not all 8, and not the other ~20k irrelevant clans) via
  CONFIG.coc_email/coc_password, which already auto-select DEV/PROD credentials for whichever
  machine the script runs on.
  Validated against known-correct answers before trusting it: correctly reconstructed both the
  (7) and (11) incidents ("Champion League III" / "Master League III") from scratch. Spot-
  checked a sample of its "disagreement" bucket (cases where multiple safe-rank clans in a
  group disagreed on current league) and confirmed it correctly declines to guess — traced to
  incomplete per-war data skewing that clan's computed rank, not a flaw in the method.
  Results for season 2026-07 (data/qapbot.db, applied): offline pass found 6,465 confirmed
  corrections (0 API calls) + 7,081 already-correct; --refresh-missing resolved 957 of the
  961 remaining candidates for 939 more corrections (5 clans had no live match — deleted/
  private). Total: 7,404 groups corrected. Remaining unresolved: 11,686 groups with incomplete
  war_summary data (can't be ranked at all — a separate, unrelated data-completeness problem,
  not fixable by any API refresh) and 274 flagged as disagreement (left untouched).
  Writes a TSV audit trail (`cwl_reconstruct_<season>.tsv`, gitignored) before any write.
  Tests: 1409 passed, 5 deselected (script has no automated tests — one-time DB tool).
  Files changed: qapbot/scripts/audit_cwl_league_rank.py, .gitignore (ignore the TSV report).

2026-07-26 (11)
- Fixed: confirmed and fixed a second, related instance of the 2026-07-26 (7)/(10) CWL
  league_rank bug for #2JYQ909PC "The Marines II" (group `e29562f5ac3ddc68`, season "2026-07"),
  found via the (10) audit and independently confirmed against the in-game war log (Kriegsliga-
  Verlauf screen), which shows the clan played July's CWL in "Meister-Liga III" (Master League
  III), not the recorded "Crystal League I".
  This exposed that the 2026-07-26 (7) fix was incomplete: the real flaw isn't just `cwl_ended`
  lag, it's that both the `raw_data` lookup and the `clan_name_cache` fallback pick a single
  "representative" clan's *current* league and write it for the whole group. That's only valid
  while the season is still active — all 8 group members share one league only by construction
  (grouping happens at that shared league). Once the real season ends, promotions/demotions
  apply almost immediately and members diverge to *different* current leagues (some promoted,
  some demoted, some unchanged) — so picking any single one is a coin flip. Confirmed root
  cause here: the two bottom-of-group clans (#292VRCJVU, #2QVGCQQPV, ranks 7-8, demoted after
  July) had "Crystal League I" as their current live league by the time the fallback ran, and
  that value got written for all 8 clans in the group — including the 6 that were promoted or
  stayed unchanged at "Master League III"/"Master League II".
  Fix: `cache_manager.py`'s `_process_league_group_response` — moved the `.state` gate (added in
  (7)) so it also covers the `raw_data` lookup, which previously ran completely unconditionally
  regardless of season state (only the `clan_name_cache` fallback was gated before). Now neither
  source is trusted once the league group reports anything other than "preparation"/"inWar".
  Data correction: corrected `league_rank` from "Crystal League I" to "Master League III" for
  all 8 clans in group `e29562f5ac3ddc68` / season "2026-07" in `data/qapbot.db`, using the
  (10) audit script (`fix e29562f5ac3ddc68 2026-07 "Master League III" --yes`).
  Added 3 tests to `tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py`: a divergent-group-members
  reproduction of this exact incident, plus two covering the newly-gated `raw_data` path
  (populates normally during an active season, skipped once the season has ended).
  Tests: 1409 passed, 5 deselected.
  Files changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (_process_league_group_response),
  tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py, qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.

  as "wrong," almost all false positives, meaning that table doesn't track real CWL promotion
  outcomes closely enough to use as ground truth. No reliable blanket detector exists; the

2026-07-26 (7)
- Fixed: `/leaderboard cwlgroup` could show a clan's post-promotion league instead of the
  league it actually competed in for the requested CWL season. Reported case: "The Marines"
  (#2J00L8YRU) competed in Champion League III for CWL 2026-07 but the generated standings
  image showed "Champion League II" (their league *after* the post-season promotion).
  Root cause: `cache_manager.py`'s `_process_league_group_response` falls back to
  `clan_name_cache` to learn a clan's league (the `get_league_group()` API response doesn't
  include `warLeague` per clan). This fallback was gated on the DB's own `cwl_ended` column
  via `update_cwl_league_rank`'s SQL guard (`league_rank IS NULL OR cwl_ended = 0`) — but
  `cwl_ended` only flips once `update_cwl_group_stats_batch` observes every clan's expected
  war count in `war_summary`, which can lag days behind the real-world season end. Clans get
  promoted/demoted almost immediately after CWL ends, so any reprocessing of the league group
  in that lag window (e.g. a delayed round/war file, or the command itself triggering a fresh
  `get_league_group()` call) wrote the clan's *new* league into `league_rank` — which then got
  frozen permanently once `cwl_ended` finally flipped to 1.
  Fix: gate the fallback on the league group's own live API `.state` field instead
  (`"preparation"`/`"inWar"` = season still active, safe to trust `clan_name_cache`; anything
  else = season already ended in-game, skip the fallback entirely and leave `league_rank`
  unset rather than risk recording the wrong league). This is a strictly more reliable signal
  than our own DB bookkeeping, which is derived data that can lag.
  Data correction: manually corrected `league_rank` from "Champion League II" to "Champion
  League III" for all 8 clans in group `8a56c4022a9b2d94` / season "2026-07" in
  `data/qapbot.db`.
  Added `tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py` (3 tests) covering active-season
  fallback, warEnded-skips-fallback, and preparation-state-is-active. Docs: added a dated note
  to `qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `league_rank` history entry.
  Tests: 1406 passed, 5 deselected.
  Files changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (_process_league_group_response),
  tests/unit/test_cwl_league_rank_season_gate.py (new), qapbot/docs/DATABASE_ARCHITECTURE.md.

2026-07-25 (4)
- Fixed: Registering by player NAME (e.g. "Killer") when the name wasn't yet in the
  war-history cache incorrectly reported "Player #KILLER could not be found in Clash of
  Clans" instead of finding the player via a live clan-roster search.
  Root cause: `normalize_clan_tag()`'s validation pattern (`^#[A-Z0-9]{5,10}$`) also matches
  short all-letter/digit player names with no explicit '#' prefix (e.g. "Killer" -> 6
  uppercase letters -> passes as a "valid tag"). `PlayerSubstringModal.on_submit()`
  (qapbot/ui_registration.py) tried the direct-tag interpretation BEFORE the live CoC API
  name search across the guild's clans, so a plain name that happened to fit the tag pattern
  hijacked the flow and skipped the name search entirely.
  Fix: reordered the fallback — an explicit leading '#' is now required to prioritize the
  direct-tag path; otherwise the live API name search (across `self.clans_in_guild`) runs
  first, and only falls back to interpreting the input as a raw tag if that search finds
  nothing. This is a pure interaction-order fix; `normalize_clan_tag()` itself is unchanged
  since it's still needed and correct for genuine tag input.
  Also audited every location that reads `member_clans`/`member_families` from guild config
  for missing family-expansion ("family-blind") bugs. Found and fixed one: the role-sync
  trigger in `qapbot/coc_cache.py` (`_maybe_trigger_role_sync`) only checked
  `clan_tag in config.get("member_clans", [])`, so guilds configured with ONLY a clan family
  (no individual member clans — like the reporter's server) never got CoC-role/clan-role
  syncs triggered for clan-member updates. Fixed to also expand `member_families` into their
  constituent clan tags, matching the pattern already used correctly everywhere else
  (`get_guild_clans_including_member_config`, `is_player_in_member_clans`,
  `guild_role_manager.sync_roles_for_user`, `cache_manager._is_clan_tracked`, etc.) — all of
  which were confirmed already family-aware and needed no changes.
  Files changed: qapbot/ui_registration.py (PlayerSubstringModal.on_submit),
  qapbot/coc_cache.py. All 1403 tests pass.

    back to the legacy single `welcome_clan_tag` column (read-only) for guilds not yet

2026-07-18 (11)
- Fixed: `/whois player <name>` was inserting a bogus placeholder row into the `clans` table
  for the *reported player's own tag*, polluting the table with fake "clans" (name='Unknown').
  Root cause: `leaderboard_messages.clan_tag` has no FK constraint on purpose — it's reused to
  store clan tags, family tags, AND (for `mode == "whois_player"`) a player tag — but
  `_save_leaderboard_message_impl()` in `qapbot/db_manager.py` unconditionally called
  `_ensure_clan_exists(clan_tag)` whenever clan_tag was truthy, which can't distinguish a
  player tag from a clan tag and silently created a placeholder `clans` row for it.
  Fix: removed the erroneous `_ensure_clan_exists()` call from `_save_leaderboard_message_impl()`
  (the column has no FK, so nothing needs "ensuring").
  Added: self-healing cleanup so bogus/orphaned `clans` rows don't accumulate going forward.
  `db_manager.is_clan_tag_referenced()` / `delete_clan_if_unreferenced()` (+
  `CACHE.purge_clan_if_orphaned()` wrapper) hard-delete a `clans` row once `_mark_clan_deleted()`
  (QBhelperfunctions.py) confirms via the CoC API that a clan_tag no longer exists (404
  NotFound) — but ONLY if nothing else in the database still refers to it (checks
  clan_family_members, user_players.current_clan_tag, guild_member_clans, guild_clan_roles,
  subscriptions, leaderboard_messages, and historical war_summary/war_attacks in both the hot
  and history DB schemas). Previously such clans were only soft-deleted (`is_deleted=True`)
  and left in the table forever.
  Added: `qapbot/scripts/cleanup_orphaned_clans.py` — one-off maintenance script to remove
  existing bad placeholder rows from PROD (dry-run by default, `--execute` to delete;
  candidates = `name='Unknown' AND last_checked_via_api IS NULL`, a pattern that only ever
  occurs via the placeholder-insert path, so real clans are never affected).
  Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py,
  qapbot/scripts/cleanup_orphaned_clans.py.
  All 1401 tests pass.
  FOLLOW-UP FIX (same day): PROD dry run showed 2 of 9 bogus rows were incorrectly kept as
  "REFERENCED" — e.g. #890JPCR00 matched a leaderboard_messages row with mode='whois_player',
  but that row's clan_tag column holds the SAME reported player's tag (the exact bug that
  created the bogus clans row in the first place). Treating a whois_player match as a real
  reference made `is_clan_tag_referenced()` circularly protect the bogus rows forever.
  Fix: `is_clan_tag_referenced()` now excludes `mode = 'whois_player'` rows when checking
  leaderboard_messages (`AND mode != 'whois_player'` in the query) — only genuine clan/family
  leaderboard-mode rows count as a real reference. Affects both the self-healing routine and
  cleanup_orphaned_clans.py (they share this method). All 1401 tests still pass.

2026-07-18 (10)
- Fixed: `/analyse` subcommand mentions in `/help` were not clickable, and `/analyse cwl_opponent`
  was missing from the help list entirely. Root cause: the help embed built Discord slash-command
  mentions (`</command:id>`) using the display text "analyse leaguegroup", but the actually
  registered subcommand is named `cwl_league_group` — Discord only renders a mention as clickable
  when the name in the mention text exactly matches a real command/subcommand path, so it silently
  fell back to plain unclickable text. Audited all registered top-level commands/groups
  (subscribe, unsubscribe, subscriptions, leaderboard, clan management, admin, list, whois, ping,
  status, help, analyse cwl_league_group, analyse cwl_opponent) against `AVAILABLE_COMMANDS` in
  the help command — confirmed `analyse cwl_opponent` was the only one missing.
  Fix: renamed the help-facing display key from "analyse leaguegroup" to "analyse cwl_league_group"
  (now matches the real subcommand name, making the mention clickable) and added
  "analyse cwl_opponent" to `AVAILABLE_COMMANDS`, the help autocomplete list, and the
  Leaderboards & Subscriptions category. Renamed the `en.json`/`de.json` translation key
  "analyse leaguegroup" → "analyse cwl_league_group" (updated usage examples to match) and added
  a new "analyse cwl_opponent" translation key (short/title/detailed) in both languages describing
  the opponent-roster dropdown flow and per-player skill/reliability/activity stats.
