  All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (9)
- Improved: nightly DB maintenance sequencing, logging, and /admin command scope (QapBot.py,
  QBdiscordcmds.py, translations). Investigated from a PROD log showing [ARCHIVE-MOVE] starting
  literally 6ms after "[CYCLE-END] Update cycle completed" — overlapping with that same cycle's
  own [COC-CACHE-CLEANUP]/gc.collect() post-cycle cleanup, both running concurrently on separate
  worker threads and contending for server-machine disk I/O and CPU.
  1. Fixed: relocated the entire nightly-maintenance scheduling block (03:00 UTC due-check +
     `asyncio.create_task(_nightly_maintenance_task())` + monthly migration due-check) from
     immediately after "[CYCLE-END] Update cycle completed" to immediately before "Sleeping for
     N seconds" — i.e. after `_post_cycle_cleanup()` (gc.collect/cache cleanup) and memtrace have
     already finished, and after the maintenance_pending/optimize_db_pending/force_cycle_pending
     early-`continue` checks (which now also correctly prevent nightly maintenance from ever
     racing a concurrent /admin Maintenance Start shutdown or an immediate-next-cycle request —
     a latent bug in the old ordering, now fixed as a side effect). Maintenance now only starts
     once the cycle has truly finished and is about to enter its sleep wait.
  2+3. Added `[NIGHTLY-MAINTENANCE] START` / `[NIGHTLY-MAINTENANCE] END — total duration X.Xs`
     log markers wrapping the whole routine (archive move + optional monthly migration + WAL
     checkpoint/REINDEX/VACUUM/ANALYZE combined) — previously only individual sub-steps
     ([ARCHIVE-MOVE], [DB-MAINT]) had their own timing, with no single marker for the nightly
     routine as a whole.
  4. Investigated /admin Optimize DB before changing it: confirmed it only ever called
     `nightly_db_maintenance()` (WAL checkpoint/REINDEX/VACUUM/ANALYZE) — it never ran the
     archive-move step or the monthly hot->history migration step that the *scheduled* nightly
     task performs, so admin-triggered runs were doing strictly less than the real nightly
     routine. Fixed: extracted the full routine into a new shared
     `run_nightly_maintenance_routine(db_mgr, run_migration)` (QapBot.py) plus a shared
     `is_monthly_migration_due()` helper (hydrates + atomically claims the day-1 migration flag,
     shared between the scheduled task and the admin command so neither double-runs migration
     the same day) — both the scheduled task and both /admin code paths (deferred-while-cycle-
     running, and immediate-when-idle) now call the exact same routine. Renamed the Discord-facing
     command choice from "Optimize DB" to "Execute Nightly Maintenance" (internal `action_norm`
     value "OPTIMIZE_DB" and the `optimize_db_pending`/`QBcore` flag names deliberately left
     unchanged — purely a user-facing rename, not a wider internal-identifier rename, to keep the
     diff scoped and low-risk). Updated all user-facing reply text ("DB optimization complete" →
     "Nightly maintenance complete", etc.) and the help-text description in both translation files.
  Files changed: QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py, qapbot/translations/en.json,
  qapbot/translations/de.json. All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (8)
- Fixed: unyielded ~26K-clan scan loop in PHASE 3B candidate-building (QapBot.py `main()`).
  Follow-up to the CWL DB-blocking investigation: PHASE 3B's candidate-building loop
  (`for safe_tag in _temp_files_by_clan:`, deciding which passive clans need temp-file
  finalization) iterates every clan with a temp file (~26K in PROD) with ZERO yield points —
  same class of bug as the earlier clan-categorization-loop fix. Tier 1 (skip-until dict) and
  Tier 2 (in-memory temp_war_metadata) are cheap, but any clan with no temp_war_metadata entry
  (or a state Tier 2 doesn't cover) falls straight through to Tier 3, which does a real
  `os.path.getmtime()` syscall per clan — a genuine blocking disk syscall directly on the
  event-loop thread, potentially thousands of times per cycle with no yields in between.
  Fix: added `await asyncio.sleep(0)` every 2000 clans (same pattern as the categorize-loop
  fix), plus `[PHASE-3B-SCAN-TIMING] Scanned N clan(s) in X.XXXs (tier3_stat_calls=N,
  candidates=N)` INFO logging to measure the real Tier-3 hit rate and cost going forward
  (previously invisible — no timing existed for this loop at all). Files changed: QapBot.py.
  All 1401 tests pass. Next step: read the next PROD capture's [PHASE-3B-SCAN-TIMING] line to
  see how often Tier 3 actually fires and whether further optimization (e.g. extending Tier 2's
  metadata coverage to more states) is worth pursuing.

2026-07-18 (7)
- Fixed: blocking synchronous DB calls in CWL-related async code paths (QBhelperfunctions.py).
  User asked to check whether any DB writes during the update cycle are blocking, specifically
  flagging that CWL season (archived wars + additional CWL tables) makes this more expensive.
  Found 6 direct, unwrapped calls to `_sync`-suffixed db_manager methods (plain `sqlite3`, not
  aiosqlite) inside `async def` functions that are part of the hot update-cycle path — each one
  blocks the event loop (Discord heartbeat/interactions) for its I/O duration every time it runs,
  and these are exactly the CWL-specific checks that fire most often during CWL season:
  1. `generate_cwlinfo_embeds()` (~line 1715) — `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync()`, called every
     cycle while posting the "cwlinfo" leaderboard for every subscribed clan.
  2. `_find_active_cwl_war_for_clan()` Guard B (~line 5421) — `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync()`,
     called from the Phase-1 hot path for every clan needing the CWL fallback.
  3+4. `fetch_clan_war_data()`'s two notInWar CWL-fallback branches (~lines 6396, 6436) — same
     `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync()` check, called for every clan every cycle.
  5. `fetch_clan_war_data()`'s "Layer 2 pre-save" guard (~line 6460) —
     `get_cwl_round_for_war_tag_sync()`, called for every CWL-active clan every cycle.
  6. `process_orphaned_cwl_wars()`'s `fetch_and_update_orphan()` (~line 7048) — same
     `get_cwl_round_for_war_tag_sync()` check, called once per orphaned CWL war inside an
     `asyncio.gather()` (up to 20 concurrent via Semaphore) — the most CWL-season-heavy of the six.
  All 6 were harmless when CWL-related activity was low (fast enough not to notice), but scale
  with CWL season's higher CWL-clan/orphaned-war counts — exactly the "gets pretty expensive"
  scenario flagged. Fix: wrapped each in `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`, matching the existing
  pattern already used correctly elsewhere in the same functions (e.g. `save_war_object` calls
  right next to several of these). One occurrence (~line 4579, inside `_process_war_history`
  called from `manage_war_files`) was already safe — confirmed it runs inside a call chain
  already dispatched via `asyncio.to_thread(_with_war_batch, ...)` from QapBot.py Phase-3, so it
  was correctly left unwrapped (wrapping it again would spawn a redundant nested thread-pool call).
  All DB WRITE paths during the cycle (war_attacks/war_summary appends via `add_war_data_sync`/
  `flush_pending_war_writes`, `bulk_update_clan_timestamps`, `upsert_cwl_league_data`) were also
  audited: writes either use aiosqlite natively (async, non-blocking by design) or run inside an
  `asyncio.to_thread(_with_war_batch, ...)`-wrapped call chain — all confirmed already
  non-blocking. Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py. All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (2)
- Analyzed: full update-cycle time-gap sweep via qapbot/scripts/log_time_gaps.py against
  data/logs/qapbot.log (190K lines, ~9h of PROD-scale activity) to find further blocking
  segments beyond the gc.collect() fix earlier today. Found two recurring, previously-silent
  gaps that repeated on EVERY cycle (confirmed via ~90-100 occurrences each in the top-400
  gap list), independent of the already-excluded nightly-maintenance/startup windows:
  1. "Starting clan war info updates..." → next log line: a consistent ~4.1-4.2s gap on
     literally every cycle, regardless of active/inactive clan counts (300 to 5300+).
     Root cause: the clan categorization loop in main() (QapBot.py) does
     `for clan_tag in list(CACHE.clan_name_cache.keys())` with ZERO yield points. This
     cache holds ~380K entries in PROD (confirmed via "[DB-READ] Loaded 379620 clans from
     database" in rotated logs — every clan ever encountered via CWL family harvesting,
     not just this cycle's counts), so the loop's ~380K synchronous dict-lookup/datetime-
     parse iterations ran as one uninterrupted block, freezing Discord heartbeat/gateway
     processing and slash-command handling for ~4s every single cycle (~5min), all day.
     Fix: added `await asyncio.sleep(0)` every 2000 iterations (~190 yields/cycle,
     negligible overhead) so the event loop can interleave other tasks throughout the
     loop instead of being frozen for its entire ~4s duration. Also added
     `[CATEGORIZE-TIMING] Categorized N clan(s) in X.XXXs` INFO log to make the total
     cost visible going forward without needing DEBUG.
  2. "Clan updates complete: ..." → "[ROLE-SYNC] Starting guild-wide role sync...": a
     consistent 8-26s silent gap on every cycle (only 120 user_accounts / ~21 active wars
     — too small for the in-memory work alone to plausibly take that long; root cause not
     yet pinpointed from static code review). The only code that runs in this window is
     check_wars_for_notifications() (qapbot/war_notifications.py). That function already
     yields via `await asyncio.sleep(0)` between wars/sub-steps, so it likely isn't a raw
     "frozen event loop" bug like #1, but the *wall-clock* cost is still large and
     unexplained (observed even when zero notifications are sent this cycle — 20 of 21
     wars have no players to notify, yet the loop still costs ~0.7s/war on average before
     reaching the one war that does need a reminder).
     Fix (partial — diagnostics only, root cause pending real PROD data): added detailed
     timing instrumentation:
       · `[NOTIFY-TIMING] index_build=... get_active_wars=... war_loop=... total=...
         (active_wars=N, slowest=war_id:X.XXXs)` — logged at the end of every call.
       · `[NOTIFY-WAR-TIMING] war=... get_reminders=... get_attacks_remaining=...
         send_channel=... total=...` — logged per-war whenever that single war's
         processing exceeds 0.3s, to pinpoint which sub-step (likely
         `_send_channel_war_notification`'s per-guild `CACHE.subscriptions` iteration,
         suspected but unconfirmed) is the actual cost driver.
     Next step once deployed: read the next cycle's [NOTIFY-TIMING]/[NOTIFY-WAR-TIMING]
     lines to confirm the culprit sub-step, then apply a targeted fix (index the
     subscriptions-by-clan-tag lookup, and/or move any confirmed synchronous hot loop to
     asyncio.to_thread()).
  Excluded from this analysis per explicit scope: nightly DB maintenance (REINDEX/VACUUM,
  already has its own EXCLUSIVE-lock + db_maintenance_mode guard) and bot startup routine.
  Files changed: QapBot.py (categorize-loop yields + timing), qapbot/war_notifications.py
  (NOTIFY-TIMING / NOTIFY-WAR-TIMING instrumentation). All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (3)
- Verified: PROD run (2 cycles, fresh restart) confirms both same-day fixes work as intended.
  · [CYCLE-CLEANUP] gc_collect now 0.000-0.005s (was 1s+) — gc.collect(1) fix confirmed.
  · [CATEGORIZE-TIMING] still ~4.1-5.3s for ~389K clans (unchanged, as expected — the fix
    adds yield points, it doesn't reduce total CPU work; the loop now yields ~194 times
    instead of running as one solid block).
  · [NOTIFY-TIMING] total=0.106-0.112s, every war <1ms (slowest=...:0.000s) — the previously
    observed 8-26s "Clan updates complete → [ROLE-SYNC]" gap did NOT reproduce. Revised
    theory: check_wars_for_notifications()'s own work was never slow; the delay was very
    likely event-loop backlog (aiohttp keep-alive housekeeping, coc.py rate-limiter timers,
    Discord gateway/heartbeat processing) queued up during the categorize loop's previous
    4s zero-yield freeze, then drained through check_wars_for_notifications()'s frequent
    per-war `await asyncio.sleep(0)` yield points instead — misattributing the delay to that
    function even though its own code was always fast. Adding yields to the categorize loop
    let that backlog drain gradually instead of dumping it all downstream.
  Next step: monitor PROD over several hours (ideally spanning higher active-war-count
  periods) for any elevated [NOTIFY-TIMING]/[NOTIFY-WAR-TIMING] values; if it resurfaces,
  the per-war breakdown will now pinpoint the exact culprit directly. No further code
  change made — both instrumented fixes confirmed working, no regression.

2026-07-18 (4)
- Optimized: clan-categorization loop micro-optimization (QapBot.py `main()`).
  Measured >99.9% of the ~389K `CACHE.clan_name_cache` entries are passively-tracked
  enemy clans rejected on the very first check — the ~4s cost is pure CPython per-
  iteration loop overhead (dict lookups, isinstance calls), not wasted per-clan work.
  Changes (no polling-eligibility semantics changed, near-zero risk):
  1. Iterate a `list(CACHE.clan_name_cache.items())` snapshot instead of
     `list(.keys())` + a second `[clan_tag]` lookup per clan — halves dict-lookup
     count. Still snapshotted (not a live view) because the loop yields via
     `await asyncio.sleep(0)`, and other coroutines (e.g. WAR-DISCOVERY upserting a
     newly scouted enemy clan) can mutate the dict between yields — a live view
     would risk "dictionary changed size during iteration".
  2. Replaced the per-clan `isinstance(clan_data, dict)` check with EAFP
     (try/except AttributeError) around the merged track_war_updates/is_deleted
     fast-reject check. isinstance() has real per-call overhead at 389K
     iterations; exception handling costs ~nothing when no exception fires
     (true for >99.9% of entries).
  Considered and rejected: an incrementally-maintained "pollable clan tags"
  set/index (would cut ~389K iterations down to ~419, the real fix) — requires
  touching ~6-8 call sites that create/mutate track_war_updates/is_deleted across
  cache_manager.py/coc_cache.py/QBhelperfunctions.py, and this exact class of
  stale-derived-state bug has bitten this codebase before (~40K Master III+ clans
  silently stopped being polled, see earlier changelog entry). Not worth the
  correctness risk for a step that's already non-blocking (yields every 2000
  clans). Also considered and rejected: multiprocessing to use both CPU cores —
  CPython's GIL means threads provide no real parallelism for this pure-Python
  CPU-bound loop; real multiprocessing (ProcessPoolExecutor) requires
  pickling/IPC of clan state every cycle (the in-place "smart backdating"
  mutations can't cross process boundaries without shipping results back and
  reapplying them in the main process), and the per-item work here is too cheap
  to amortize that overhead — likely a net loss, not a win. All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (5)
- Investigated: recurring ~3s silent gap between "[PHASE-1] All API fetches completed"
  and "[PHASE-3] Starting file processing..." every cycle (user-reported from PROD log).
  What happens in that window (QapBot.py `main()`, all logged only at DEBUG before this
  change, hence invisible in the INFO-only PROD log):
  1. Cycle-stat bookkeeping over fetch_results (O(clans fetched this cycle) — cheap).
  2. PRE-SCAN: two directory scans — glob.glob() over temp/shard_*/*.json (tens of
     thousands of files) and os.scandir() over the entire archive/ tree (hundreds of
     thousands of files as of 2026-07). Both already wrapped in asyncio.to_thread(), so
     the event loop is NOT blocked during this — it's real disk-I/O wall-clock latency
     (prod-server external-drive directory listing), not a blocking bug.
  3. PHASE 1.5 (CWL war-tag recovery batch) — conditional on
     CACHE.pending_cwl_recovery, usually a no-op.
  4. PHASE 2 (process_orphaned_cwl_wars) — yes, Phase 2 exists; it finalizes CWL wars for
     clans whose API fetch failed this cycle (orphan detection), using the pre-scanned
     file lists so it needs no extra I/O. Usually fast/near-no-op when few fetches failed
     (as in the captured cycles).
  5. WAL checkpoint dispatched as a fire-and-forget background task (instant, non-blocking).
  6. In-memory prescan-dict cleanup for clans Phase 2 finalized (no I/O).
  Confirmed NOT blocking: unlike the earlier categorize-loop/gc.collect() bugs, both
  directory scans already use asyncio.to_thread() correctly, so Discord heartbeat/
  interactions stay responsive during this ~3s — it's backgrounded I/O latency, not a
  frozen event loop.
  Fix (diagnostics only — root cause not yet conclusively split between the two scans
  from static analysis, need real numbers): added INFO-level timing:
  `[PRE-SCAN-TIMING] temp_scan=X.XXXs (N files, M clans) archive_scan=X.XXXs (K files)
  total=X.XXXs`, `[PHASE-1.5-TIMING] Completed in X.XXXs` (only logs when CWL recovery is
  active), `[PHASE-2-TIMING] Completed in X.XXXs (failed_clans=N, finalized=M)`.
  Working theory (not yet confirmed): the archive/ directory scan (hundreds of thousands
  of files) is the dominant cost, since it's the larger and slower-growing of the two
  directories. If confirmed, a real fix would be an incrementally-maintained in-memory
  archive-filename index instead of a full rescan every cycle — actually lower-risk here
  than the earlier clan_name_cache case, since ALL archive/ mutations are already
  in-process (Phase-3 moves files in, `_archive_move_nightly()` moves files out to
  archive_old/; the external ArchiveOld.bat only touches archive_old/, never archive/
  directly) — but not yet implemented pending confirmation from real PROD timing data.
  Files changed: QapBot.py. All 1401 tests pass.

2026-07-18 (6)
- Verified + fixed: PROD run (2 cycles) with the new PRE-SCAN/PHASE-1.5/PHASE-2 timing
  confirmed both directory scans are non-blocking (asyncio.to_thread, as expected) and
  revealed a new, previously-invisible cost center:
  · [PRE-SCAN-TIMING] temp_scan=0.155s (~26K files) archive_scan=0.985-0.993s (~320K
    files) total=1.14-1.15s — confirms the working theory: archive_scan (hundreds of
    thousands of files) dominates the PRE-SCAN, as suspected. Not fixed this round
    (still pending a decision on the incremental archive-index approach).
  · [PHASE-2-TIMING] Completed in 1.44-1.47s (failed_clans=0, finalized=0) — bigger
    than the entire PRE-SCAN, despite finding ZERO orphaned wars. Root cause:
    process_orphaned_cwl_wars()'s `_scan_orphans()` helper (QBhelperfunctions.py,
    already correctly wrapped in asyncio.to_thread() — confirmed non-blocking) iterates
    every one of the ~26K single-file clans in the pre-scanned temp-file map every
    cycle. For each, it parses the filename timestamp via
    `datetime.strptime(fn_ts, "%Y%m%d%H%M")` to check whether the file is old enough to
    possibly be an orphan — strptime's generic format-string matching machinery is
    ~10-20x slower than direct construction for a fixed, pre-validated 12-digit string.
  Fix: replaced the strptime() call with manual slice+int parsing
  (`datetime(int(fn_ts[0:4]), int(fn_ts[4:6]), ...)`) — safe because `_extract_ts()`
  already guarantees fn_ts is exactly 12 digits (isdigit()+len==12 check) before this
  code runs; invalid date components still raise ValueError via the datetime()
  constructor itself, preserving the existing except-ValueError fallback behavior
  exactly. No other hot per-cycle loop uses strptime() (checked; the other 5 call sites
  are low-frequency: per-notification, tooling scripts, or log analysis).
  Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (process_orphaned_cwl_wars._scan_orphans). All
  1401 tests pass. Next step: confirm the speedup in the next PROD capture via
  [PHASE-2-TIMING], and revisit whether the archive-scan and/or orphan-scan full-rescan
  approaches are worth replacing with incremental indexes once both are as cheap as
  reasonably possible without that added complexity/risk.

2026-07-18
- Fixed: `[COC-CACHE-CLEANUP]` → `[CYCLE-END] [MEMORY CONSUMPTION]` gap regularly >1s,
  effectively freezing the whole bot once per cycle (QapBot.py `_post_cycle_cleanup`).
  Root cause: end-of-cycle cleanup called a full `gc.collect()` (generation 2) inside
  `asyncio.to_thread()`. Offloading to a worker thread only keeps discord.py's heartbeat
  *task* schedulable — it does NOT make `gc.collect()` itself non-blocking, because
  CPython does not release the GIL for the duration of that single C call. A full
  collect walks every long-lived object in the process (the entire CACHE state — clan
  caches, war caches, user accounts, easily hundreds of thousands of objects), which is
  what actually took 1s+ on the server-machine CPU and stalled the event loop despite "running in
  a thread". The full collect was only added (see 2026-05-xx entry) to reclaim aiohttp
  `ClientResponse` body-buffer chunks that get promoted from gen-0 to gen-1 via `__del__`
  (5M+ allocations/cycle) — `gc.collect(0)` skips gen-1 entirely so those buffers never
  got reclaimed.
  Fix: changed `gc.collect()` to `gc.collect(1)` — collects gen-0 AND gen-1 (reclaiming
  the aiohttp buffers exactly like a full collect would) while skipping the expensive
  gen-2 walk of the bot's long-lived CACHE state. Python's automatic generational GC
  (still enabled, default thresholds, never disabled in this codebase) continues to run
  real gen-2 sweeps on its own schedule, so genuine long-lived reference cycles are still
  collected — just not force-walked every single cycle.
  Also added: per-step timing breakdown logged as
  `[CYCLE-CLEANUP] clear_expired=X.XXXs gc_collect=X.XXXs malloc_trim=X.XXXs total=X.XXXs`
  so future regressions can be pinpointed immediately instead of re-instrumenting.
  File changed: QapBot.py (`_post_cycle_cleanup` inside `periodic_main`).

2026-07-16
- Fixed: two follow-up bugs found in production logs right after the LEFT-JOIN
  materialization fix (below) shipped:
  1. `[DB-QUERY-SYNC] get_player_monthly_star_dist_sync failed: ambiguous
     column name: is_cwl` — caused by the fix itself. Rewriting the LEFT JOIN
     to hit `main.war_summary ws_h` and `history.war_summary ws_a` directly
     (instead of one merged `ws` CTE) means BOTH now expose a real `is_cwl`
     column, so `GROUP BY month, is_cwl, season_key` became ambiguous between
     the two real columns and the `is_cwl` SELECT alias (previously only one
     source had that column, so it was unambiguous). Same latent bug existed
     in QBhelperfunctions.py's `compute_roster_stats_sync()` query 2
     (`GROUP BY wa.player_tag, is_cwl`) from the earlier audit fix, just not
     yet hit in the field. Fixed both by grouping on the full
     `COALESCE(ws_h.is_cwl, ws_a.is_cwl, ...)` expression instead of the bare
     alias. Verified against the full-size prod DB copy — no more error,
     results correct, still <5ms.
  2. `/status` taking ~21s: `get_global_db_statistics_sync()`'s `wars_count`
     (COUNT DISTINCT war_id over hot+history war_summary, ~5.1M rows) and
     `attacks_count` (COUNT(*) over hot+history war_attacks, ~94M rows) each
     require a full index scan — there is no O(1) row-count shortcut across a
     UNION ALL of two attached schemas, even with a covering index. Measured
     ~1.8s + ~1.2s = ~3s combined on a fast local SSD; server-machine/slower prod storage
     (per the existing "made /status take many seconds on HDD" comment already
     in this function) explains the rest of the 21s. Since these are reporting
     stats (not used for any business logic), added a 5-minute TTL cache
     (`WarHistoryDB._global_stats_cache` / `_GLOBAL_STATS_TTL = 300s`) so
     repeated `/status` calls within the window are served from memory instead
     of re-scanning. Verified: first call 2.83s, second call within the TTL
     window 0.00001s. All 1401 tests pass.

- Fixed: /whois player was extremely slow (>13s, causing "Interaction expired
  before defer" on the follow-up dropdown/chart interactions) and, under load,
  crashed the prod process with an out-of-memory error. Root cause: the
  hot/history query helpers added by the 2026-07-11 DB split joined a
  `war_summary` UNION ALL CTE (`ws AS (SELECT * FROM main.war_summary UNION
  ALL SELECT * FROM history.war_summary)`) via `LEFT JOIN ws ON ...`. SQLite
  cannot use a co-routine for the right-hand side of a LEFT JOIN, so a compound
  (UNION ALL) subquery used there is fully MATERIALIZED first — i.e. every
  single row of BOTH main.war_summary and history.war_summary (~2.7M + ~2.4M
  rows on prod) was scanned into a temp table on EVERY /whois call, regardless
  of how few rows the player actually had. Confirmed via EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN
  ("MATERIALIZE ws" + "SCAN main.war_summary" / "SCAN history.war_summary")
  and empirically: 59s for a single query against a full-size prod DB copy.
  Fix: replaced the `ws` CTE + single `LEFT JOIN ws` with two direct LEFT JOINs
  — one against `main.war_summary`, one against `history.war_summary` — and
  COALESCE the columns (main wins on the vanishingly rare overlap). Each table
  keeps its own UNIQUE(war_id, clan_tag) index usable as a normal indexed
  lookup, so SQLite performs a cheap per-row SEARCH instead of a full scan.
  Verified: same query dropped from 59s to 0.02s on the same prod DB copy;
  EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN now shows "SEARCH ws_h/ws_a USING COVERING INDEX
  sqlite_autoindex_war_summary_1" with no MATERIALIZE step.
  Functions fixed in qapbot/db_manager.py: get_player_war_history_sync
  (both the actual_rows and sentinel_rows queries), get_player_attack_summary_sync
  (both total_row and dist_row queries), get_player_monthly_star_dist_sync.
  Other `ws AS (...)` usages (get_direct_cwl_attacks_sync, get_cwl_roster_sync,
  the CWL cross-clan attack queries) use INNER JOIN or a scalar subquery, not
  LEFT JOIN, so they don't hit this SQLite materialization rule and were left
  unchanged. All 1401 tests pass.

- Fixed: a *different* hot/history regression found while auditing other commands
  for the same class of bug: QBhelperfunctions.py's `compute_roster_stats_sync()`
  (used by `build_cwl_opponent_embeds` — the CWL opponent-preview roster stats
  shown by `/cwlinfo`) queried `war_attacks`/`war_summary`/`cwl_league_groups`/
  `cwl_league_rounds` completely unqualified. Since this function computes
  all-time skill/reliability/activity (uses the player's oldest war date as the
  activity-normalisation baseline, same as `_player_report_logic`), it was never
  updated when the 2026-07-11 hot/history split landed and was NOT in that
  changelog's list of 20 rewritten functions — it was missed. Effect: for any
  roster member with wars older than the 2-month hot retention window (i.e.
  most established players), stats silently reflected only the hot window
  instead of full history — no crash, just quietly wrong numbers. Fixed by
  applying the same `UNION ALL` + two-direct-LEFT-JOIN (`ws_h`/`ws_a`) pattern
  used in db_manager.py. Verified query plans on a full-size prod DB copy show
  indexed `SEARCH ... USING INDEX idx_wa_player_tag` / `COVERING INDEX
  sqlite_autoindex_war_summary_1` with no MATERIALIZE step, ~0.05s for a 3-player
  roster. All other raw SQL in QBdiscordcmds.py / QBhelperfunctions.py that
  references war_attacks/war_summary/cwl_league_rounds unqualified was audited
  and confirmed to be intentionally current-CWL-season-only (which can never
  live in the history DB by construction of the monthly migration — same
  precedent as the season auto-detect query in analyze_cwl_rounds.py), so no
  further changes were needed there. Known lower-priority follow-up (not fixed,
  admin-only/rarely invoked, no crash risk): the `/admin` CWL-group-backfill
  handler in qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py
  (`_handle_backfill_cwl_groups_inner`) filters war_summary by `cwl_season` alone
  without a supporting index (existing index is `(clan_tag, cwl_season)`),
  which likely full-scans hot+history war_summary; low impact since it's a
  manually-triggered maintenance command, not user-facing.

2026-07-11
- Fixed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py (CWL round-completion diagnostic script) was not
  hot/history-DB-split-aware — a known gap flagged when the split first landed earlier the same
  day (see the Hot/History database split entry below). All 11 of its raw sqlite3 connections
  now ATTACH data/qapbot_history.db (read-only) and its 7 query helper functions plus the inline
  diagnostic/completeness queries in main() were rewritten to UNION main.<table> + history.<table>
  for war_summary, cwl_league_groups and cwl_league_rounds. The season auto-detect query
  (MAX(cwl_season), used when no season argument is given) was deliberately left main-only — the
  most recent season can never live in history by construction of the monthly migration.
  Verified with a synthetic hot+history DB where an entire CWL season lived only in history:
  before the fix the script reported 0 archive rows for that season; after the fix it correctly
  reports all 7 war_summary rows and the round/league/max-rounds breakdowns.
  File changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py

  "known remaining gap" note, now marked done).

- Added: qapbot/scripts/run_history_migration_now.py — lets an operator trigger
  WarHistoryDB.monthly_history_migration() on demand (e.g. right after deploying the
  hot/history split, instead of waiting for the next day-1 nightly window). Confirmation
  prompt by default (--yes to skip), idempotent/safe to re-run. Verified with a synthetic
  hot DB containing one old war — correctly moved it to history.

- Added: Hot/History database split to stop data/qapbot.db from growing unboundedly
  (33 GB and counting), which was the root cause of an ever-slower nightly VACUUM
  (2688s / 44.8 min observed on 2026-07-11) that made the update cycle stall and
  backlog for 20-25 minutes afterward, making the bot feel unresponsive to Discord
  commands. Full investigation + design rationale: qapbot/docs/CLAN_AND_WAR_CYCLE_ARCHITECTURE.md
  § Hot/History DB Redesign.

  Design: data/qapbot.db (hot) now always holds the current calendar month + the
  immediately preceding calendar month, in full, for war_attacks, war_summary,
  cwl_league_groups and cwl_league_rounds. Everything older lives in a second
  file, data/qapbot_history.db, ATTACHed as SQLite schema `history` on every
  connection (both the async connection and every pooled sync connection).
  Existing queries bounded to recent data are unchanged; all-time/historical
  queries were rewritten as `UNION ALL` across `main.<table>` and `history.<table>`
  (verified empirically that `VACUUM <schema> INTO '<file>'` works correctly on
  attached, non-main schemas). Once a month (day 1, inside the existing nightly
  maintenance window), a new batched migration job moves data older than the
  retention window from hot to history — batched (not one giant transaction) to
  avoid repeating the original 44-minute-stall mistake.

  Implementation (multi-step, each step verified with the full 1401-test suite
  plus targeted smoke tests using real hot+history data before proceeding):
    1. Schema/ATTACH plumbing: qapbot/config.py (new `history_db_path` /
       `HISTORY_DB_PATH` env var), qapbot/db_manager.py (`WarHistoryDB.initialize()`
       now ATTACHes history + creates its schema; `_SyncConnectionPool` and the
       bare-connection fallback both attach it too; new module-level
       `_create_history_schema_sync()` / `attach_history_db()` helpers for
       standalone scripts).
    2. Monthly migration job: `WarHistoryDB.monthly_history_migration()` +
       `_migrate_table_batch_by_date()` / `_migrate_cwl_table_by_season()` (CWL
       tables have no `date` column — keyed on `normalize_cwl_season()`'s
       normalized `YYYY-MM` prefix). Wired into QapBot.py's nightly scheduler

- Fixed: /leaderboard mode=cwlgroup with the `season` option supplied was
  rendered as a generic image/text leaderboard instead of the CWL
  group-standings image (bot logged "Posting leaderboard for clan ...,
  mode cwlgroup_cwl_2026-06 ..." and Discord showed a generic leaderboard
  image with no group standings).

  Root cause: supplying `season` forces cwl_only=True. The local
    apply_cwl() helper in the leaderboard() command unconditionally appends
    "_cwl" to the resolved mode whenever cwl_only=True and the mode doesn't
    already end with "_cwl", turning "cwlgroup" into "cwlgroup_cwl". But
    "cwlgroup" (like "cwlinfo", "cwlinfo_comp", "currentwar") is already
    inherently CWL-specific / has no "_cwl" variant in MODE_REGISTRY, and
    the special-mode dispatch further down only matches the exact strings
    'cwlinfo' / 'cwlinfo_comp' / 'cwlgroup'. "cwlgroup_cwl" matched none of
    them, so execution fell through to the generic
    generate_leaderboard_text()/post_leaderboard_to_discord() branch, which
    doesn't understand "cwlgroup_cwl" and produced the wrong output.
  Fix: extracted the mode/cwl_only suffix logic into a new public helper
    `apply_cwl_mode_suffix(mode, cwl_only)` in qapbot/formatting.py, guarded
    by a `NO_CWL_SUFFIX_MODES` set ({'cwlgroup', 'cwlinfo', 'cwlinfo_comp',
    'currentwar'}) — these modes are now left unchanged regardless of
    cwl_only. QBdiscordcmds.py's local apply_cwl() now delegates to it.
  Files changed: qapbot/formatting.py (new apply_cwl_mode_suffix +
    NO_CWL_SUFFIX_MODES), QBdiscordcmds.py (leaderboard(): apply_cwl
    delegates to the new helper)
  Tests added: tests/unit/test_formatting_modes.py
    (TestApplyCwlModeSuffix — no-suffix modes unchanged with/without
    cwl_only, regular modes still get "_cwl" suffix, no double-suffixing,
    registry-drift guard asserting no "<mode>_cwl" variant exists for any
    NO_CWL_SUFFIX_MODES entry).
  All 1401 tests pass.

- Fixed: /leaderboard cwlgroup with only month/year given (no explicit season
  option) resolved to the wrong CWL sub-season whenever a regular monthly
  CWL and a mid-month bonus CWL both existed in the same month — e.g.
  month=6/year=2026 returned the June mid-month bonus group ("2026-06-16")
  instead of the regular June CWL group ("2026-06").

  Root cause: get_latest_cwl_season_for_clan_in_month() picked the match via
    "ORDER BY cwl_season DESC LIMIT 1". String comparison always ranks a
    dated sub-season ("2026-06-16") above the plain regular key ("2026-06")
    for the same month (longer string with matching prefix sorts higher),
    so the bonus event silently won even when the user just wanted the
    regular monthly standings. This was actually the intended behavior of a
    prior change (see 2026-06-xx entry) but contradicts the documented
    /leaderboard contract: explicit month/year should fall back to the
    plain "YYYY-MM" key; only the explicit `season` option should be able to
    target a specific mid-month sub-season (e.g. "2026-06-15").
  Fix: query now orders by "LENGTH(cwl_season) ASC, cwl_season DESC" so the
    7-char plain "YYYY-MM" key is always preferred when present; falls back
    to the most recent dated sub-season only when no plain regular-season
    entry exists for that month. The explicit `season` slash-command option
    is unaffected — it already used the exact (normalized) string supplied
    by the user and correctly differentiates "2026-06" / "2026-06-15" /
    "2026-07".
  File changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (get_latest_cwl_season_for_clan_in_month)
  Tests added: tests/unit/test_db_manager_async_phase5.py
    (TestGetLatestCwlSeasonForClanInMonth — plain-key preference, bonus-only
    fallback, multiple-bonus tie-break, no-match, cross-month prefix safety)
  All 1389 tests pass.

- Fixed: regular monthly CWL seasons mislabeled with a full date instead of
  "YYYY-MM" (e.g. the July 2026 CWL was stored as cwl_season="2026-06-29"
  instead of "2026-07").

  Root cause: the CoC API briefly returns the league-group's literal
    creation date ("YYYY-MM-DD", always a Monday) as the `season` field
    while the group is still being assembled, instead of the final
    "YYYY-MM" form. Because CWL matchmaking always starts on a Monday, this
    creation-date snapshot can fall in the last few days of the previous
    month (e.g. "2026-06-29" for the July CWL) or the first few days of the
    target month. normalize_cwl_season() previously treated ANY
    "YYYY-MM-DD" value as a genuine mid-month bonus CWL and snapped it to
    that week's Monday, so these month-boundary snapshots were stored
    verbatim instead of being folded into the correct "YYYY-MM" key.
  Fix: normalize_cwl_season() now disambiguates "YYYY-MM-DD" input by
    day-of-month: day <= 6 -> fold into that month's "YYYY-MM"; day >= 25 ->
    fold into NEXT month's "YYYY-MM" (with Dec -> Jan year rollover); day
    7-24 -> genuine mid-month bonus CWL, unchanged behavior (snap to ISO
    Monday). This keeps correctly identifying any future mid-month bonus
    CWL events while fixing regular monthly season labeling.
  File changed: qapbot/constants.py (normalize_cwl_season)
  Tests added: tests/unit/test_constants.py (TestNormalizeCwlSeason —
    month-end/month-start boundary rollover, genuine mid-month snap,
    unparseable input)

- Added: one-off repair script to correct historically mislabeled
  cwl_season values across all affected tables (war_summary,
  cwl_league_groups, cwl_league_rounds), recomputing the season-derived
  league_group_id hash and merging with any already-correctly-labeled
  duplicate row (keeping the most complete stats) to avoid primary-key
  conflicts on cwl_league_groups(cwl_season, clan_tag). Dry-run by default;
  --apply commits. Run against the local dev DB and confirmed clean
  afterwards (re-run reports "No mislabeled CWL season keys found").
  File added: qapbot/scripts/fix_cwl_season_labels.py

2026-06-29
- Added: CWL recovery batch now upserts untracked M3+ clans into clan_name_cache
  after writing their temp file, mirroring the _upsert_enemy_clan_on_war_start
  mechanism used in the regular update cycle.

  New DB method: db_manager.get_war_tag_leagues(war_tags) — bulk JOIN of
    cwl_league_rounds + cwl_league_groups to return {war_tag: league_rank}
    for all tags in a batch.  No extra CoC API calls needed.
  Upsert logic in _fetch_and_write (after successful save_war_object):
    - Skips clans already in clan_name_cache.
    - Looks up the war_tag's league from wt_to_league (pre-loaded before batch).
    - If league in _CWL_HARVEST_LEAGUES (M3+): writes a minimal clan_name_cache
      entry (name from war object, track_war_updates=True) and calls persist_clan.
    - Clans below M3 or with unknown league are not added (no extra tracking).
  Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (get_war_tag_leagues),
                 QBhelperfunctions.py (process_cwl_recovery_batch)

2026-06-29
- Fixed: CWL recovery batch (process_cwl_recovery_batch) writing 0 temp files
  despite 200 OK from CoC API for all war_tags.

  Root cause: _fetch_and_write() gated writes on CACHE.clan_name_cache —
    if neither war participant was in the in-memory cache (i.e. untracked
    opponent clans in Master III+ groups), the write was silently skipped with
    no counter increment and no re-queue.  This affected:
      - "missing one" tags (2,644): the already-written tracked-clan side was
        correctly skipped (n_skipped+1), but the missing opponent side fell
        through the cache guard → to_write empty → silent drop.
      - "missing both" tags (260): both sides are inter-opponent wars with no
        tracked participant → both fail cache check → silent drop.
  Fix: removed the clan_name_cache guard from _fetch_and_write().  The only
    gate now is the existing_pairs DB check.  save_war_object(force_save=True)
    does not require the clan to be in clan_name_cache — it only needs the
    war object and the target clan tag.
  File changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (process_cwl_recovery_batch._fetch_and_write)

- Fixed: find_missing_war_tags() now returns (list, missing_both, missing_one)
  tuple; terminal output shows the breakdown:
    -> Missing both entries (0 rows in war_summary): N
    -> Missing one entry   (1 row in war_summary):   N
  File changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py

- Fixed: UnicodeEncodeError in analyze_cwl_rounds.py when running --no-log on
  Windows (cp1252 console).  Replaced all non-ASCII characters in print()
  statements (→, —, ≥) with ASCII equivalents (->, --, >=).
  File changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py

- Fixed: dict[tuple, int] bare-tuple type annotation in
  query_league_round_breakdown() rejected by Pylance strict mode.  Changed to
  dict[tuple[Optional[int], Optional[str]], int].
  File changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py

- Fixed: CWL missing-war recovery script (analyze_cwl_rounds.py) severely
  undercounting recoverable war_tags (4,524 identified vs ~54,315 missing).

  Root cause 1 — Master III+ league filter:
    find_missing_war_tags() only considered war_tags from groups whose
    league_rank was Master III or higher, silently discarding all Crystal /
    Gold / Silver league wars.  Crystal League I alone contains 50,308 clans.
  Root cause 2 — opponent-only group exclusion:
    cwl_league_rounds has 38,592 distinct group IDs but cwl_league_groups
    only registers 20,407.  The 18,185 "opponent-only" groups (discovered
    when fetching league group data for tracked clans) were also filtered out.

  Fix — tiered recovery policy (find_missing_war_tags):
    Master III+ groups  → always recover all missing wars globally.
    Crystal I and lower → recover only if the group contains at least one
      clan with has_active_subscriptions = 1 in the clans table.
    Groups absent from cwl_league_groups → skip (opponent-only, no primary
      tracked clan member; archiving them would inflate the DB without
      serving any subscription).
  Also updated docstring and removed the now-obsolete league_group_set /
  _MASTER_PLUS variables.  Step 4 now updates highest_round via the
  war_tag's own group ID so groups absent from cwl_league_groups are still
  tracked correctly for the ended_season=False guard.

- Added: CWL league demotion support in CoCClanCache._update_clan_metadata
  (qapbot/coc_cache.py).

  Previously track_war_updates was a one-way ratchet: once set True it
  never reverted, causing demoted clans to remain in the 22h polling pool
  forever and accumulate temp war files after the season ended.

  New behaviour (triggered when the CoC API returns a new war_league value):
    Promotion  (Crystal I → Master III+, non-subscribed, track=False):
      track_war_updates → True  (unchanged from before)
    Demotion   (Master III+ → Crystal I, non-subscribed, track=True):
      1. _cleanup_temp_war_files() removes every temp war JSON for the clan
         from the sharded temp dir (prevents orphaned war files).
      2. In-memory war state cleared via set_temp_war_stats(clan_tag, {}).
      3. track_war_updates → False  (clan leaves 22h polling pool).
    Subscribed / member clans (has_active_subscriptions=True):
      Immune to both directions — track_war_updates stays True permanently.

  New helper: CoCClanCache._cleanup_temp_war_files(clan_tag) → int
    Locates the correct shard via QBcsvhandling.get_war_shard_dir, removes
    all matching *_war_data.json files, clears in-memory caches, returns
    removed count for log message.

  Tests updated in tests/unit/test_coc_cache_coverage.py:
    - test_ratchet_does_not_revert_on_demotion replaced by
      test_demotion_reverts_track_for_non_subscribed (now expects False)
    - Added test_subscribed_clan_immune_to_demotion

2026-06-29
- Fixed: [ORPHANED-CWL] log explosion (925 k entries / 310 MB log) and
  permanent orphan-retry loop caused by CWL prep-state temp files written
  during CoC server maintenance that shifted war start_time forward.

  Root cause A — API expired (1 729 files):
    CWL round-5 prep files written June 18-19 (start_time 08:01 UTC) were
    never finalized because CoC maintenance pushed start_time to 09:28 UTC,
    causing save_war_object to write to a different filename on every
    force-save attempt.  By June 29 the CoC API had purged the war data,
    leaving the orphan scanner in an infinite "API returned None → keep
    file → retry" loop.
  Fix: in fetch_and_update_orphan, when API returns None and the filename
    timestamp is >7 days old, delete the temp file immediately with a
    WARNING log.  Prep-state files have 0 attacks; data loss is accepted
    for wars this old.

  Root cause B — start_time drift / 157× force-save loop (143 files):
    After a successful force-save the war_ended JSON was written to the
    maintenance-shifted 09:28 filename, while the original 08:01 prep file
    remained in temp forever (manage_war_files CWL-ORPHAN-SKIP ignores all
    non-war_ended CWL files).  The 09:28 file was cleaned up each cycle but
    the 08:01 file was rediscovered, triggering another force-save.
  Fix A (post-war): after a successful force-save / war_ended finalization,
    re-read the original orphan file; if it is still in non-war_ended state
    (save_war_object wrote elsewhere) delete it.
  Fix B (during-war): in the "still in_war" refresh path, if old_state ==
    'preparation' and the file is still prep after save_war_object, delete
    it — maintenance-extended wars have 0 attacks in the prep snapshot.

  Both fixes are in fetch_and_update_orphan() (QBhelperfunctions.py).
  These measures apply to any future CWL season where CoC maintenance
  occurs during the prep or war phase.

2026-06-26
- Analysis / Recovery: identified and wrote 9,044 missing CWL war_tags for
  the 2026-06-15 bonus season into data/missing_cwl_war_tags.txt.

  Root cause of gap: the ~40 000 newly-promoted Master III+ clans (see fix
  below) had never been tracked before, so wars between two previously-
  untracked clans in the same group were never archived into war_summary.
  Wars where at least one participant was previously tracked were already
  present (those are ~109 925 of 118 848 Master III+ round-war_tags).

  Fix (analyze_cwl_rounds.py — find_missing_war_tags / Rule 1):
  - Narrowed the cwl_league_rounds completeness check from all leagues to
    Master III+ groups only (EXISTS subquery against cwl_league_groups
    league_rank).  This eliminates ~135 000 false positives caused by wars
    between untracked Crystal / Gold clans that are legitimately absent.
  - Result: 8 923 Rule-1 missing + 1 887 Rule-2 asymmetric = 9 044 total.
  - Recovery file written to data/missing_cwl_war_tags.txt; bot re-fetches
    up to 5 000 tags per cycle via process_cwl_recovery_batch().

- Fixed: _harvest_cwl_group_clans() silently skipped existing clans that
  needed promotion to track_war_updates = True.

  Root cause: the function's per-clan loop contained an early `continue` for
  any clan already present in clan_name_cache, so clans that were originally
  discovered in a lower league (Crystal / Gold) and later promoted to Master
  III+ were never re-evaluated.  Because track_war_updates = False kept them
  out of the 22 h polling loop, their war_league in the clans table was never
  refreshed by _update_clan_metadata either — a chicken-and-egg deadlock that
  caused ~40 000 Master III+ clans to accumulate with track_war_updates = 0.

  Diagnosis: cwl_league_groups.league_rank for season 2026-06-15 shows
  15 633 clans in Master League III, 2 320 in Master II, etc., all with
  clans.track_war_updates = 0 and stale clans.war_league values (Crystal /
  Gold / Silver).  All 59 855 "untracked" group clans were already in the
  clans table — confirming the issue is a missed promotion, not a missing
  insert.

  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py — _harvest_cwl_group_clans):
  - The `continue` that skipped already-known clans is replaced by a
    promotion check: if track_war_updates is False and the clan is not
    actively subscribed, it is promoted immediately (track_war_updates = True)
    and its stale war_league is updated to the group's league tier.
  - No API call needed: the group league is guaranteed to be Master III+
    by the tracked_league guard at the top of the function, so every clan
    in the group is provably playing at that tier.
  - persist_clan() is called to write the promotion to the DB.

  New script (qapbot/scripts/promote_master_clans.py):
  - One-time retroactive fix for the ~40 000 already-accumulated clans.
  - Finds all clans in cwl_league_groups (any season) with league_rank in
    Master III+ that still have track_war_updates = 0 and is_deleted = 0.
  - Dry-run by default; requires explicit --apply and a typed YES confirmation.
  - Auto-detects DEV vs PROD via CONFIG.is_dev_mode / CONFIG.db_path.
  - Also corrects stale war_league values in the same UPDATE.

2026-06-24
- Fixed: DB lock regression introduced by the Layer 2 Tier B daily refresh
  added in the same session.

  Root cause: Tier B called get_league_group(max_age=86400) for *every* CWL
  war with a known war_tag inside the Phase 1 hot path.  On cache cold-start
  (bot restart or 1 h in-memory cache TTL expiry) this cascaded into hundreds
  of concurrent upsert_cwl_league_data async writes, each holding the SQLite
  WAL write lock, starving other sync DB writers and producing a burst of
  [DB-RETRY] database-is-locked errors.

  Fix: removed Tier B entirely from fetch_clan_war_data.  Tier A (unknown
  war_tag → immediate get_league_group before save_war_object) fully maintains
  the pre-save invariant on its own.  New rounds are picked up by Tier A the
  moment their first temp file would be written.  The now-unused _war_tag_l2 /
  _round_l2 tracking variables were also removed, simplifying the block.

  The orphan pre-save guard in process_orphaned_cwl_wars is unaffected.

2026-06-24
- Fixed: CWL temp files could be written with a war_tag not yet present in
  cwl_league_rounds, causing the bot to produce temp files whose round number
  could never be resolved (showing indefinitely as stale prep/in-war files).

  Root cause: the pre-save guard that triggered get_league_group() when a
  war_tag was unknown fired AFTER save_war_object(), so the temp file already
  existed before cwl_league_rounds was populated.  A second path
  (process_orphaned_cwl_wars → fetch_and_update_orphan) had no guard at all.

  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py):
  - fetch_clan_war_data: pre-save Layer 2 Tier A guard (war_tag unknown →
    immediate get_league_group call, max_age=300) moved to BEFORE
    save_war_object().  Post-save Tier B daily refresh (max_age=86400) kept
    after the save using the pre-computed _war_tag_l2 / _round_l2 values so
    no extra DB lookup is needed.
  - process_orphaned_cwl_wars / fetch_and_update_orphan: new pre-save guard
    added after get_league_war() succeeds but before either save_war_object()
    call in that function.

  Invariant enforced: no CWL temp file with a war_tag is ever written while
  that war_tag is absent from cwl_league_rounds for the current season.
  cwl_league_rounds uses INSERT OR IGNORE and has always stored correct round
  numbers on first insert — no change to that behaviour.

  Also added: daily Tier B refresh call (get_league_group, max_age=86400) for
  already-known war_tags so newly-revealed rounds in the same group are picked
  up within 24 h without hammering subscribed clans polled every 3 min.

  Verification: 1376 tests pass.  analyze_cwl_rounds.py for season 2026-06-15
  shows UNKNOWN=0 and Temp round coverage 100% from the first run of the
  season, confirming the invariant holds in production.

  Legend, Titan I and Titan II where those tiers had no participants in 2026-06.

2026-06-20
- Added: /leaderboard command `season` parameter now works across all leaderboard modes,
  not just cwlgroup. Supports both YYYY-MM (monthly CWL) and YYYY-MM-DD (mid-month CWL)
  formats. When `season` is given it overrides month/year, forces CWL mode, and filters
  results to only that exact CWL season via war_summary.cwl_season. The cwlgroup mode
  branch also respects the normalised season directly.

  Specific cases covered:
    1. cwl_only=True, no month/year → all CWL stats for current month (unchanged)
    2. cwl_only=True, month/year given → CWL stats for given month/year (unchanged)
    3. season given (YYYY-MM or YYYY-MM-DD) → stats for that exact CWL season;
       overrides month/year/cwl_only when all are given.

  Changes:
  - [QBhelperfunctions.py] _load_history_filtered: added cwl_season param; when set,
    filters history rows to war_ids with matching cwl_season via war_summary lookup.
    Cache key updated to (clan_tag, month, year, cwl_season).
  - [QBhelperfunctions.py] calculate_leaderboard: added cwl_season param, threads it
    to _load_history_filtered; forces cwl_only=True when cwl_season is given.
  - [QBhelperfunctions.py] generate_leaderboard_text: added cwl_season param; forces
    cwl_only=True when cwl_season is given; passes it to calculate_leaderboard and
    _load_history_filtered.
  - [QBhelperfunctions.py] post_leaderboard_to_discord: added cwl_season param; when
    set uses mode_str "mode_cwl_SEASON" for message tracking instead of month/year.
  - [QBdiscordcmds.py] Updated season parameter description to mention both formats.
    Season parsing normalises YYYY-MM-DD → Monday via normalize_cwl_season(), overrides
    month/year from the season prefix, and forces cwl_only=True before mode resolution.
    cwl_season threaded to generate_leaderboard_text and post_leaderboard_to_discord.
    cwlgroup branch uses cwl_season directly when given.
  - [tests] Updated 3 test assertions / mocks for new 4-element cache key and
    _load_history_filtered signature.



  Problem 1 — '2026-06-18' (Thursday) not normalised to '2026-06-15' (Monday):
    The initial fix script only targeted '2026-06-16' and '2026-06-17'. The CoC API
    also returned '2026-06-18' for some clans. normalize_cwl_season() already handles
    this correctly (Thursday → Monday), but the fix script hadn't covered that date.

  Problem 2 — Empty cwl_season for is_cwl=1 wars:
    After removing the YYYY-MM fallback in build_war_summary(), wars whose JSON had no
    league_group data now stored cwl_season=''. All 4,102 affected rows had matching
    war_tags in cwl_league_rounds, so they were fully backfillable.

  Changes:

  1. [qapbot/scripts/fix_cwl_season_2026_06.py]
     - Added '2026-06-18' to WRONG_SEASONS and _DATED_SEASONS (blanket fix).
     - Added backfill_empty_seasons(): fills cwl_season='' rows in war_summary by
       joining cwl_league_rounds on war_tag. Runs after date-based fixes so the
       source (cwl_league_rounds) is already clean.
     - DEV DB: 56,359 rows fixed (2026-06-18 across 3 tables) + 4,102 empty
       rows backfilled.

  2. [qapbot/db_manager.py] flush_pending_war_writes (APPEND + UPDATE batch paths):
     After each executemany(INSERT OR REPLACE INTO war_summary), immediately backfill
     any CWL rows in the batch with cwl_season='' from cwl_league_rounds (scoped to
     the war_ids in the current batch — near-zero cost when there is nothing to fix).

- Fixed: CWL season normalisation — mid-month soccer CWL entries stored with wrong
  season values ('2026-06', '2026-06-16', '2026-06-17') instead of the correct
  Monday-anchored key ('2026-06-15').

  Root causes (two separate issues):

  1. Some new entries still wrote '2026-06': the YYYY-MM fallback in build_war_summary()
     fired when league_group data was absent in the JSON.

  2. The CoC API returns the per-clan war-start date (e.g. Tue 2026-06-16 or Wed
     2026-06-17) rather than a stable season identifier, so clans in the same CWL
     event got different cwl_season values.

  Fix — new normalize_cwl_season() helper + applied at all write paths:

  1. [qapbot/constants.py] Added normalize_cwl_season(raw) — passes YYYY-MM through
     unchanged (regular monthly CWL); snaps YYYY-MM-DD to the ISO Monday of that week
     so all clans in the same mid-month event share one stable key.
     (2026-06-16 Tue → 2026-06-15, 2026-06-17 Wed → 2026-06-15)

  2. [qapbot/cache_manager.py] Import added. Normalisation applied at:
     - save_war_object: league_group.season written to JSON is now normalised.
     - get_league_group: _lg_season normalised before _process_league_group_response,
       so cwl_league_groups and cwl_league_rounds rows get a consistent key.

  3. [QBcsvhandling.py] build_war_summary: applies normalize_cwl_season() on the
     league_group season read from JSON. Removed the YYYY-MM fallback entirely —
     when no league_group data is present, cwl_season is left as '' rather than
     fabricating a wrong 'YYYY-MM' value.

  4. [qapbot/scripts/fix_cwl_season_2026_06.py] Rewritten to fix all three wrong
     season values in war_summary, cwl_league_groups, and cwl_league_rounds:
     - '2026-06'    (cutoff-filtered, only date > 2026-06-15)
     - '2026-06-16' (blanket — these can only be mid-month CWL rows)
     - '2026-06-17' (blanket — same reason)
     All → '2026-06-15'. DEV DB fixed: 537,938 rows updated across 3 tables.

  using strftime("%Y-%m") or only YYYY-MM format, causing mid-month bonus CWL seasons
  (e.g. "2026-06-16") to be mixed with the regular June CWL ("2026-06") or ignored.

     addition to YYYY-MM / YYYYMM. Default season now queries MAX(cwl_season) from

- Fixed: /status in_war counter depleting from ~30,619 to ~8,710 over a 2+ day runtime
  without a bot restart, while cwl= count showed a matching drop (~27,553 → 5,882).
  All counters are correct at startup (loaded from disk) but degrade during runtime.

  Root cause: Phase-2 orphan processing (process_orphaned_cwl_wars) calls save_war_object
  for OLD CWL war files (from a previous round, now war_ended) belonging to a clan that
  Phase-1 already polled with a NEW active war. Phase-2's save_war_object call
  unconditionally overwrote temp_war_metadata[clan_tag] with the old file's
  state="war_ended" and filepath=old_file. Then Phase-3's manage_war_files archived the
  old file and _archive_and_log_war_file saw state=="war_ended" → evicted the metadata
  entirely. The new file (correctly in_war/preparation) was still on disk but had no
  metadata entry → clan disappeared from all /status counters until the next restart.

  This affected all three counters (in_war, prep, war_ended) — any time an older war
  file was processed concurrently with a newer one for the same clan.

  Fix (qapbot/cache_manager.py — save_war_object):
  Added a "newer war guard" before the temp_war_metadata.setdefault().update() call.
  If temp_war_metadata already contains an entry for this clan with a filepath whose
  YYYYMMDDHHMM timestamp (filename index [2]) is STRICTLY NEWER than the file being
  written, the metadata update and in_war_clan_tags update are skipped entirely.
  Lexicographic comparison of YYYYMMDDHHMM strings equals chronological order.
  Any parse error falls back to allowing the update (safe default).

  1376 tests pass (unchanged).

2026-06-17
- Fixed: CWL preparation-phase war files not written for public-warlog clans during any
  CWL (regular or mid-month bonus / soccer CWL).
  Root cause: fetch_clan_war_data() only invoked _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan() inside
  the except (coc.PrivateWarLog, coc.NotFound) branch. For public-warlog clans, the
  /currentwar endpoint cleanly returns notInWar during the preparation phase (no
  exception is raised). The CWL war-tag fallback — which calls the
  /clanwarleagues/wars/{tag} endpoint that DOES return the correct "preparation" state —
  was therefore never attempted. No prep-phase war files were ever written for
  public-warlog clans; the issue was masked because in_war files (saved during the war
  itself) were written correctly via the private-warlog exception path once the war
  went live.

  Fixes (three files):
  1. qapbot/db_manager.py — added has_active_cwl_group_sync(clan_tag) -> bool
     Synchronous method: returns True iff at least one cwl_league_groups row with
     cwl_ended=0 exists for the clan. Unlike the conservative
     is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync (returns False=unknown when no row), this method
     returns False strictly when no active season row is present — so the guard fires
     only for clans genuinely enrolled in an ongoing CWL. Prevents extra
     get_league_group() API calls for all non-CWL clans on every cycle.

  2. QBhelperfunctions.py — modified if not coc_war_obj: block in fetch_clan_war_data
     Before returning None (which previously happened unconditionally), now checks
     has_active_cwl_group_sync; if True, calls _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan with
     label "CWL-NOTINWAR-FALLBACK". If a war is found (preparation/inwar state), sets
     coc_war_obj and falls through to the normal save path. If not found, returns None.

  3. QBhelperfunctions.py — modified if state == 'not_in_war': block in same function
     Same guard + fallback for the rare case where coc.py returns a War object with
     state=notInWar (empty clan/opponent) instead of None. On success, re-derives
     state/my_clan/opp_clan/opponent_tag from the freshly fetched war object and falls
     through to save.

  Regression safety:
  · Regular CW: no cwl_ended=0 row → has_active_cwl_group_sync=False → no change.
  · Between CWLs: all rows have cwl_ended=1 → False → no change.
  · New clan (no history): no rows → False → no change.
  · Private-warlog CWL clan: exception branch handles it before reaching these blocks.
  · Regular CWL (start of month): same fix applies — the bug existed there too.

  Tests: 2 new test cases added to tests/unit/test_cwl_helpers.py
  (TestFetchClanWarDataNotInWarCwlFallback). 1374 tests pass (was 1372).

2026-06-16
- Fixed: Soccer CWL handicap battle modifiers ('minusOne', 'minusTwo', 'minusThree')
  caused 65 clan war fetches to fail per update cycle on prod.
  Root cause: Supercell introduced new 'battleModifier' API values for the soccer-themed
  bonus CWL. The upstream coc.py library's BattleModifier enum only defined 'none' and
  'hardMode'. Passing any other value to BattleModifier() raised ValueError inside the
  coc.py War.__init__ constructor, before our code received the war object.

  Confirmed from prod log: 65 WarDataFetchError failures, each retried 3 times (195 total
  API calls, 6+ seconds wasted per cycle in backoff sleep for non-fixable parse errors).

  Fixes (three changes):

  1. [QBcore.py] Added `_patch_coc_battle_modifiers()` startup function that injects the
     missing enum members into BattleModifier at runtime using Python's internal enum
     machinery (_value2member_map_, _member_map_). Called immediately at module load,
     before the coc client is created. Survives pip upgrades — no library file changes
     are required in prod. New members: minus_one="minusOne", minus_two="minusTwo",
     minus_three="minusThree".

  2. [venv/Lib/site-packages/coc/enums.py] DEV venv patched directly: added the three
     new enum values and updated the in_game_name lookup with human-readable labels
     ("-1 Handicap", "-2 Handicap", "-3 Handicap"). The venv patch makes local runs and
     tests work even before QBcore.py's startup patch fires.
     NOTE FOR PROD DEPLOY: apply the same venv patch manually OR rely solely on the
     QBcore.py startup patch (which fires before any war fetch).

  3. [qapbot/coc_health.py] coc_retry: added ValueError fast-fail. A ValueError from
     coc.py always indicates a parse error (unknown enum, malformed data) — retrying
     the same call produces the same error every time. Before this fix, each failing
     clan was retried 3 times with exponential backoff (1s + 2s = 3s wasted per clan).
     Now ValueError is caught immediately: logged at ERROR and re-raised without any
     retry or sleep. For 65 failing clans this saves ~195 seconds per cycle.

  4. [tests/unit/test_health_coverage.py] Updated TestCocRetryGenericException:
     - test_retries_then_raises: switched from ValueError to RuntimeError (generic
       errors still retry; parse errors don't).
     - test_value_error_no_retry: new test confirming ValueError fires exactly once.
     - test_recovers_on_retry: switched from ValueError to RuntimeError.
     All 1372 tests pass.

- Fixed: Mid-month bonus CWL season (e.g. Supercell's soccer-themed CWL) and non-
  multiple-of-5 team sizes (e.g. 11v11) were not handled correctly.
  Root causes and fixes:

  1. [QapBot.py] CWL-GROUP-EXPAND: removed `if now.day > 14: _tracked_active_tags = []`
     date guard. Renamed `_cwl_season_now` → `_cwl_season_prefix`. The LIKE-prefix query
     in get_active_cwl_group_member_tags now finds groups for any season in the month
     (e.g. "2026-06-01" and "2026-06-16" both match prefix "2026-06").

  2. [db_manager.py] get_active_cwl_group_member_tags: changed `WHERE cwl_season = ?`
     to `WHERE cwl_season LIKE ?` with param `f"{prefix}%"`. This allows the YYYY-MM
     prefix from `now.strftime("%Y-%m")` to match API-keyed season strings like
     "2026-06-01" or "2026-06-16" stored in cwl_league_groups.

  3. [db_manager.py] Added `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync(clan_tag) -> bool`. Queries
     the most recent cwl_season for a clan by `ORDER BY cwl_season DESC LIMIT 1` and
     returns its cwl_ended flag. Lexicographic ordering works correctly for both legacy
     "YYYY-MM" and new "YYYY-MM-DD" formats. Returns False conservatively when no entry
     exists. Used by items 4 and 5 below to replace all `day > 14` date guards.

  4. [QBhelperfunctions.py] generate_cwlinfo_embeds: replaced the nine-line comment block
     plus `if _now.day > 14` + `is_cwl_ended_for_clan_sync(clan_tag, _now_season)` with
     a single `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync(clan_tag)` call. No day-of-month
     assumptions remain; the per-clan cwl_ended flag is the sole signal.

  5. [QBhelperfunctions.py] _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan: same replacement as item 4.
     Removed `_now_dt`, `_now_season`, and both guards. Function now relies entirely
     on is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync (returns False → fetch proceeds; 404 from API
     is caught and logged as normal).

  6. [QBcsvhandling.py] build_war_summary: cwl_season now sourced from the API's season
     field (`league_group_data.season`, e.g. "2026-06-16") stored in the war JSON.
     Falls back to `f"{yr}-{mo:02d}"` derived from war start_time for historic records
     that pre-date this change. This eliminates the "2026-06" key collision between a
     regular and a mid-month bonus CWL season.

  7. [QBdiscordcmds.py] cwlgroup subscription dispatch: default season for current-
     month now fetched via `get_latest_cwl_season_for_clan()` instead of `strftime`.
     Avoids a "2026-06" key that would miss "2026-06-16" entries in the DB.

  8. [QBdiscordcmds.py] /cwlopponent command: removed `if _now.day <= 14` guard around
     the live `get_league_group()` call. Always tries the live API so a mid-month CWL
     returns the current season's opponents rather than DB archive data.

  9. [QBhelperfunctions.py] Predicted-round team-size inference: added 11 to the list
     `(5, 10, 11, 15, 30)`. Without this, an 11-member eligible roster was rounded up
     to 15, causing predicted rounds to display a 15v15 roster for an 11v11 CWL.

  10.[QBhelperfunctions.py] _cwl_compute_skill_factors season label: changed
     `season == current_month` to `season.startswith(current_month)` so the "(current)"
     tag appears correctly for API-keyed seasons like "2026-06-16" when the current month
     prefix is "2026-06".

  11.[tests/unit/test_cwl_helpers.py] TestFindActiveCwlWarGuards: replaced the three
     original tests (which patched `datetime.now` to simulate day > 14) with three new
     tests that use `is_latest_cwl_season_ended_sync`. The day-of-month patching is no
     longer needed. All 1371 tests pass.

  Note: `is_cwl_ended_for_clan_sync(clan_tag, cwl_season)` and
  `is_cwl_ended_for_clan(clan_tag, cwl_season)` are retained for callers that already
  hold an exact season key (e.g. the cwlgroup subscription dispatch in QapBot.py and the
  /cwlopponent archive fallback). They continue to work without modification.
