2026-06-13
- Fixed: cwl_league_groups.league_rank storing stale (pre-promotion) league for CWL-only
  clans whose get_clan() had never been called since their last promotion/demotion.
  Root cause: the get_league_group API endpoint does not return warLeague per clan, so
  _process_league_group_response falls back to clan_name_cache. CWL-only clans with a
  private war log and no active subscriptions (track_war_updates=False) never trigger
  the 22h passive poll between seasons, leaving clan_name_cache stale from the previous
  season. The stale value was locked in permanently by update_cwl_league_rank's
  "WHERE league_rank IS NULL" guard, which rejected the correct value written the next day.
  Confirmed case: clan #2RY8GPRJ0 promoted Crystal I → Master III for 2026-06, but
  its group (13e5d07e52105722) had all 8 clans stored as "Crystal League I".
  Fix (two changes):
  1. db_manager.py – update_cwl_league_rank: changed SQL guard from
       WHERE league_rank IS NULL
     to:
       WHERE (league_rank IS NULL OR cwl_ended = 0)
     This allows the correct league to overwrite a stale one during the active season,
     while cwl_ended = 1 freezes the row once the season ends (preventing a post-season
     get_clan() returning the NEW league from overwriting the historical value).
  2. cache_manager.py – _process_league_group_response fallback: added 8-day staleness
     check on last_checked_via_api. If all clan_name_cache entries for a group are older
     than 8 days, one get_clan() API call is fired for the first candidate clan. The fresh
     response updates clan_name_cache in-place (via _update_clan_metadata) and the correct
     war_league is then read and written to the DB. At most 1 extra API call per group per
     CWL season; zero cost in the common case where at least one clan is fresh.
     Safety: even if get_clan() runs after season end and returns the post-promotion league,
     the cwl_ended = 0 guard (fix 1) prevents it from being written.
  Also added timedelta and timezone to the top-level import in cache_manager.py.
  Data correction: manually updated all 8 clans in group 13e5d07e52105722 (2026-06)
  from "Crystal League I" to "Master League III".
  Tests: 1371 passed, 5 deselected.
  Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (update_cwl_league_rank), qapbot/cache_manager.py
  (_process_league_group_response, top-level import).

2026-06-13
- Fixed: analyze_cwl_rounds.py --fetch-missing silently skipped trailing CWL rounds (rounds
  6 and 7 for most clans) and produced wildly under-counted recovery files.
  Root causes (three separate bugs):
  1. Missing Rule 3 in find_missing_war_tags(): Rule 1 requires a higher round to already
     exist (impossible for round 7, the last round) and Rule 2 requires at least one side
     of the war to already be in war_summary. When neither condition holds — as is normal
     for the trailing round(s) of CWL — both rules silently passed and the war_tags were
     never added to the recovery file.
  2. "Confirmed incomplete clans" summary used HAVING COUNT(*) != MAX(round_number).
     MAX(round_number) is the clan's own highest archived round, not the group's true max
     round count. A clan with rounds 1–6 in a 7-round group evaluated 6 != 6 = False and
     was never counted as incomplete.
  3. The summary query did not join cwl_league_rounds for the ground-truth group size, so
     it could never detect that round 7 was still missing.
  Fix:
  - Added Rule 3 to find_missing_war_tags(): trailing-round detection. Fires when a clan's
    highest archived round is below a round that exists in cwl_league_rounds for its group,
    AND at least one other clan in the same group already has that round (or higher) archived
    (proof the round has ended). Implemented in Python via three bulk SELECTs + in-memory
    dict lookups to avoid a timeout-inducing multi-way correlated JOIN across 94k × 330k rows.
  - Fixed the incomplete-clans summary query to LEFT JOIN cwl_league_rounds and use the
    group's true max round (COALESCE(lr.max_rounds, ws.max_round)) instead of the clan's
    own MAX(round_number).
  Result (2026-06 season):
    Confirmed incomplete clans : 1 → 2,065
    Total missing war records  : 1 → 2,136
    War_tags written to recovery file : 4 → 3,799
  File changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py (find_missing_war_tags, summary query).

2026-06-05
- Added: /analyse cwl_opponent command (QBdiscordcmds.py, QBhelperfunctions.py).
  Description: New subcommand of /analyse that shows a per-player stats table for a
  selected opponent clan from the requesting clan's CWL league group.
  Flow: user selects their clan (same autocomplete as /leaderboard) → bot fetches the
  live CWL league group (or falls back to most recent DB season when CWL is not active)
  → bot shows an ephemeral dropdown with up to 7 opponent clans → user selects one →
  bot computes the table and posts it as a non-ephemeral channel message.
  Roster source: live CWL group member list when active; DB get_cwl_roster_sync() when
  CWL is over.
  Stats per player (as hyperlinks to in-game profile):
    - CWL Attack Skill = (3★ − 1★) / n × 100%
    - CW Attack Skill = same for regular CW wars
    - Reliability = total attacks used / total max attacks × 100%
    - Activity = time-normalised attack participation rate (identical formula to /whois player)
  Performance: all stats for up to 35 players computed in 3 SQL queries via new
  compute_roster_stats_sync() helper (bulk IN-clause queries + Python post-processing).
  Embeds split dynamically to stay under Discord's 4096-char description limit.
  Tests: full test suite ran — 1371 passed, 5 deselected.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (analyse_cwlopponent command + _cwlopponent_select_callback
  + autocomplete), QBhelperfunctions.py (compute_roster_stats_sync, build_cwl_opponent_embeds).

- Enhanced: `/analyse cwl_opponent` presentation, roster selection, and table rendering.
  Summary: polish and correctness pass over multiple UX and data-source edge cases.
  - Title now shows `CWL Round N Opponent: <clan_name>` when the round is known, otherwise
    falls back to `CWL Opponent: <clan_name>`.
  - Dropdown labels resolved from live `league_group.rounds` when available so preparation
    (round n) war tags are shown; fallback lookup uses `war_summary` + `cwl_league_rounds`
    and `CACHE.temp_war_metadata` for the current active war.
  - Roster selection is now round-aware. Source priority:
      1. Ended rounds → exact per-round lineup from `war_attacks` by `war_id` (DB)
      2. In-war / Preparation with known `war_tag` → live API via `get_league_war(war_tag)`
      3. Future/unstarted rounds → `league_group.clans[x].members` (registered roster)
      4. Last-resort → `get_cwl_roster_sync()` (most-recent archived war)
  - The per-player table is rendered inside a monospace code block and uses aligned
    fixed-width columns; the player `tag` column was removed to avoid line-wrapping.
  - Player names now reuse the leaderboard pipeline (`best_practice_player_cell`) so
    display, truncation and gap-filling match the existing `currentwar` leaderboards.
  - Name column width reduced (now 13 chars) to ensure rows fit inside Discord embeds.
  - Dropdown message is deleted after selection; roster rows are numbered by `map_position`
    (war lineup order) and sorted accordingly.
  - Introduced `war_info_map` (opponent → round_number, war_id, war_tag, state) populated
    from `war_summary` + `cwl_league_rounds` and enriched from `league_group.rounds`.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (analyse_cwlopponent, war_info_map enrichment, callback
    roster logic, autocomplete/callback plumbing), QBhelperfunctions.py (build_cwl_opponent_embeds
    rewrite, compute_roster_stats_sync), qapbot/formatting.py (reused best_practice_player_cell).
  Tests: full test suite ran after changes — 1371 passed, 5 deselected.

- Fixed: [CMD-DONE] log line missing user name — only logged user ID (QBdiscordcmds.py).
  Root cause: _log_cmd_done() used interaction.user.id directly instead of the user object,
  so the format was "user=<USER_ID>" instead of "user=<username> (<USER_ID>)".
  Fix: capture interaction.user and log "user={user} ({user.id})" — identical to _log_cmd().
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (_log_cmd_done).

- Fixed: ServerDisconnectedError (and sibling aiohttp network errors) logged as [ERROR] instead
  of [WARNING] and attempted a dead-connection reply (QBcore.py).
  Root cause: on_app_command_error only had an early-exit guard for discord.NotFound(10062).
  aiohttp.ServerDisconnectedError, ClientConnectionResetError, and ClientConnectorError are
  not discord.HTTPException subclasses, so they fell through to the generic "Unhandled slash
  command error" branch: logged [ERROR] + full traceback, then tried to send a user reply
  through the already-dead connection (which silently failed). Observed in /status at 22:41
  during the DNS outage recovery window when the aiohttp connection was still torn down.
  Fix: added a second early-exit block in on_app_command_error that catches these three
  aiohttp.ClientError subclasses before logging.error(), logs [WARNING] only, and returns
  immediately — same pattern as the existing NotFound(10062) guard. Also added import aiohttp
  to QBcore.py (was not previously imported at module level).
  Files changed: QBcore.py (import aiohttp, early-exit block in on_app_command_error).

- Fixed: /ping reported high latency (used WS heartbeat) instead of actual API RTT
  (QBdiscordcmds.py + translations).
  Root cause: `QBcore.bot.latency` is the WebSocket heartbeat RTT (background probe,
  updated infrequently and routed via the gateway), not the per-request HTTP API
  round-trip time used when replying to an interaction. We previously displayed
  that WS value, which is both stale and typically higher than the immediate
  HTTP POST latency.
  Fix: Rewrote `/ping` to measure the real API latency by timing `await
  interaction.response.defer()` (actual HTTP round-trip) and also report the
  current WS heartbeat RTT for diagnostics. Updated translation keys to accept
  `api_ms` and `ws_ms` and display both values. Tests: full test suite ran —
  1371 passed, 5 deselected.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (ping command timing), qapbot/translations/en.json,
  qapbot/translations/de.json.

- Reverted: /ping experimentation and restored `bot.latency` as the canonical ping metric.
  Summary: Several approaches were tried to expose a "ping" value (dispatch via
  `interaction.created_at`, timing `defer()` RTT, and a combined RTT/dispatch display).
  Findings:
    - `interaction.created_at` is a Discord-side timestamp; comparing it to the bot's
      system clock is vulnerable to clock skew and load-balancer routing variance.
    - Timing `defer()` measures the REST API POST RTT (valid same-host monotonic timing)
      but is not the same as the lightweight WebSocket heartbeat RTT and shows much
      higher variance because it includes TLS and HTTP processing.
    - The original `bot.latency` is the discord.py WebSocket heartbeat RTT: both
      timestamps are on the bot host, low-overhead, and a technically sound RTT metric.
  Decision: revert to `bot.latency` as the primary "ping" shown to users. It is the
  most stable, same-machine RTT metric and aligns with expectations for a Discord bot's
  heartbeat latency. Experimental code added during debugging was reverted.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (reverted to use `bot.latency`), qapbot/translations/*
  Tests: full test suite ran after changes — 1371 passed, 5 deselected.

2026-06-04
- Fixed: analyze_cwl_rounds.py --fetch-missing writing 108,521 tags instead of the ~509 truly
  missing wars (qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py).
  Root cause: find_missing_war_tags() used a candidates query that pulled ALL war_tags for the
  season from cwl_league_rounds (108,521 entries) and subtracted those already in war_summary.
  This incorrectly flagged ongoing/prep wars as missing — they simply hadn't ended yet.
  Fix: replaced the entire function with two precise, independent rules:
    Rule 1 (gap detection): clan has a round N row in war_summary → any round M < N absent for
      that clan in war_summary is definitively missing; the war_tag for M is fetched from
      cwl_league_rounds. Ongoing wars can never trigger this (no higher round archived yet).
    Rule 2 (asymmetric archive): war_summary has a row for clan A in war_tag X (opponent=B),
      but clan B — which has other CWL rows this season (was tracked) — has no row for war_tag X.
  The two result sets are unioned and returned sorted. The output count now correctly reflects
  only wars confirmed missing by these two criteria.
  Files changed: qapbot/scripts/analyze_cwl_rounds.py (find_missing_war_tags() rewritten).

2026-06-04
- Hardened: All interaction.response.defer() calls wrapped via _safe_defer() helper (QBdiscordcmds.py).
  Root cause: During network instability (DNS failures, WebSocket reconnects), the Discord gateway
  may close and reopen mid-command. The replacement WebSocket session invalidates the old interaction
  token, so defer() raises discord.NotFound (code 10062 — Unknown Interaction). Without a local
  guard, this propagated to the global on_app_command_error handler (which already silenced it), but
  left state undefined for handlers that set flags before calling defer().
  Fix: Added _safe_defer(interaction, *, thinking, ephemeral) → bool helper near the top of
  QBdiscordcmds.py. Returns True on success, False on NotFound (logs WARNING with command/user/guild
  context). All 36 bare await X.response.defer() call sites (slash commands, modal on_submit
  handlers, select/context-menu callbacks) replaced with if not await _safe_defer(X, ...): return.
  The MAINTENANCE_START handler already had its own specialised try/except (resets maintenance_mode
  on failure) and was left unchanged. Only two await .defer() calls remain in the file: one inside
  _safe_defer itself, one in the MAINTENANCE_START try block.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (added _safe_defer(), updated 36 call sites).

- Hardened: DNS outage circuit breaker added to coc_retry() (qapbot/coc_health.py).
  Root cause: ISP DNS failures (observed 2026-06-03 22:00 – 2026-06-04) caused every concurrent
  CoC API fetch to raise aiohttp.ClientConnectorDNSError. Without a circuit breaker, each of the
  ~1000+ concurrent clan fetches made 3 retry attempts, flooding the log with redundant ERROR
  entries and wasting async thread pool capacity during a recovery window where all retries fail.
  Fix: added _dns_failure_detected flag (same pattern as the existing CoC maintenance circuit
  breaker). The first DNS error per cycle logs a WARNING and sets the flag; all subsequent
  coc_retry() calls that encounter a DNS error fast-fail immediately at DEBUG level. The flag is
  cleared by clear_dns_detection() at the start of each update cycle so the next cycle re-probes
  normally. clear_dns_detection() is now called in main() alongside clear_maintenance_detection().
  Files changed: qapbot/coc_health.py (added aiohttp import, _dns_failure_detected flag,
  clear_dns_detection(), is_dns_failure_detected(), DNS branch in except Exception handler),
  QapBot.py (added clear_dns_detection to import and main() cycle reset).

2026-06-03
- Fixed: /list Tracked Clans crashing with [Errno 104] Connection reset by peer (QBdiscordcmds.py).
  Root cause: make_chart_bytes() (matplotlib rendering) was called synchronously on the asyncio
  event loop. On server-machine ARM hardware rendering takes 15–20 s, blocking the entire event loop.
  During that block Discord gateway heartbeats could not be sent → Discord closed the connection
  → followup.send() tried to write to a dead socket → ClientOSError Errno 104.
  The concurrent update cycle was coincidental, not causal.
  Fix: wrap the blocking call in asyncio.to_thread() so rendering runs in a thread pool worker
  while the event loop stays free for heartbeats and other coroutines.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (TRACKED_CLANS handler).

- Fixed: /admin Start Maintenance leaving bot in unresponsive limbo when issued during a busy
  event loop (QBdiscordcmds.py).
  Root cause: maintenance_mode = True is set BEFORE interaction.response.defer(). If the
  interaction expired (Discord's 3 s window elapsed — e.g. because the event loop was stressed
  after a WebSocket reconnect triggered by the matplotlib blocking bug), defer() raised
  NotFound(10062). The global error handler caught it and logged "Interaction expired before
  response in /admin" — but maintenance_mode was never reset. Result: all user commands blocked,
  no new update cycles, but resources (DB, CoC client) still open. Bot appeared alive but
  inconsistent. Triggered this session by the /list Tracked Clans matplotlib freeze causing a
  reconnect, followed by /admin Start Maintenance hitting the stressed event loop.
  Fix: wrap defer() in try/except discord.NotFound; reset maintenance_mode = False and return
  on failure so the bot remains fully operational and the user can retry.
  Files changed: QBdiscordcmds.py (MAINTENANCE_START handler).

- Fixed: "generator already executing" crash in process_orphaned_cwl_wars() (QBhelperfunctions.py).
  Root cause: for each CWL war match, both sides (#CLAN_A and #CLAN_B) have separate temp files
  and appear as separate entries in orphaned_wars. Both tasks call get_league_war(war_tag) and
  receive the same shared coc.War object from _league_war_cache. Both then call
  asyncio.to_thread(CACHE.save_war_object, final_war, clan_tag) simultaneously. Inside
  save_war_object(), simple_clan/simple_member iterate coc.War.members and coc.War.attacks —
  which are generator-backed properties in coc.py. Two threads simultaneously advancing the
  same generator raises ValueError: generator already executing.
  Observed: war #8R0GYPL22 — clan #2VRG9GCQ succeeded; #2PQ8L0V99 crashed with this error.
  Fix: per-war-tag asyncio.Lock (_war_save_locks dict) acquired before each asyncio.to_thread
  call in fetch_and_update_orphan(). The lock serializes the two save_war_object calls for both
  sides of the same match, preventing concurrent generator iteration. API fetches remain fully
  parallel (the lock only guards the save, not the get_league_war call).
  Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (process_orphaned_cwl_wars).

- Analysed: temp_war_objects in-memory cache size and eviction strategy (cache_manager.py).
  Measured actual Python dict size: avg 61 KB/entry (≈2× JSON on disk), not the 40 KB
  previously documented. Updated _MAX_TEMP_WAR_OBJECTS comment to reflect the corrected
  figure: 100,000 entries ≈ 6.1 GB (sized against the prod server's RAM budget).
  Access pattern (corrected understanding):
    · Subscribed (actively tracked) clans: ~10–20 total. Polled every cycle (~5 min).
      get_current_war_data() called every cycle for notifications/leaderboards. Hot.
    · Passive clans: ~100k during CWL peak. Polled ~5× per war via the 22h routine +
      intelligent backdating. get_current_war_data() almost never called between polls —
      war notifications skip non-subscribed clans; manage_war_files uses temp_war_metadata
      (always resident). Effectively write-only between polls.
  Evaluated LRU vs FIFO:
    · LRU IS theoretically better here: subscribed clans (polled every 5 min) stay MRU-hot;
      cold passive entries drift to LRU-end and are evicted first if pressure arises.
    · However, cap (100k) ≥ working set (~100k passive war clans), so no eviction fires
      in practice. Strategy choice is irrelevant when there is no cache pressure.
    · Bulk eviction and skip-one eviction: miss count = max(0, W−C) regardless of which
      entries are chosen; only redistributes victims, not total evictions. Not beneficial.
  Conclusion: current implementation (plain dict, FIFO, cap=100k) is correct and sufficient.
  The cap is a memory ceiling, not an eviction tuning problem. If memory pressure ever
  becomes a concern, switching to LRU (OrderedDict + move_to_end) would be the right move
  — it would protect the subscribed clans and evict cold passive entries first.
  Files changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (_MAX_TEMP_WAR_OBJECTS comment updated only).

- Enhanced: /admin Check Logs now shows throttle backlog stats (cycles throttled, min/max/avg deferred clans)
  Parses [THROTTLE] lines: "Capped ... (deferred N to next cycle)" to extract the deferred
  clan count per throttled cycle. Displays as: "X/Y cycles throttled — Min: A | Avg: B | Max: C"
  where X=throttled cycles, Y=total cycles (from [CYCLE-END]), A/B/C use comma formatting
  for large numbers (e.g. 9,926). Shows "0/Y cycles throttled ✅" when no throttling occurred.
  Avg is over throttled cycles only (not all cycles), giving meaningful backlog size context.
  Files changed: qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py (scan_logs, format_log_summary).

- Fixed: save_war_object() FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP now correctly identifies CWL wars in archive
  Root cause: the skip check built a 3-part archive filename
  ({CLAN}_{OPP}_{TS}_war_data.json) but CWL wars are archived with a 4-part name
  ({CLAN}_{OPP}_{TS}_{WARTAG}_war_data.json). os.path.exists() always returned False
  → _in_archive was always False → the skip condition (archive+history+db=war_ended)
  was never satisfied → temp file written every cycle for already-finalized CWL wars.
  Symptom: archive_differs=1 and late_updated_DB=1 every 5-min cycle for clan
  #2RLU2QCL2 (StayAwake). The CoC API returns the old war_ended CWL war (#8GP0U88G0
  from 2026-05) via /currentwar; each cycle: temp written (fresh seconds_until) →
  ARCHIVE-DIFFERS fires → archive overwritten → temp deleted → repeat.
  Fix: moved _cwl_war_tag_clean/_is_cwl_war extraction before the skip block; added
  a 4-part filename fallback check: if 3-part not found and is_cwl, try 4-part too.
  Files changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (save_war_object FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP block).

- Fixed: Phase-2 of load_all_temp_war_stats() now parses JSON for subscribed clans only
  (was: subscribed + all state=="in_war" clans, which meant ALL clans because Phase 1
  always hardcodes state="in_war" due to CoC maintenance extension semantics).
  Root cause: Phase 1 cannot synthesize "war_ended" from filename timestamps because
  CoC maintenance extends ongoing wars by the downtime duration — making start+24h
  an unreliable end-time upper bound. So state is always "in_war" in Phase 1; the
  Phase-2 filter `state == "in_war"` was therefore always True → all 44K-100K files
  parsed → 95s startup on server-machine during CWL season.
  Fix: Phase 2 condition changed to `clan_tag in subscribed_tags` (typically ~10-20 clans).
  Non-subscribed clans get temp_war_stats={} until Phase-3 repopulates them in the
  first update cycle (~5-10 min). Safe because:
    · _get_active_wars() already skips clans with empty stats (if not temp_stats: continue)
    · Notifications/leaderboards are only sent for subscribed clans anyway
    · War-critical throttle exemption uses temp_war_metadata (Phase 1, unchanged)
    · manage_war_files, Phase-3B skip-until use temp_war_metadata (Phase 1, unchanged)
    · /currentwar player search has a ~5-min cold-start gap for non-subscribed clans
  Impact: startup Phase-2 drops from 44K-100K JSON reads to ~10-20 reads (<1 s).
  Also: removed the now-redundant 6h look-ahead window logic and its constant
  _STATS_LOAD_LOOKAHEAD_HOURS; added comprehensive docstring explaining the
  maintenance-extension reason for hardcoding state="in_war" in Phase 1.

2026-06-02
- Changed: Throttle cap reduced 15000 → 5000 inactive clans/cycle (QapBot.py).
  With the no_war timestamp bug fixed, all polled clans now leave the backlog for 22h
  (not just the ~18% that previously had war data). 5000/cycle is more than sufficient
  to keep the backlog near zero and avoids unnecessary API load on the CoC servers.
  Context: cap was raised to 15000 this session as a temporary measure to drain the
  94K backlog; now that steady-state is reached (backlog=0), 5000 is appropriate.

- Removed: Special CWL recovery sleep reduction logic (QapBot.py).
  The `sleep_interval // 10` when `CACHE.pending_cwl_recovery` was non-empty is now
  redundant: the dynamic sleep (max(30, sleep_interval - cycle_elapsed)) already
  reduces idle time proportionally to how long recovery cycles take. Removing the
  special case simplifies the sleep path with no loss of recovery speed.

- Changed: Dynamic sleep duration — cycle time subtracted from sleep interval (QapBot.py).
  Previously: fixed sleep_interval (e.g. 300s) after every cycle regardless of how long
    the cycle took, so fast cycles (20s) still waited the full 5 min, giving a 5min20s
    wall-clock period between cycle starts.
  Now: sleep = max(30s, sleep_interval - cycle_elapsed). A 20s cycle sleeps 280s,
    keeping the wall-clock period at ~300s and evening out bot operations. The 30s
    floor ensures Discord heartbeats and interactions always have an idle window.
  CWL recovery path unchanged (continues to use sleep_interval // 10).
  The sleep log now includes the cycle duration for easy monitoring, e.g.:
    "Sleeping for 280 seconds. (cycle took 20.3s)"

- Fixed: LIFETIME log ([LIFETIME] lines) showing throttle_backlog=0 (qapbot/cache_manager.py).
  Root cause: _format_stats_log_lines() reads throttle_backlog from the `stats` dict passed
    to it. For the CYCLE-SUMMARY path it receives cycle_stats (correct). For the LIFETIME path
    it receives lifetime_stats — which never contains throttle_backlog since it is a per-cycle
    gauge, not a lifetime accumulator. So the LIFETIME log always showed backlog=0.
    The previous fix (preserve across reset_cycle_stats) only helped /status (which calls
    format_lifetime_summary(), which reads cycle_stats directly) but not format_lifetime_log().
  Fix: In _format_stats_log_lines(), fall back to self.cycle_stats for the gauge when
    throttle_backlog is absent from the passed stats dict. Both CYCLE-SUMMARY and LIFETIME
    now show the last computed post-cycle backlog value.

- Fixed: LIFETIME summary and /status showing throttle_backlog=0 (qapbot/cache_manager.py).
  Root cause: throttle_backlog is a gauge written at the end of each cycle. reset_cycle_stats()
    calls self.cycle_stats.clear(), which wiped it. So every cycle start the gauge was gone,
    and format_lifetime_summary() / /status fell back to cycle_stats.get("throttle_backlog", 0) = 0.
  Fix: reset_cycle_stats() now preserves throttle_backlog across the clear by saving and
    restoring it, keeping it as the last computed post-cycle value across cycle boundaries.

- Fixed: throttle_backlog gauge showing pre-cycle snapshot instead of post-cycle remainder
         (QapBot.py).
  Root cause: CACHE.cycle_stats["throttle_backlog"] was set to _overdue_total (the
    count of overdue clans before capping) at the start of the cycle and never updated.
    So both CYCLE-SUMMARY and /status always showed the backlog from the beginning of
    the last cycle, not the actual remaining count after this cycle's polls completed.
    Example: cycle started with 87522 overdue, polled 14964 → summary still showed 87522.
  Fix: After Phase 3 completes (and all _phase3_ts_batch timestamp writes are done),
    overwrite throttle_backlog with max(0, _overdue_total - inactive_count).
    inactive_count is the number of inactive clans actually polled this cycle; since
    all no_war clans now get their timestamps updated (see no_war fix below), subtracting
    it from the pre-cycle total gives the true remaining overdue count.
  Impact: CYCLE-SUMMARY and /status now accurately reflect the current backlog state
    at the end of each cycle rather than the state from the beginning.

- Fixed: no_war clans permanently occupying the inactive-clan polling backlog (QapBot.py).
  Root cause: When fetch_clan_war_data() returned None (clan not in war), Phase 3
    used the same code path for both "API said not in war" and "API call failed".
    Neither case updated last_war_update, so all ~12K no_war clans per cycle stayed
    permanently overdue and were re-polled every cycle — consuming 81% of the 15K/cycle
    budget (2847 ok + 12479 no_war; only the 2847 ok clans had timestamps updated).
    This was the primary reason the overdue backlog barely drained despite the cap
    increase from 5K to 15K.
  Fix: Introduced a module-level _NOT_IN_WAR sentinel (dict, distinct from None).
    fetch_single_clan() now returns _NOT_IN_WAR for confirmed no-war API responses
    and None only for real exceptions. Phase 3 updates last_war_update = now when
    _NOT_IN_WAR, so those clans leave the backlog for 22h. Genuine failures still
    skip the timestamp update so they're retried next cycle.
  Impact: Effective drain per cycle jumps from ~2.8K to ~15K clans; the 94K backlog
    should drain in ~a day. "Clan updates complete" log now shows no_war separately
    from failed for clarity.

- Fixed: Lingering preparation temp files removed as stale (throttle starvation).
         (QapBot.py — inactive-clan thundering-herd guard).
  Root cause: NOT a flaw in the prep→in_war replacement logic. Smart-backdating
    correctly schedules a prep clan's in_war-capture poll at battle_start+30min by
    backdating last_war_update so the clan becomes overdue at the right time. But the
    per-cycle throttle (_MAX_INACTIVE_PER_CYCLE) sorted ALL overdue inactive clans by
    raw staleness and kept only the stalest 5000 — and the overdue backlog is
    structurally over-capacity and growing (90,490 → 93,787 over June 1, 94,909 by
    June 2). A freshly-due war clan (staleness ~22h) was repeatedly out-prioritized by
    tens of thousands of staler warless clans, so its time-critical poll was deferred
    past the 24h battle day. The in_war snapshot was never captured → the preparation
    file was never overwritten/finalized → it lingered and was removed as stale
    (e.g. 812 removals after the June 2 09:26 restart, 3497 after June 1 17:32).
  Fix part 1 — War-critical exemption: clans whose temp_war_metadata.state is
    preparation/in_war are now EXEMPT from the per-cycle cap, so their scheduled
    war-capture polls always run on time. Near-zero cost: of ~5050 inactive clans
    polled per cycle, only ~2 are actually in a war (ok≈2, no_war≈5048 in
    CYCLE-SUMMARY). Generic warless overdue clans still fill the remaining budget by
    staleness as before.
  Fix part 2 — Cap raised 5000 → 15000 to drain the persistent backlog. Measured
    throughput: 226 cycles/day × 5000 = 1.13M inactive polls/day vs demand of ~1.04M
    clans needing ~1.09 polls/day — only ~0.3%/day deficit, so the backlog crept up.
    At 15000/cycle the surplus (~10k/cycle) drains the 94K backlog within ~a day and
    keeps it near zero. Monitor cycle duration / CoC API load after deploy.

2026-06-01
- Fixed: CWL group expansion dormant during war-1 preparation of a new season
         (QBhelperfunctions.py, QapBot.py, tests/integration/test_db_manager.py).
  Gap A — On day 1 of a new CWL season, CWL-GROUP-EXPAND added 0 group members.
    Root cause: cwl_league_groups for the new season is populated by get_league_group(),
    which for PUBLIC-warlog clans only fired at war_ended (Layer 2) — never during
    war-1 preparation/in_war. So the new season's group stayed empty until the first
    war finalized, leaving round-1 non-tracked group-mates uncaptured.
    Verified on restored prod data: cwl_league_groups had 0 rows for 2026-06 while
    4881 CWL prep wars were being fetched; CWL-GROUP-EXPAND stopped logging after UTC
    midnight June 1.
    Fix: broadened the Layer-2 league-group trigger to fire on preparation/in_war/
    war_ended (was war_ended only), keeping the per-war_tag idempotency guard so each
    group still triggers exactly one get_league_group() per season.
  Gap B — cwl_ended optimization flag rarely set, so two read paths that rely solely
    on it kept doing work all month on stale current-season groups.
    Purpose of cwl_ended (verified): per-group flag = "this CWL season is complete →
    stop live API polling and serve cached/archived data." Set by
    update_cwl_group_stats_batch() only when ALL clans have >= (n-1) ended rounds, so
    it almost never flips on incomplete capture (2026-05 groups were 99.96% still
    cwl_ended=0 long after that season ended). Per the documented design

    and cwl_ended only handles the days 11-14 tail — but get_active_cwl_group_member_tags
    (CWL-GROUP-EXPAND) and _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan lacked that date guard.
    Fix: applied the documented day>14 season-over date guard to both paths (skip
    expansion / skip league-group fetch past day 14). Did NOT touch the fragile
    all_ended computation.
  Added: TestCwlGroupExpansion in tests/integration/test_db_manager.py covering the

  Issue 1 — Check Data taking 50+ seconds:
    The check loaded all (clan_tag, war_id) pairs from war_attacks (27M+ rows on server-machine)
    into a Python frozenset for the missing_from_db and orphaned_summaries calculations.
    Fix: replaced get_all_war_attacks_existing_sync() + get_all_wars_in_db_sync() calls
    with a new get_all_war_summary_keys_sync() that queries war_summary only (~2.35M rows,
    indexed). war_summary covers all wars including PrivateWarLog wars with no attack rows.
    The orphaned_summaries count is set to 0 (skipped) since computing it requires a full
    war_attacks scan; total_records still reports the correct attack count via the fast
    COUNT(*) in get_global_db_statistics_sync().

    get_recent_war_summaries_sync(days=14) checked 14 days of DB wars against the archive,
    but move_old_archive_files.py moves files older than 7 days to archive_old. The 7-day
    gap caused every war between day 7 and day 14 to appear as "missing from archive".

- Fixed: /admin Check Data falsely reporting 3922 "Wars in Archive but Missing from Database"
  and 1 spurious "duplicate war" (CWL consecutive-round match).
  Root cause 1 (missing_from_db):
    check_database_consistency() used get_all_war_attacks_existing_sync() as the source of
    truth for "is this war in the DB?". PrivateWarLog wars and other wars with no attack
    rows are stored in war_summary only (no war_attacks rows). These 3922 wars were
    correctly archived and in war_summary but showed as "missing" because war_attacks was
    checked exclusively.
  Root cause 2 (duplicate_wars):
    The 48-hour duplicate detection did not exclude CWL wars. CWL clans legitimately fight
    the same opponent in consecutive rounds within 48 hours; flagging these is a false
    positive. get_all_war_summaries_brief_sync() did not return is_cwl so there was no
    way to filter them.
  Fixes (qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py,
         tests/unit/test_phase9_history_and_dbcheck.py,
         tests/unit/test_admin_command_phase5.py):
  · Added get_all_wars_in_db_sync() to db_manager: queries war_attacks UNION war_summary
    so that wars with no attack rows are still recognised as "in the DB".
  · missing_from_db check now uses get_all_wars_in_db_sync() (union) instead of
    get_all_war_attacks_existing_sync() (war_attacks only). The war_attacks-only query
    is retained for the orphaned_summaries count (which intentionally finds wars
    in war_summary with no attack rows — a legitimate diagnostic metric).
  · get_all_war_summaries_brief_sync() updated to also return is_cwl (now returns
    4-tuples: war_id, clan_tag, date, is_cwl).
  · Duplicate detection loop skips any war where is_cwl=1.
  · All comments updated; CWL 4-part archive filename format documented.
  · Updated all affected test mocks (4-tuple, get_all_wars_in_db_sync stub).
  Result: /admin Check Data now reports 0 missing + 0 duplicates for a clean DB.
  All 1353 tests pass.

2026-05-31
- Fixed: CoC API maintenance causing 3204s update cycle (normally ~90s).
  Root cause (identified from qapbot.log.2026-05-26, cycle 09:37-10:30 UTC):
  CoC entered maintenance at 09:37:17 UTC. Each of the ~5052 clans fetched in
  parallel hit coc.Maintenance. coc_retry() had been retrying per-clan: sleep 10s →
  retry → Maintenance → raise. With 20 concurrent slots and 1889 maintenance-failing
  clans, effective wait = 1889 × 30s / 20 slots ≈ 2833s extra on top of normal cycle work.
  Cycle summary showed: failed=1949 (Maintenance=1889) and 3778 [COC-MAINTENANCE] WARNING
  log lines.
  Fix (qapbot/coc_health.py, QapBot.py, tests/unit/test_health_coverage.py):
  · Added module-level _maintenance_detected: bool flag in coc_health.py.
  · coc.Maintenance is now a hard no-retry exception, like coc.NotFound:
    - First hit: set _maintenance_detected=True, log one [WARNING][COC-MAINTENANCE]
      naming the clan and explaining the cycle will skip remaining API calls, raise.
    - Subsequent hits (flag already set): log at DEBUG only, raise.
    - No sleep, no retry — ever. CoC maintenance lasts 10-30+ min; per-clan
      retries within the same cycle are always pointless.
  · Added clear_maintenance_detection() and is_maintenance_detected() helpers.
  · clear_maintenance_detection() called alongside reset_cycle_stats() at cycle
    start (QapBot.py main()) so each new cycle re-probes the API fresh.
  · Per-clan [PHASE-1] ERROR log suppressed to DEBUG when maintenance is already
    detected — eliminates ~1889 redundant log lines per maintenance event.
  · After asyncio.gather() completes, one [PHASE-1] WARNING summarises the total
    count of affected clans and confirms Phases 2-4 still run on existing temp files.
  · TestCocRetryMaintenance class rewritten: new tests assert single-call raise,
    flag-setting, and fast-fail for subsequent calls; setup_method() resets flag
    between tests to prevent state bleed.
  Impact: maintenance cycles complete in ~5 min (fast-fail) instead of ~53 min.
  Log noise: 3778 WARNINGs → 1 WARNING + N DEBUG lines per maintenance event.
  All 1353 tests pass.
- Changed: Inactive clan fetch log lines now grouped in batches of 10 (QapBot.py).
  Background: with 5000 inactive clans per cycle, Phase-1 produced 5000 individual
  "[INACTIVE] Fetching #TAG (22h check)..." lines — the dominant source of log volume.
  Implementation: inactive tags are pre-grouped into chunks of 10 before the gather.
  Each clan carries a (group_index, [tags]) reference.  Inside fetch_single_clan(),
  after acquiring the semaphore (i.e. just before the API call fires), the first clan
  in its group to run logs the whole group header via an asyncio.Lock + double-checked
  set to prevent duplicate logs from concurrent coroutines:
    "[INACTIVE 22H FETCHING] #TAG1, #TAG2, ... #TAG10"
  This means each group header appears immediately before the API calls for that batch
  fire, so any errors/warnings stay adjacent to the relevant fetch info in the log.
  Active clans keep individual [ACTIVE] lines (only ~17 of them).
  Log reduction: 5000 lines → ~500 lines per cycle for inactive fetches (~90% drop).
  All 1353 tests pass.
- Cleaned: Removed all one-time migration code from db_manager.py startup routines.
  All ALTER TABLE / DROP TABLE migrations that had accumulated since Phase 1 are gone;
  the schema now only contains CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS with all current columns
  baked in — safe for fresh installs and restarts alike.  Removed:

  · cwl_league_groups ALTER TABLE loop (5 columns — folded into CREATE TABLE)
  · _clans_migrations (3 ALTER TABLE for clans columns)

  Also stripped all "(replaces X.json)" / "(nested array from X.json)" / "Phase 3"

  All 1353 tests pass.

- Fixed: /whois chart selector — "Unknown interaction" (Discord error 10062) when chart
  generation exceeds Discord's 3-second response window.
  Root cause: _WhoisChartSelectView._on_select called interaction.response.edit_message()
  AFTER asyncio.to_thread(make_*_chart_bytes). If chart rendering took >3 seconds the
  interaction token expired, causing 10062 and an unhandled exception logged as both
  [WARNING] WHOIS-CHART and [ERROR] Ignoring exception in view.
  Fix: defer immediately with await interaction.response.defer() at the start of _on_select,
  then use await interaction.edit_original_response() after chart generation.
  Deferring extends the window from 3 seconds to 15 minutes with no visible loading indicator.
  All 1352 tests pass.
- Fixed: /admin Check Logs hangs indefinitely when bot uptime is long.
  Root cause: the scanner iterated rotated log files newest-first, reading each file entirely
  in reverse, looking for the [INFO] QapBot started marker to stop. With weeks of uptime the
  startup marker is buried in a 7+-day-old rotation, forcing reads of many large files.
  Fix: slice log_files[:2] after sorting — only qapbot.log (today) and the most-recent
  rotation (yesterday) are ever scanned. If the bot started more than two days ago,
  bot_start_date stays None and the summary shows 48 h of recent data instead.
  All 1352 tests pass.

2026-05-23
- Fixed: ArchiveOld.bat — four robustness issues resolved.
  1. Pipeline data-loss risk: removed --remove-files from tar; pipeline now writes to a .tmp
     file inside a (set -o pipefail; ...) subshell. Source files are deleted via xargs rm only
     after a successful mv .tmp → final archive name; on any failure .tmp is removed and sources
     are fully preserved.
  2. SSH exit code ignored: hardcoded exit /b 0 replaced with set SSH_RC=%ERRORLEVEL% /
     endlocal & exit /b %SSH_RC% so callers and scheduled tasks can detect failures.
  3. Race condition: double find (count + archive) replaced by a single find into $(mktemp);
     both wc -l and tar -T read from the same temp file, then rm -f cleans it up.
  4. archive_compressed/ prerequisite: mkdir -p added as the first remote step so the
     destination directory is always guaranteed to exist.

- Fixed: /whois Activity — CWL denominator now uses actual max rounds per season from DB instead
  of a hardcoded constant of 7.
  Root cause: smaller CWL groups (< 8 teams) have fewer than 7 rounds, making the fixed constant
  overestimate the denominator and understate the activity score.
  Implementation:
  · New DB function get_cwl_max_rounds_sync(season_clan_pairs) in db_manager.py.
    Queries cwl_league_rounds → cwl_league_groups (COUNT DISTINCT cwl_round per league_group_id)
    for each (cwl_season, clan_tag) pair; returns {cwl_season: max_rounds}.
    Seasons with no cwl_league_rounds data (group never fetched) are omitted — callers fall back to 7.
  · _player_report_logic (QBdiscordcmds.py): extracts CWL (cwl_season, clan_tag) pairs from war_rows
    using set comprehension [*{...}] (avoids the list() builtin shadowed by the discord /list command);
    added to asyncio.gather as a 5th parallel query.
    Activity loop now uses cwl_max_rounds.get(f"{_y:04d}-{_m:02d}", 7) instead of the constant.
  · _WhoisChartSelectView: stores cwl_max_rounds dict; passes it to make_activity_chart_bytes.
  · make_activity_chart_bytes (chart_player_report.py): new optional cwl_max_rounds parameter.
    CWL bar divisor and overall reference line loop both use _cwl_max.get(season, 7).

2026-05-16
- Changed: player_name_index now indexes ALL players — sentinel rows (attack_order=0, i.e., players
  who were in the war lineup but missed ALL attacks) are now included.
  · Root cause of change: reliability-factor calculation must account for no-shows; excluding
    sentinel rows would silently drop players who never attacked from the name index.
  · Impact: _upsert_player_name_index_in_conn() sentinel exclusion filter removed; backfill script
    WHERE clause removed; search_players_by_name_sync() slow-fallback WHERE removed.
  · Tests: test_sentinel_rows_excluded → test_sentinel_rows_included in both
    TestSearchPlayersByNameSync and TestUpsertPlayerNameIndexInConn.
- Added: API-driven player name change detection and propagation to player_name_index.
  · Root cause: CoC players can rename in-game; war history rows capture the name at match time
    but the live API returns the current name.  The index would stay stale until the next war.
  · Implementation: coc_cache.update_player_info_in_user_accounts() now detects changes between
    player["player_name"] and member.name; stores updates in name_changes list; after persisting
    affected users it propagates changes to CACHE.player_name_index (in-memory) and calls
    db_manager.update_player_name_index_sync() in a thread (async-safe).
  · New method: db_manager.update_player_name_index_sync(updates: List[Tuple[str,str,str]]) —
    batch upsert using _sync_conn + _sync_write_lock; last_seen = current UTC so API names
    always supersede older war-history names.
  · Logging: [PLAYER-NAME-UPDATE] per tag, [PLAYER-NAME-INDEX] summary per clan cycle.
  · Tests: TestUpdatePlayerNameIndexSync (5 tests — new entry, newer wins, older loses,
    empty no-op, not-initialized raises); integration test mock updated with name + player_name
    fields; TestUpdatePlayerInfo mocks updated (_make_member sets m.name = tag, all player dicts
    carry player_name, test_no_changes_no_persist gets explicit cm.player_name_index + db_manager=None).
  · _make_db() test helper updated to set _sync_write_lock (threading.Lock()) to match __init__.
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/coc_cache.py, qapbot/scripts/backfill_player_name_index.py,
         tests/unit/test_db_search_players_by_name.py,
         tests/unit/test_coc_cache_coverage.py,
         tests/integration/test_coc_cache_failures.py.
  All 1352 tests pass.
- Fixed: backfill script docstring was stale — still claimed sentinel rows were excluded after
  that filter was removed.  Updated to document sentinel inclusion and the conditional-upsert
  behaviour (API-sourced names with utcnow() last_seen are never clobbered by re-runs).

2026-05-15
- Added: player_name_index — fast in-memory /whois name search replacing slow LIKE full-table-scan.
  · Root cause: search_players_by_name_sync used LIKE '%substr%' on 50 M row war_attacks table →
    50–100 s on server-machine, completely unusable.
  · Solution: new player_name_index(player_tag PK, player_name, last_seen) SQLite table (~125 K rows).
    Loaded at startup into CACHE.player_name_index: Dict[str,str] in < 100 ms.  In-memory O(n)
    Python scan over 125 K entries = < 5 ms per /whois query.
  · Incremental maintenance: every war write path now calls _upsert_player_name_index_in_conn()
    within the same connection/transaction — no extra commits.  Write paths hooked:
    _flush_pending_war_writes() APPENDS batch, _flush_pending_war_writes() UPDATES batch,
    add_war_data_sync(), update_war_data_sync().
  · Upsert uses ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE WHERE excluded.last_seen > stored (only advances forward).
    Sentinel rows (attack_order=0) excluded from upsert.
  · Cache plumbing: CACHE.player_name_index attr, load_player_name_index() async method called in
    load_all(), search_player_names(query, limit=25) synchronous in-memory search sorted
    alphabetically, capped at 25 (Discord limit).
  · whois_slash updated to call CACHE.search_player_names() directly (no asyncio.to_thread).
  · Backfill script: qapbot/scripts/backfill_player_name_index.py — offline one-time script to
    populate table from existing 50 M rows; chunks of 500 K; idempotent; ~60–120 s on server-machine.
  · 17 new tests (in addition to the existing 13 for the slow fallback): TestUpsertPlayerNameIndexInConn
    (6 tests), TestLoadPlayerNameIndexSync (3 tests), TestSearchPlayerNames (8 tests).
  Files: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBdiscordcmds.py,
         qapbot/scripts/backfill_player_name_index.py,
         tests/unit/test_db_search_players_by_name.py.
  All 1347 tests pass.

2026-05-15
- Optimized: generate_cwlinfo_embeds() skips the CoC API call when CWL is over.
  Two complementary guards before the get_league_group() call:
  1. Date-based: now.day > 14 → go straight to archive. CWL runs ~days 1-10;
     day 14 is the outer bound even for the latest possible group. This covers
     clans with NO cwl_league_groups entry (didn't participate / not tracked),
     for which is_cwl_ended_for_clan_sync() always returns False.
  2. Per-clan DB: cwl_ended=1 in cwl_league_groups → go straight to archive.
     Catches groups that finished on days 11-14, before the date threshold fires.
  Eliminates guaranteed 404 API calls per cwlinfo-subscribed clan every cycle
  after the season closes. Mirrors the guard already on the cwlgroup path.
  File: QBhelperfunctions.py. All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-15
- Added: /admin "Backfill CWL Groups" cycle-safety guards.
  Previously the command ran immediately regardless of update-cycle state, and a new cycle could
  start while the backfill was still making CoC API calls.
  Changes:
  · New QBcore.backfill_idle_event (asyncio.Event, starts set) — cleared while backfill runs,
    set again in a finally block when it completes (success or error). Mirrors the existing
    db_maintenance_idle_event pattern.
  · handle_backfill_cwl_groups() refactored into a thin wrapper + _handle_backfill_cwl_groups_inner().
    Wrapper: (1) awaits cycle_idle_event.wait() (10 min timeout) so it only starts during the
    sleep phase; returns a warning string if the timeout fires. (2) clears backfill_idle_event,
    calls the inner function, sets backfill_idle_event in finally.
  · periodic_main (QapBot.py): added a gate before cycle_idle_event.clear() — if
    backfill_idle_event is not set, logs and awaits it before starting the next update cycle.
    Ensures no cycle starts while backfill API calls are in flight.
  Note: the HAVING COUNT(*) < 7 filter is intentionally left unchanged — the command is designed
  for post-season use when the CoC API still serves the season's group data. Running it mid-season
  would target all active clans (excessive API calls), which is a known limitation documented here.
  Files: QBcore.py, qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py, QapBot.py. All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-15
- Added: /admin "Backfill CWL Groups" command (bot-admin only).
  Fetches CWL league groups from the CoC API for all clans with <7 rounds in war_summary
  for a given season (entered via modal). API calls are deduplicated per group: after one
  clan's group is fetched, all group members are added to a seen set and skipped.
  Writes to cwl_league_groups + cwl_league_rounds via the existing get_league_group() /
  upsert_cwl_league_data() pipeline (idempotent, no duplicate writes).
  Post-fetch analysis compares war_summary round counts against cwl_league_rounds counts
  per clan, reporting three buckets: genuinely short groups (nothing missing), clans with
  real missing war_tags, and clans where the API fetch failed. "Next step" hint to run
  analyze_cwl_rounds.py only shown when actually_missing > 0.
  Files: QBdiscordcmds.py, qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py.

2026-05-15
- Fixed: CWL group standings cwl_ended flag never set for short groups (6 clans / 5 rounds).
  Root cause: all_ended check in get_cwl_group_war_stats() hardcoded >= 7 ended wars per clan.
  For short groups the max is 5, so cwl_ended was never written as 1 — the fast-path cache
  branch was permanently bypassed and standings were recalculated from DB on every request.
  Fix: replaced hardcoded 7 with expected_rounds = max(len(clan_tags) - 1, 1), the standard
  CWL round-robin formula valid for any group size.
  File: QBhelperfunctions.py. All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-14
- Fixed: Discord 502 Bad Gateway in send_user_dm() was logged as [ERROR] with full Cloudflare
  HTML response body. Root cause: discord.DiscordServerError (transient 5xx) was caught by the
  generic Exception handler in send_user_dm(), which logged exc_info=True (full traceback + HTML),
  then returned False. The caller in war_notifications.py then incorrectly logged "DMs disabled or
  bot blocked" for every False return regardless of reason.
  Fix: Added explicit except discord.DiscordServerError in send_user_dm() that logs as WARNING
  with just the HTTP status code and re-raises. Added matching except discord.DiscordServerError
  handlers in _send_aggregated_war_reminder_dm() (re-raises past the "DMs disabled" branch) and
  the outer notification loop (logs WARNING and continues). 502s now produce one clean WARNING line
  with no HTML dump and no misleading "DMs disabled" message. All 1317 tests pass.
  Files: qapbot/cache_manager.py, qapbot/war_notifications.py

2026-05-14
- Modified: Sleep cycle now uses sleep_interval // 10 when CACHE.pending_cwl_recovery is non-empty
  (i.e. CWL backfill recovery still has war_tags queued). This reduces idle time between recovery
  batches (each batch processes up to 5 000 tags) so the 48 k missing wars are recovered ~10×
  faster. Sleep returns to the normal sleep_interval automatically once the queue drains.

  Implementation: QapBot.py periodic_main sleep block — computed _effective_sleep local variable;
  base sleep_interval unchanged. All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-14
- Fixed: find_missing_war_tags() in analyze_cwl_rounds.py silently missed most partially-archived
  CWL wars due to two independent bugs — both rooted in misusing clans.track_war_updates=1 as the
  "is tracked" filter.  track_war_updates only distinguishes active (=1, every cycle) vs passive
  (=0, every ~22 h); BOTH states are fully tracked and their wars ARE archived.
  Bug 1 (candidates query): JOIN clans ON track_war_updates=1 excluded league groups whose only
    tracked members are passive clans.  Fix: removed the track_war_updates filter from the JOIN;
    any clan present in the clans table (either state) now qualifies a group as needing recovery.
  Bug 2 (partial_missing query): used JOIN clans ON c.clan_tag = ws.opponent_tag AND
    track_war_updates=1 to detect wars partially archived (one side present, opponent missing).
    For #20002280V (passive, track_war_updates=0) the join produced 0 rows → its round-5 war
    (#8GR99QU09) was in existing_any (from opponent #VQGJY0U8's row) but NOT in partial_missing
    → silently filtered out.  Fix: replaced the clans JOIN with a war_summary self-join that
    checks whether the opponent_tag has any is_cwl=1 / cwl_season row for this season, making
    the detection tracking-state-independent.
  Impact: OLD partial_missing=155 → NEW=48,417; total recoverable war_tags 48,375 (up from ~few
    hundred), now closely matching the 34,486 missing war records from the war_summary analysis.
  All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-14
- Fixed: [CWL-ROUNDS] INFO log entries repeating every cycle after CWL season ended.
  Root cause (A — wasted API calls): _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan() had no guard against
    calling get_league_group() after CWL ended. Private-warlog clans still trigger the
    PRIVATE-WARLOG fallback path every cycle (get_current_war raises PrivateWarLog), which
    called get_league_group(max_age=300) → CoC API every 5 min → _process_league_group_response
    always fired, even with no active war and nothing new to write.
  Root cause (B — misleading log): _process_league_group_response logged INFO with "upserted"
    unconditionally, even when all INSERT OR IGNORE statements were no-ops (all rows already existed).
  Fix A (QBhelperfunctions.py, _find_active_cwl_war_for_clan):
    Added is_cwl_ended_for_clan_sync() guard before the get_league_group() call.
    If cwl_ended=1 for the clan's current season, return None immediately — no API call.
    Conservatively returns False (allows the fetch) when the clan has no DB row yet.
  Fix A (qapbot/db_manager.py):
    Added is_cwl_ended_for_clan_sync(clan_tag, cwl_season) -> bool using the shared
    _sync_conn() pattern (same as get_cwl_round_for_war_tag_sync).
  Fix B (qapbot/db_manager.py, upsert_cwl_league_data):
    Changed return type from None to int. Uses total_changes() snapshot before/after
    the INSERT OR IGNORE executemany calls to count actual inserts (ignored rows = 0 delta).
  Fix B (qapbot/cache_manager.py, _process_league_group_response):
    Captures the return value (_new_rows). Logs INFO only when _new_rows > 0 (new data written).
    Logs DEBUG when _new_rows == 0 (all rows already present — the post-season no-op case).
  All 1317 tests pass.

    Fast-path cache check: same guard extended to include 'friendly'.

        (X_STARS_N / X_STAR_I) to open space for the new medals column.
      Loads league icon PNG and medal icon PNG via matplotlib.image.imread (try/except;
        degraded rendering when files missing — image still renders correctly).

        left zone; "Medals earned: NNN" text + medal icon in the bottom-right corner.

2026-05-12
- Added: backfill_cwl_rounds_via_api.py — standalone script to fix UNKNOWN round numbers
  for CWL wars where cwl_league_rounds was never populated.
  - Root cause: clans in whole-group dark zones (all 8 clans unsubscribed) never trigger
    get_league_group(); also, wars archived before 2026-05-09 predate the feature.
  - Investigation found 1,559 wars with NULL round_number in 2026-05; 258 affected clans.
  - Script: calls coc_client.get_league_group() for each affected clan; INSERT OR IGNORE into
    cwl_league_rounds; then UPDATE war_summary.round_number.
  - Dedup: only within the current run (seen_group_ids set). INSERT OR IGNORE handles DB-level
    dedup. Do NOT skip groups that already have partial cwl_league_rounds rows (they may have
    been fetched before all rounds were revealed).
  - CLI: python qapbot/scripts/backfill_cwl_rounds_via_api.py [--season YYYY-MM] [--dry-run]
         [--db PATH] [--concurrency N]
  - NOTE: 2026-05 window MISSED — CWL ended ~2026-05-12 before script could run.
    All 258 clans returned 404. The 1,559 UNKNOWN rows for 2026-05 are unrecoverable.
    Run this script EARLY in future seasons (during rounds 2-3) to avoid missing the window.

- Fixed: generate_leaderboard_text() now returns early for cwlgroup mode (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  - Root cause: cwlgroup is an image-only subscription type; if it ever reached the text
    leaderboard path (e.g. before the cwlgroup elif branch was deployed), render_leaderboard()
    would crash with IndexError on cols[4] because cwlgroup's MODE_REGISTRY entry only defines
    4 columns (Rank/Clan/Stars/Destr.%) while the else-branch accessor expects cols[4..5].
  - Production evidence: repeated "[LEADERBOARD-POST] Error processing subscription
    #L2J0C0PY/cwlgroup: list index out of range" from 2026-05-11 00:00 until ~19:34 when
    the bot was restarted with the new cwlgroup elif routing branch.
  - Fix: added explicit early-return guard after MODE_REGISTRY check in generate_leaderboard_text():
      if mode == "cwlgroup":
          return "[cwlgroup] mode is rendered as an image, not text. Subscription routing error."
  - Defence-in-depth: the cwlgroup elif branch in QapBot.py._process_channel already routes to
    generate_cwl_group_image() and continues — this guard is a belt-and-braces fallback.

2026-05-11
- Added: backfill_cwl_groups_from_war_summary.py — standalone migration script to
  reconstruct cwl_league_groups rows for past CWL seasons from war_summary data.
  - Finds all clans in war_summary with a full 8-clan group (exactly 7 distinct opponents).
  - Computes total_stars (with +10 win bonus), total_destruction, and group_rank.
  - Derives league_rank via majority/median vote from the clans table.
  - Uses INSERT OR IGNORE + targeted UPDATE — fully idempotent, safe to re-run.
  - Sets cwl_ended=1 for all inserted rows.
  - Auto-detects prod vs dev DB path; --dry-run flag available.
  - CLI: python qapbot/scripts/backfill_cwl_groups_from_war_summary.py 2026-04 [--dry-run]

- Fixed: /leaderboard cwlgroup error message showed raw clan dict instead of clan name.
  Root cause: clan_name_cache stores a dict per tag; code passed the whole dict as
  {clan_name}. Fix: extract entry.get("name") with fallback to tag.
- Improved: cwlgroup_no_data error message rewritten (en + de).
  - Removed confusing "KLA-Gruppen" terminology.
  - Now shows: "Für <clan name> (<tag>) wurden in Saison <season> keine CWL-Daten
    in der Datenbank gefunden."
  - Always includes both clan name and tag in parentheses.

- Fixed: generate_cwl_group_image() race condition producing blank (brown) images when
  two channels subscribed to cwlgroup for the same clan process concurrently.
  Root cause: plt.subplots() / plt.savefig() / plt.close() share matplotlib's global
    "current figure" state. Two asyncio.to_thread() workers raced on it — one thread's
    savefig captured the other thread's blank figure.
  Fix: replaced global pyplot API with the thread-safe OO matplotlib API:
    Figure(figsize=...) + FigureCanvasAgg(fig) + fig.add_axes() + fig.savefig().
    Each call creates a fully independent Figure object with no shared state.
  Also removed the now-unused plt / matplotlib.pyplot import from the function.

  - X_NAME: X_L+1.10 → X_L+1.30 (+0.20 right).
  - X_STARS_N: X_R−1.64 → X_R−2.10; X_STAR_I: X_R−1.54 → X_R−1.95 (more separation).
  - X_DESTR: X_R−0.28 → X_R−0.20 (further right, avoids star overlap).

  - Header box height H_HDR: 0.82 → 1.091 in (+33%).
  - Header league-name font: 24 → 31.9; subtitle font: 14 → 18.6 (+33% each).

- Improved: /leaderboard cwlgroup season-resolution logic redesigned.
  - No month/year given: tries current month; if no data, automatically falls back to

  - year without month: returns a user-friendly error asking to also specify a month.

    the base card — only the two top corners are visible and rounded.

  - H_ROW reduced by 10% twice (0.65→0.585→0.527); light strip height auto-scales.
  - H_GAP increased by 1 px per step (+0.009 in → 0.239); visible gap ≈ 9 px at 115 dpi.
  - FH formula corrected: n*(H_ROW+H_GAP) → n*H_ROW + (n-1)*H_GAP — only n-1 inter-row
    gaps exist; old formula over-counted by one H_GAP, making the bottom margin larger.
  - H_COL increased 0.42→0.68 for yellow column-label breathing room.

- Fixed: matplotlib findfont WARNING spam on server-machine Linux for every cwlgroup image render.
  Root cause: generate_cwl_group_image() set font.family to ["Lato", "Segoe UI", "Arial",
    "DejaVu Sans"]. Lato, Segoe UI, and Arial are absent on the prod Linux server; matplotlib
    logged a WARNING for each missing family before falling back to DejaVu Sans — producing
    3+ warnings per render, interleaved with ORPHANED-CWL log lines.
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py, superseded by the Clash font change above): font-matching is
    now bypassed entirely; findfont is never called for cwlgroup renders.

- Fixed: CWL recovery "Batch done" log line repeating every cycle after queue was empty.
  Root cause: CACHE.last_cwl_recovery_summary was set by process_cwl_recovery_batch()
    but never cleared. The cycle-end block in QapBot.py re-logged it every cycle even
    after pending_cwl_recovery was fully consumed and the batch function stopped running.
  Fix (QapBot.py): clear last_cwl_recovery_summary to "" immediately after logging it,
    so it only appears once per batch run.

- Fixed: cwlgroup delete-and-repost — old messages piling up instead of being deleted.
  Root cause: post_discord_content_with_tracking (update_existing=False) delegated
    deletion to delete_leaderboard_messages_for_context(), which appends _{month}_{year}
    to the mode string (e.g. "cwlgroup_2026-05_current_0"). The stored cache key uses
    the bare mode "cwlgroup_2026-05", so the filter never matched and no message was
    ever deleted before posting the new one.
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py): In the delete-and-repost path, bypass
    delete_leaderboard_messages_for_context and call _delete_messages_by_filter directly
    with an exact-mode match — the same pattern the edit-in-place path already uses.

- Changed: cwlgroup /leaderboard command now uses delete-and-repost instead of
  edit-in-place (update_existing=False in QBdiscordcmds.py call).

- Fixed: Lingering old temp war files never being finalized (Phase 3B stuck files).
  Root cause: load_all_temp_war_stats() had a 24h lookback window that was added during
    a special backfill session (2026-05-09) to avoid parsing 21k old CWL recovery files.
    In normal operations no such volume exists, and old files that fell outside the window
    received filename-only (Phase-1) metadata with state="in_war" and no war_tag/is_cwl.
    Phase 3B Tier-2's war_ended bypass therefore never fired; the files lingered until
    the next bot restart reset _mtime_threshold.
  Fix (qapbot/cache_manager.py load_all_temp_war_stats):
    Removed the _lookback = timedelta(hours=24) lower-bound guard entirely. All temp
    files whose clan is state="in_war" (every file — we never synthesize war_ended from
    the filename) now always receive full JSON parsing in Phase 2, regardless of age.
    state, war_tag, and is_cwl are therefore always correct at startup.
  Removed (QapBot.py Phase 3B):
    The Tier 2.5 filename-timestamp fallback block introduced 2026-05-10 is deleted;
    it was only a workaround for the now-fixed root cause.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: /leaderboard cwlgroup "Best Attackers" showing avg opponent map positions > team_size (e.g. 15.1 on a 15-player team)
  Root cause: The CoC API returns mapPosition as the player's slot in the full 30-player CWL
    league group roster (1–30), not their sequential position within the 15-player war lineup.
    Both the live path (CACHE.get_league_war()) and the DB path (defender_map_position column)
    stored these raw roster slots, so attacker averages could exceed team_size.
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py):
    Live path: after collecting all members per war side, sort them by raw mapPosition ascending
      and assign sequential ranks 1–N (rank 1 = strongest). war_member_map_pos now maps every
      player tag to a normalized 1-to-team_size rank, guaranteeing avg positions ≤ team_size.
    DB path (_load_cwl_analysis_from_db_sync): added war_id to the SELECT; before processing
      attacks, build a normalized_def_pos lookup by grouping all defender raw positions per
      (war_id, attacker_clan_tag), sorting by raw value, and assigning sequential ranks 1–N.
      The attack-processing loop uses this normalized rank instead of the raw defender_map_position.
  All 33 CWL tests pass.

- Fixed: /admin subcommands intermittent Discord timeout ("Die Anwendung reagiert nicht")
  Root cause: START_UPDATE_CYCLE, OPTIMIZE_DB, REFRESH_DATA, and MEMORY_PROFILE all
  performed auth/guard checks via interaction.response.send_message() before deferring,
  or (for START_UPDATE_CYCLE) called force_cycle_event.set() which woke the update loop
  and caused the event loop to be contested before the response was sent — both patterns
  can exceed Discord's 3-second response deadline.
  Fix: moved interaction.response.defer() to the very top of each handler block; all
  subsequent sends (auth failures, queued/running messages, results) use
  interaction.followup.send(). The redundant inner defer in OPTIMIZE_DB was also removed.

- Fixed: CWL group standings — two calculation bugs corrected (verified against in-game screenshot):
  1. Total stars: added 10 bonus stars per won war (via CASE WHEN result='win' THEN 10 in
     get_cwl_group_war_stats SQL). Live in-war round is unaffected (win not yet determined).
  2. Total destruction: was SUM(avg_destruction) per war — changed to
     SUM(clan_destruction * team_size) in SQL to match the in-game sum of all individual
     attack destruction values. Live in-war data likewise multiplied by team_size from
     the war payload. Both changes in db_manager.get_cwl_group_war_stats() +
     update_cwl_group_stats() live-war block (QBhelperfunctions.py).

- Added: CWL group standings feature — `/leaderboard cwlgroup` sends a PNG image showing
  all 8 clans in the league group ranked by stars (then destruction), with promo/demotion
  zone highlighting and CoC dark-theme styling.
  Implementation:
    DB (qapbot/db_manager.py):
      - 5 new columns on cwl_league_groups: league_rank, cwl_ended, group_rank,
        total_stars, total_destruction — added via idempotent ALTER TABLE in _setup_tables().
      - get_cwl_group_info(clan_tag, cwl_season) — returns group-level info + all 8 clan rows.
      - get_cwl_group_war_stats(cwl_season, clan_tags) — returns stars_map + ended_map
        from war_summary for a season (public method; replaces direct _conn access).
      - update_cwl_group_stats_batch(cwl_season, group_id, clan_stats, set_cwl_ended) —
        bulk-UPDATE changed rows; skips unchanged rows; returns count updated.
      - update_cwl_league_rank(cwl_season, group_id, league_rank) — sets league_rank once
        per group (no-op if already set).
    Cache (qapbot/cache_manager.py):
      - _process_league_group_response(): after upsert, extracts warLeague.name from
        raw API data and calls db.update_cwl_league_rank() to persist it.
    Helpers (QBhelperfunctions.py):
      - Module-level TTL cache (_cwl_group_stats_cache, TTL=600s) avoids DB re-queries.
      - update_cwl_group_stats(clan_tag, cwl_season): aggregates war_summary stars for
        all 8 clans, folds in any live in_war round, ranks them, writes to DB.
        Short-circuits on cwl_ended=1 + stored stats non-null.
      - _build_standings_result(rows, clan_tags, league_rank): merges DB rows with
        clan_name_cache; returns list sorted by group_rank.
      - generate_cwl_group_image(standings, cwl_season): matplotlib Agg PNG.
        Dark CoC background (#3B2A1F); gold title; promo rows green ▲,
        demotion rows red ▼, stable rows alternating brown.
    Discord (QBdiscordcmds.py):
      - Added optional season parameter to /leaderboard command.
      - cwlgroup excluded from war-info update loops (like cwlinfo).
      - Dispatch block: resolves season, calls update_cwl_group_stats(), renders PNG,
        posts as discord.File attachment; sends ephemeral error if no data.
    Translations (en.json, de.json):
      - Added commands.errors.cwlgroup_no_data key.
    Formatting (qapbot/formatting.py):
      - Added cwlgroup to MODE_REGISTRY (sort_key=group_rank).
    Migration:
      - qapbot/scripts/migrate_cwl_league_groups_v2.py — backfills league_rank for a
        given season using majority war_league from the clans table. Schema columns are
        created by bot startup (_setup_tables), not this script. Fails clearly if the
        column doesn't exist yet.

- Added: cwlinfo/cwlinfo_comp now also refresh CWL group standings as a fire-and-forget
  side-effect (generate_cwlinfo_embeds, QBhelperfunctions.py).
  Because the league_group is already fetched from the API for cwlinfo rendering,
  calling update_cwl_group_stats() costs only a DB read + in-memory computation —
  no extra API calls. cwlinfo_comp is a thin wrapper so it benefits automatically.

- Revised: migration script migrate_cwl_league_groups_v2.py completely rewritten.
  Previous version: applied ALTER TABLE to add the 5 new columns (wrong — that is the
    bot's job at startup via _setup_tables idempotent ALTER TABLE calls).
  New version: only backfills the league_rank column using majority-vote across all
    8 clans in each group from the clans table's war_league field.
  Rationale for majority vote: after CWL ends, 2 clans promoted and 2 demoted already
    show new leagues in clans table; 4 stable clans still show the original league.
    4-of-8 majority is always the correct original league rank for the whole group.
  Script verifies column exists first (instructs user to start bot if not); logs per-group
    tally for verification; only writes rows where league_rank IS NULL.
  Tie-handling: majority vote now detects ties (equal top counts) and falls back to
    median-by-tier heuristic — sorts the distinct leagues present by CWL tier order
    and picks the middle one. Works correctly after multiple seasons of accumulated
    promotions/demotions where the original league is always the median value.
    Added full _CWL_LEAGUE_RANK tier table to the script. Validated against all
    observed tie cases from 2026-04 backfill (10 groups, all resolved correctly).
  Tested on seasons 2026-05 and 2026-04 successfully before PROD deployment.

- Fixed: cwlgroup mode left orphaned messages in Discord channels — no message-ID tracking.
  Root cause: dispatch block called interaction.channel.send() directly, bypassing the
    leaderboard_messages tracking system used by all other modes.
  Fix (QBdiscordcmds.py): dispatch now calls post_discord_content_with_tracking() with
    update_existing=True and mode=f"cwlgroup_{season}". On subsequent invocations, the
    existing message is edited in-place when content changed, skipped when unchanged, and
    a new message posted only if the old one was deleted on Discord (NotFound fallback).

- Refactored: Consolidated two Discord posting functions into one unified function.
  Removed post_cwlinfo_embeds_to_discord() and post_cwl_group_image_to_discord(); both
  replaced by post_discord_content_with_tracking() (QBhelperfunctions.py).
  The unified function accepts embeds= or file_bytes= (mutually exclusive) and an
  update_existing flag that switches between delete-and-repost (False, default, used by
  cwlinfo/cwlinfo_comp) and edit-in-place (True, used by cwlgroup).
  All 6 call sites updated across QapBot.py and QBdiscordcmds.py.
  All 1322 tests pass.

- Fixed: CWL group standings race condition — non-tracked group members contributed stale
  stars because their wars were only fetched on the 22h passive cycle, not every cycle.
  Root cause: update_cwl_group_stats() reads star data from war_summary, which is only
  written when a clan's war is fetched.  Group members without active subscriptions were
  fetched at most once per 22h, making their per-round star tallies up to 22h stale.
  Fix: added get_active_cwl_group_member_tags() to db_manager.py — a single JOIN query
  on cwl_league_groups that returns all 8 members of every non-ended group (cwl_ended=0)
  that contains at least one actively subscribed clan.  In QapBot.py, right after the
  thundering-herd guard, the result is deduped against the already-scheduled set and the
  remaining tags injected as (tag, False) entries so they are fetched every cycle during
  CWL.  Adds at most 7 × N_groups extra get_current_war calls per cycle; deduplication
  ensures family clans sharing a group are never double-fetched.  No schema changes.
  All 1322 tests pass.

- Fixed: Phase 3B latent bug — war_ended temp files for passive clans stuck indefinitely
  after bot restart when load_all_temp_war_stats() Phase-1-only metadata omits state.
  Root cause: load_all_temp_war_stats() (fix from 2026-05-09) only full-parses files <24h
  old at startup; older files get filename-only (Phase-1) metadata with no state field.
  Phase 3B's Tier-2 war_ended bypass requires state from temp_war_metadata.  Without it,
  those old files fall through to Tier-3 (mtime check), which permanently skips them
  because their mtime is old.  After the first cycle post-restart (mtime_threshold=0)
  the files were finalized once; but re-written recovery-pipeline files (same clan, same
  round) triggered the same trap on subsequent restarts.
  Fix (QapBot.py): added Tier 2.5 between Tier-2 and Tier-3.  Extracts the war-start
  datetime from the filename (YYYYMMDDHHMM segment) without reading the JSON.  If the
  start timestamp is >25h in the past, the war is definitely over — add clan to the
  finalization list and bypass the mtime check.  Only one file per clan is inspected
  (single stat() call on the basename string, no open()).  Zero performance impact during
  normal operation (≤1 µs per clan): the 25h gate skips fresh in_war files instantly.
  All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-09
- Fixed: backfill_last_cwl_for_clan() and backfill_cwl_from_war_tags() passed wrong temp path
  to _process_war_history(), causing it to silently fail (file not found).
  Root cause: both functions predicted a 4-part filename
    ({CLAN}_{OPP}_{TS}_{WAR_TAG}_war_data.json) but save_war_object() writes a 3-part name
    ({CLAN}_{OPP}_{TS}_war_data.json). The 4-part path never exists on disk.
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py): after save_war_object() returns True, read the actual written
    path from CACHE.temp_war_metadata[clan_tag]["filepath"] — set by save_war_object()
    as the single source of truth. Eliminates all filename-prediction logic from both
    callers; future renames to save_war_object's convention can't cause this bug again.

2026-05-09
- Fixed: load_all_temp_war_stats() — Phase 2 lookahead condition incorrectly included ALL
  past-ended files, causing 24,807 unnecessary full JSON reads on startup instead of ~17.
  Root cause: condition `(end_dt - now_utc) <= lookahead` where lookahead=6h is always
  True for negative timedeltas (wars ended days ago), so every old CWL recovery file
  (battles ended ~May 1-7, now=May 9) triggered a full JSON parse.
  Fix (qapbot/cache_manager.py): added lower-bound check:
    `(now_utc - timedelta(hours=24)) <= end_dt <= (now_utc + lookahead)`
  The 24h lookback preserves full parsing for wars that ended within the last day
  (covers late notification processing); all older files are filename-only (Phase 1 only).
  Expected startup time improvement: from ~120 s back to < 1 s for the TEMP-WAR-LOAD step.

2026-05-09
- Changed: Cache load timeout increased from 60 s to 120 s.
  Root cause: With 49,255 temp war files present (CWL recovery in progress), the
    load_all_temp_war_stats step exceeded the 60 s limit on restart → bot terminated
    immediately with "Cache loading timed out" and had to be manually restarted again.
  Fix (QapBot.py): asyncio.wait_for(CACHE.load_all(), timeout=60.0) → timeout=120.0.
    Critical log message updated to match. The startup_login timeout (separate 60 s
