2026-05-09
- Changed: analyze_cwl_rounds.py — internal season format switched from YYYYMM to YYYY-MM.
  Root cause: YYYYMM was stored internally and converted to YYYY-MM via a `season_fmt`
    local variable before every DB query. All DB tables use YYYY-MM; stripping the dash
    internally was needless and inconsistent.
  Fix: `_main_inner` now normalises both accepted input forms (2026-05 / 202605) to
    YYYY-MM immediately. All eight `season_fmt = f"{season[:4]}-{season[4:]}"` conversions
    removed from find_missing_war_tags(), query_archive(), query_temp() and the
    completeness-summary block. The only remaining strip-dash call is
    `season_from_filename(basename, season.replace('-', ''))` in query_temp() where the
    filename timestamp prefix must be dashless. `season_display` variable removed (was
    identical to `season` after the change).

- Changed: process_cwl_recovery_batch() — pipelined fetch+write (eliminates pauses).
  Root cause: the original design was two-phase: (1) asyncio.gather all 5000 fetches
    (throttled by semaphore=20 → ~25 s at 100 ms/fetch), then (2) dispatch writes in
    chunks of 250 (_CWL_RECOVERY_WRITE_JOB_CHUNK). This caused ~25 s of dead time before
    any file was written, followed by a burst-pause-burst write pattern at chunk
    boundaries (observable in the log as files appearing in bursts with visible gaps).
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py): replaced two-phase with pipelined _fetch_and_write()
    coroutines. Each coroutine fetches its war_tag then immediately writes temp files for
    its participants, so writes begin ~100 ms into the batch while other fetches are still
    in flight. Clan + opponent writes are sequential within the same coroutine (no
    locking needed — replaces _write_locks_by_wt dict). All gather()s run in a single
    call. _CWL_RECOVERY_WRITE_JOB_CHUNK constant removed (no longer needed).
    All error tracking (non-200, exception, false-return, no-write re-queue) preserved.

- Changed: _CWL_RECOVERY_BATCH_SIZE increased from 2000 to 5000.
  Allows up to 5000 war_tags fetched+written per cycle instead of 2000.

2026-05-09
- Changed: analyze_cwl_rounds.py — integrated harvest_cwl_war_tags.py functionality;
    harvest_cwl_war_tags.py is now obsolete.
  New CLI flags:
    --fetch-missing  Identifies war_tags in cwl_league_rounds (for tracked clans) that are
                     absent from war_summary, using a DB join + Python set difference (no
                     API call needed — the DB already has all required data):
                       cwl_league_rounds → cwl_league_groups → clans (track_war_updates=1)
                     then subtracts war_tags already in war_summary via Python set diff.
                     Appends missing war_tags to data/missing_cwl_war_tags.txt for the
                     bot's existing recovery pipeline (load_cwl_recovery_file() at startup,
                     process_cwl_recovery_batch() each cycle up to 2,000 tags/cycle).
    --dry-run        Report what would be written without touching the recovery file.
  New analysis output: always prints a completeness summary (incomplete clan count and
    total missing wars for clans with known round_numbers).
  Key improvements over harvest_cwl_war_tags.py:
    - No API calls required — cwl_league_rounds already has all war_tags the bot has seen.
    - Restricted to tracked clans (track_war_updates=1) via join through cwl_league_groups.
    - Performance: two indexed queries + O(n) Python set difference instead of correlated
      SQL subquery against un-indexed war_summary.war_tag.
  harvest_cwl_war_tags.py can be deleted once verified in prod.

2026-05-09
- Fixed: Cycle performance regression — 997s cycle caused by per-group CWL round backfill
  Root cause: _process_league_group_response() in cache_manager.py called

    CWL league groups fetched per cycle (during CWL season), each backfill acquired

    17:18 in prod log). A separate post-P0-flush backfill already runs once per cycle in
    QapBot.py, making the per-group call redundant.
  Fix (cache_manager.py): removed the backfill_cwl_round_numbers() call and its
    surrounding if _revealed: block from _process_league_group_response(). Added comment
    explaining why it must stay out. The post-flush backfill in QapBot.py is sufficient
    and runs in ~0.1-2s since the partial index keeps it fast.

- Fixed: [PHASE-2] UNIQUE constraint failed: war_attacks.war_id, player_tag, attack_order
  Root cause: flush_pending_war_writes() UPDATES section used plain INSERT INTO war_attacks
    (not OR IGNORE). When a clan has both a stale 3-part and a current 4-part temp file for
    the same war — and both have been archived before with different content (ARCHIVE-DIFFERS)
    — both call update_war_data_sync() in the same war_write_batch(). Two (clan_tag, war_id)
    entries with identical attack rows enter the UPDATES batch. flush_pending_war_writes()
    collects all DELETEs first, then all INSERTs. After both DELETEs run, the first INSERT
    set succeeds, but the second identical set hits UNIQUE constraint on
    (war_id, player_tag, attack_order). Plain INSERT doesn't tolerate within-batch duplicates.
  Fix (qapbot/db_manager.py): changed INSERT INTO war_attacks to INSERT OR IGNORE INTO
    war_attacks in the UPDATES section of flush_pending_war_writes(). The DELETE before
    INSERT already clears existing rows; OR IGNORE only needs to guard against the rare
    case of duplicate (clan_tag, war_id) entries within the same batch (identical data,
    so ignoring the second set is correct).
  Expected behaviour: Phase-2 batch finalization no longer fails when a clan has both
    3-part and 4-part temp files for the same CWL war. All 1044 orphaned wars finalized.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: [MANAGE-WAR-FILES] FileNotFoundError on archive read (shard mismatch)
  Root cause: _process_war_history() checks _archive_exists via archive_set (built by
    scanning ALL shards at cycle start), then computes archive_file path using
    get_war_shard_dir() with the CURRENT shard bounds. If the file was archived under
    older shard bounds (_SHARD_UPPER_BOUNDS changed between archival and now), archive_set
    finds it in its actual shard but get_war_shard_dir() returns a different shard →
    archive_file doesn't exist → open() raises FileNotFoundError. The except clause only
    caught WarProcessingError, not OSError/FileNotFoundError, so the error propagated as
    [MANAGE-WAR-FILES] Error processing ...
  Fix (QBhelperfunctions.py, _process_war_history):
    1. After computing _archive_exists from archive_set, add os.path.exists(archive_file)
       check. If file is missing at computed path, search all 10 shards as fallback; if
       found in a different shard, update archive_file to the real path; if not found
       anywhere, set _archive_exists = False (treat as new war, safe via INSERT OR
       IGNORE/REPLACE semantics).
    2. Broadened except clause from WarProcessingError to (WarProcessingError, OSError)
       as defense-in-depth so any future archive I/O errors are handled gracefully.
  Expected behaviour: No more MANAGE-WAR-FILES errors for files in wrong shards. Such
    files are still processed correctly (compared and updated/archived to the correct
    shard path on the next cycle).
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: DB binding error "uses 23, and there are 24 supplied" in flush_pending_war_writes
  Root cause: The 2026-05-08 evening Discord outage caused three separate failure modes:
    1. Bot crashed on restart while Discord was down (503 during login → DiscordServerError
       propagated out of bot.run() uncaught → process exited → ~2 hour gap until manual
       restart because Docker backoff policy).
    2. "Task exception was never retrieved" Python warning in _clear_dev_guild_commands_before_prod():
       when the temp bot_task already finished with DiscordServerError (503) before the
       30-second timeout fired, the finally: block's "if not bot_task.done()" branch was
       skipped, leaving the exception unretrieved.
    3. DM fetch failures (429 error code 40062 "service resource rate limited" and 503)
       were logged at ERROR level with full tracebacks — noisy and misleading since these
       are Discord-side transient errors, not bot bugs.
  Fix (4 files changed):
    - qapbot/discord_health.py: Added global outage detection (_discord_consecutive_5xx,
      _discord_outage_detected, _discord_outage_since). discord_retry() now calls
      _mark_discord_5xx() on 5xx responses (threshold=3 → outage declared) and
      _mark_discord_success() on any successful call (clears outage state).
      Added is_discord_available() public function.
    - QapBot.py: Added _wait_for_discord_connection() pre-flight check that uses aiohttp
      to poll Discord's /api/v10/gateway endpoint with exponential backoff (10s→10min)
      until Discord responds non-5xx before calling bot.run(). This prevents the bot from
      crashing on restart during an outage and eliminates the multi-hour recovery gap.
      Fixed _clear_dev_guild_commands_before_prod() to retrieve the bot_task exception
      when it is already done (prevents "Task exception was never retrieved" warning).
      Added is_discord_available() check before role sync, leaderboard posts, and
      registration reposts — all Discord posting operations are skipped as a block
      when an outage is detected, rather than burning retries per operation.
    - qapbot/war_notifications.py: Added is_discord_available() check at start of
      check_wars_for_notifications() so DM war reminders are skipped during outage.
    - qapbot/cache_manager.py: get_user_for_dm() now distinguishes Discord server errors
      (HTTP 5xx and 429 code 40062 "service resource rate limited") from real errors;
      the former are logged at WARNING level without traceback, not ERROR.
  Expected behaviour after fix: When Discord is unavailable for any reason, the bot
    continues regular CoC API update cycles normally, skips all Discord posts/DMs
    gracefully with a single WARNING per cycle, and automatically resumes full operation
    (including missed posts) once Discord becomes available again.

2026-05-05
- Added: Per-cycle RSS log line at end of each update cycle (QapBot.py)
  Each time the bot finishes an update cycle and enters sleep mode, a single INFO log line
  "[CYCLE-END] [MEMORY CONSUMPTION] RSS=XXXX.X MB" is emitted. Reads /proc/self/status (one kernel file read —
  negligible overhead on server-machine Celeron). Provides a continuous RSS time-series in the plain log
  without requiring /memprofile snapshots, making memory trends visible across CWL rounds.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: CWL-season RSS ratchet causing OOM risk (QapBot.py)
  Root cause: Python's pymalloc never returns memory arenas to the OS until an arena is completely
  empty. During CWL, every 5-min cycle allocates and frees millions of small dicts (temp_war_stats
  processing: ~24k clans × 30 players × 11 fields). Arenas fill with a mix of live and freed
  objects, so gc.collect() frees the Python objects but glibc cannot return the partially-used
  arenas — RSS ratchets upward by ~25 MB/min. At CWL round transitions all ~8k CWL clans flip
  simultaneously (warEnded → inWar), causing a burst that permanently raises the RSS floor.
  Over 3 CWL rounds this session, RSS climbed from ~2.2 GB to ~5.3 GB in 3h.
  Fix: added malloc_trim(0) call in _post_cycle_cleanup() immediately after gc.collect().
  malloc_trim(0) instructs glibc to return all free top-of-heap memory to the OS via
  MADV_DONTNEED. Runs in asyncio.to_thread (same thread as gc.collect()). No-op on non-Linux
  platforms (ctypes CDLL load fails silently). RSS should stabilize near the live-object
  footprint (~1-1.5 GB) and not stair-step upward across CWL rounds.
  All 1317 tests pass.

2026-05-04
- Fixed: /status temp total still wrong after pre-scan fix (QapBot.py)
  Root cause: temp_total_file_count was set once at the cycle pre-scan (cycle start). During the
  cycle, Phase-3 and Phase-3B archived 859 files via deferred os.replace() moves. By cycle end
  the disk count was 71,405 but /status still showed the pre-scan value of 72,264. A formula-based
  approach (pre_scan + new - archived) was also unreliable because "new" writes sometimes replace
  paths just archived in the same cycle. Fix: added a post-cycle re-scan of all temp shards after
  all deferred file moves complete, updating CACHE.temp_total_file_count to the true disk count.
  Runs via asyncio.to_thread, same as pre-scan; negligible overhead at end of ~124s cycle.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: temp_war_objects unbounded growth risking OOM (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  Root cause: temp_war_objects cached the full JSON payload (~40 KB) per clan with no eviction
  limit. Over a full 22h polling cycle covering all 70k in_war clans this accumulates
  70k × 40 KB ≈ 2.8 GB. With 10 GB server-machine RAM this is safe in isolation, but left no headroom
  for OS, other processes, and memory spikes. Fix: added a FIFO cap at 100,000 entries
  (≈ 4 GB ceiling). When the cap is exceeded, the oldest entry is evicted; get_current_war_data()
  falls back to disk transparently for any cache miss.
  Also added temp_war_objects count and cap to /memprofile cache summary for future visibility.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: /status temp total under-counted vs disk by ~1394 files (cache_manager.py, QapBot.py)
  Root cause: get_war_file_stats() computed "total" as sum of temp_war_metadata entries.
  temp_war_metadata has ONE entry per clan (deduplicated to the latest file). Clans that
  received a new CWL war file during the cycle have their metadata entry overwritten with
  the new filepath — the old file still exists on disk, untracked. Result: /status total
  was always less than countprodfiles by exactly the number of "extra" (non-latest) temp files.
  Fix: Added self.temp_total_file_count attribute to CacheManager. Set from len(_all_shard_files)
  in load_all_temp_war_stats() and refreshed from len(_all_temp_files) at each cycle pre-scan
  in QapBot.py. get_war_file_stats() uses this raw count as "total" instead of the metadata sum.
  Per-state breakdown (prep/in_war/war_ended) remains from metadata (accurate for tracked states).
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: war_ended metadata eviction never fired for new war finalizations (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  Root cause: _process_war_history() has two sub-paths after deciding the war_ended file is ready:
    1. "Already in history" → calls _archive_and_log_war_file() → evicts metadata ✓
    2. "New war" → calls _append_current_war_to_history() (which archives internally) → return True
       _archive_and_log_war_file() is NEVER called → metadata state="war_ended" stays forever ✗
  All 393 finalizations in the observed cycle were "new war" (history: new=393). Every one left a
  war_ended ghost in temp_war_metadata. /status over-count = exactly 392 war_ended + 388 total diff.

- Fixed: temp_war_objects skip for war_ended did not work; eviction condition too narrow (cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  Root cause 1: save_war_object() skipped caching war_ended wars by checking
  war_state.lower() != 'warended', but coc.WarState.war_ended.name == "war_ended" (snake_case),
  so "war_ended" != "warended" was always True — war_ended wars WERE cached, keeping the 400 MB
  memory leak active despite the earlier fix. Fixed by normalising: .replace('_', '') before compare.
  Root cause 2: _archive_and_log_war_file() eviction required an exact filepath match in metadata.
  This worked when Phase-1 startup set the filepath, but missed cases where save_war_object() had
  already confirmed state=war_ended via API poll (updating metadata.state but not necessarily
  triggering eviction). Fixed by evicting if state=="war_ended" OR filepath matches — ensuring
  both Phase-3B (Phase-1-startup-derived in_war state, filepath match) and Phase-1-polled
  (state=war_ended directly from API) cases are covered without evicting valid in_war entries
  for clans that started a new war. Files changed: cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py.
  Effect: war_ended count in /status should drop to 0 after one cycle; /status total should
  match countprodfiles disk count to within a few files. All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: CWL-season memory explosion crashing PROD after ~25h (qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  Root cause: Three simultaneous memory sources during CWL season drove RAM to 7.6 GB / swap full:
  1. _league_war_cache: No size cap. process_orphaned_cwl_wars() fetched 7k+ CWL wars/cycle,
     each stored as a coc.ClanWar Python object (~200 KB each) with 2h TTL. Peak: 7k × 200 KB ≈ 1.4 GB.
     Fix: Added _LEAGUE_WAR_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=1000 and _LEAGUE_GROUP_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=200 hard caps.
     evict_stale_cwl_caches() (called every cycle) now evicts oldest entries when over limit.
  2. temp_war_objects: Cached full war JSON for war_ended wars (10k finalized/cycle × ~40 KB = 400 MB).
     war_ended wars are immediately queued for finalization; the JSON is read from disk anyway.
     Fix: save_war_object() skips caching when war_state == 'warEnded'; evicts stale in_war entry.
  3. [CWL-ORPHAN-SKIP] logged at INFO: 7,463 lines/cycle × 6 cycles/hr = 1M lines/day (>130 MB/day).
     Fix: Downgraded to logging.debug.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: /status over-reports war_ended temp files (qapbot/cache_manager.py Phase 1 startup)
  Root cause: load_all_temp_war_stats() Phase 1 derives state from filename timestamp only.
  It synthesized state="war_ended" when estimated end_time (start_time + 24h) < now_utc.
  This is wrong for CWL wars: they restart daily with a new opponent/timestamp, so a file
  from yesterday (e.g. ts=202605031656 → estimated end 202605041656) gets flagged war_ended
  in temp_war_metadata even though its actual JSON says in_war (the bot hasn't re-polled yet).
  /status reads temp_war_metadata exclusively (zero disk I/O) and therefore over-counted by
  ~4000-5000 entries during CWL season. Confirmed: scanning all 50k actual JSON files showed
  zero war_ended state; filename-derived count showed 5253.
  Fix: removed the if end_dt < now_utc: state = 'war_ended' branch. Phase 1 now always
  synthesizes in_war (safer default). save_war_object() corrects to the real state after
  the first poll. Phase-3B's end_time-in-the-past bypass for in_war entries still works
  correctly (it uses the end_time value, not the state label).
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: aiohttp response buffers accumulating ~361 MB per 2 cycles unclaimed (QapBot.py)
  Root cause: aiohttp ClientResponse has __del__, so body buffer chunks (74B × 5M+ per cycle)
  survive gen-0 GC during the cycle and get promoted to generation 1. End-of-cycle cleanup
  called gc.collect(0) which only sweeps generation 0 — generation 1 was skipped entirely.
  Confirmed by memprofile_20260504_191941.txt: #1 growth site was aiohttp/client_reqrep.py:773,
  +369 MB in 13 minutes (2 cycles), 5,105,738 allocations at 74B average.
  At observed growth rate (~28 MB/cycle × 6 cycles/hr): +4 GB/day from this alone.
  Fix: changed gc.collect(0) to gc.collect() in _post_cycle_cleanup() (all generations).
  Runs in asyncio.to_thread() so does not block discord.py heartbeat.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: war_ended temp files lingering up to 22h before archival (QapBot.py Phase-3B)
  Root cause: Phase-3B's Tier-2 metadata check handled in_war and preparation states but silently
  skipped war_ended — falling through to Tier-3 mtime check, which rejected files written more
  than one cycle (~5 min) ago. A war_ended file is written at the clan's last Phase-1 poll (up to
  22h before the file actually reaches war_ended state), so Tier-3 skipped it every cycle until
  the next 22h poll fired. During CWL season with ~10k wars ending/day, this left 10k+ files
  stranded for up to 22h (17,112 observed in bot status).
  Fix: Added war_ended branch in Phase-3B Tier-2 that bypasses Tier-3 and processes immediately.
  After first deployment: war_ended count should drop to near-zero within one cycle.
  All 1317 tests pass.

- Reduced: Log noise — downgraded 9 high-volume INFO lines to DEBUG (QBhelperfunctions.py, QBcsvhandling.py)
  Combined these generated ~6,700 redundant lines/cycle (≈ 95% of daily log volume during CWL):
  · [COC-API-CALL] "COMPLETED successfully" — 3,619/cycle (every inactive poll, covered by [INACTIVE] line)
  · "No active war for clan" (2 variants) — 2,505/cycle (notInWar result, no action needed)
  · [FINALIZE-OLD-WAR] per-file trace — 188/cycle (covered by [MANAGE-WAR-FILES] summary)
  · [DUPLICATE-CHECK-START] per-file trace — 188/cycle (internal step of finalization)
  · append_current_war_to_history called — 166/cycle (internal step, covered by [DB-WRITE])
  · Using war data from JSON — 166/cycle (internal step, covered by [FINALIZE])
  · Moved temp war file to archive — 166/cycle (internal step, covered by [MANAGE-WAR-FILES])
  · [SKILL-LOAD] per-war probability calc — 87/cycle (useful but too verbose for INFO)
  Meaningful single-line summaries ([MANAGE-WAR-FILES], [FINALIZE], [DB-WRITE], [ORPHANED-CWL],
  [CYCLE-SUMMARY]) remain at INFO. Expected log volume: ~75 MB/day baseline even during CWL.
  All 1317 tests pass.

  · `from datetime import datetime` inside the else/investigate block made Python treat `datetime`
    as a local variable for the entire function, causing UnboundLocalError on every clan fetch.
  · Fix: replaced with `import datetime as _dt_mod` (distinct name, no conflict).
  · No data corruption — every affected clan raised WarDataFetchError caught by Phase-1 handler.

- Fixed: skip_stale guard replaced with corrupt-state guard in save_war_object() (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · Old logic: skipped any war_ended war with end_time >24h ago — this wrongly blocked valid unfinalized
    old wars (e.g. clans that were inactive and the API kept returning an ended war) from ever being
    written and properly archived. They would hit skip_stale every cycle indefinitely.
  · New logic: skip only when state != war_ended AND end_time >24h ago. This is the genuinely corrupt
    case: the API is stuck returning in_war/preparation for a war that objectively ended days ago.
    Logs a WARNING [STALE-INVALID-SKIP] with clan tags and hours-since-end.
  · All valid war_ended wars (regardless of age) now flow through to skip_finalized or get written.
    skip_finalized is the sole guard against reprocessing already-completed wars.
  · Counter save_skip_stale retained, now only fires for the corrupt-state path.
  · Tests updated: test_stale_war_skipped → test_old_war_ended_not_finalized_is_written (asserts True);
    added test_stale_invalid_state_skipped (in_war + 48h old → False).
  · All 1317 tests pass.

- Fixed: Refactored save_war_object() skip logic; closed temp-write accounting gap (qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py, QBdiscordcmds.py)
  · Clarified two independent rules that were conflated in a single "friendly AND already archived" guard:
    1. Friendly wars: never written under any condition — early bail added right after invalid-clan check.
       Counter: save_skip_friendly (replaces save_skip_friendly_archived).
    2. Already-finalized: skip only when ALL THREE are true: archive exists AND in history (DB) AND
       db state == war_ended. Any unmet condition → write so next cycle can update/correct.
       Warning logged if all 3 are met but API returns a non-war_ended state (unexpected edge case).
       Counter: save_skip_finalized (unchanged name, simplified logic, gate no longer limited to war_ended state).
  · Removed dead counter save_override_db_not_ended (no longer needed — the new 3-condition gate
    handles the premature-archive correction case implicitly by not skipping).
  · Removed stale fallback that opened the archived JSON file to check its type field — no longer needed
    since friendly wars are blocked before any file I/O.
  · Added save_skip_no_clan counter + WARNING log in fetch_clan_war_data() else branch (QBhelperfunctions.py).
    The else fires when coc_war_obj is non-null but my_clan or opp_clan is None — previously
    a silent logging.debug with no counter. Now visible in /status and produces a WARNING with
    clan tag so the root cause can be investigated.
  · Temp-write line in /status now shows: skip_finalized / skip_stale / skip_friendly / skip_no_clan
    (skip_no_clan omitted when 0). Format: "written=74 (new=59, upd=15)  skip_finalized=0  skip_stale=2  skip_friendly=0  skip_no_clan=4"
  · Expected accounting after next PROD cycle: written + skip_stale + skip_friendly + skip_no_clan = ok.
  · Added test: test_friendly_war_skipped in test_war_save_notify_phase5.py.
  · All 1316 tests pass.

- Fixed: 3,908 ended in_war temp files permanently stuck — never finalized (QapBot.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause 1 (Phase-3B Tier-2 + Tier-3 deadlock): load_all_temp_war_stats derives
    end_time = filename_timestamp + 24h. At restart (20:14 May 1), April 30 22:xx files had
    end_time = May 1 22:xx (future) → Tier-2 set _p3b_skip_until = end_time - 4.5 min.
    Once skip_until expired and end_time was past, Tier-2 fell through to Tier-3 mtime check.
    File mtime = April 30 → older than last-5-min threshold → permanently skipped. Never
    reached manage_war_files.
  · Fix (QapBot.py Phase-3B Tier-2): split the ≤300s case:
    • _secs_to_end > 300: set skip_until = end_time - 270, skip (unchanged).
    • 0 < _secs_to_end ≤ 300: fall through to Tier-3 mtime — Phase-1's 22h re-poll should
      fire imminently; if it already wrote a fresh war_ended file, mtime is recent and Tier-3
      passes; otherwise one more cycle is skipped. No premature finalization.
    • _secs_to_end ≤ 0 (past): bypass Tier-3, force into _passive_shard_tags.
      manage_war_files' own end_time guard (end_time > now → skip) provides a second check.
  · Root cause 2 (guard accidentally removed): a prior attempt to fix the stuck files
    incorrectly removed the end_time guard from manage_war_files. Without the guard, in_war
    files could be finalized while the war was still running (up to 5 min early via the
    Tier-2 bypass window). Guard restored with clarified comment.
  · Invariants guaranteed by the code:
    1. in_war + current_opponent set → new war is active → finalize immediately (orphan).
    2. in_war + no current opponent → only finalize when end_time is in the past.
    3. CWL in_war → never finalized; the earlier is_cwl guard skips it for process_orphaned_cwl_wars().

2026-05-01
- Fixed: Incomplete in_war war snapshots storing speculative missed-attack data (QBcsvhandling.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: when an orphaned regular war was finalized from an in_war snapshot (war never
    reached war_ended before the bot moved on), build_per_attack_rows was called with
    for_finalization=True, creating sentinel rows (attack_order=0, missed_attacks=max_attacks)
    for players who had 0 attacks at snapshot time — but may have attacked later — and setting
    missed_attacks>0 on partial-attack rows.
  · Fix: _append_current_war_to_history and the late-update path in _process_war_history now
    detect state='in_war' and pass for_finalization=False, include_missed_sentinels=False to
    build_per_attack_rows. Only real attacks are persisted; no speculative misses stored.
  · New parameter: build_per_attack_rows(include_missed_sentinels=True) — backward-compatible;
    defaults preserve existing behavior for war_ended / CWL finalization paths.
  · Migration applied 2026-05-01: deleted 488,551 sentinel rows, zeroed missed_attacks on
    122,740 real rows across 85,578 in_war wars. Script removed after successful run.
  · Tests: TestBuildPerAttackRowsIncompleteWar (test_csv_handling_extended.py) + caller-level
    integration test TestAppendCurrentWarToHistoryIncompleteWar.
- Fixed: /whois player_tag — CWL seasons missing for pre-Dec 2025 data (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: old historical import created war_attacks rows for CWL (max_attacks=1) but NO
    matching war_summary rows. The LEFT JOIN already returned them, but the fallback
    COALESCE(ws.is_cwl, 0) defaulted to is_cwl=0 (regular CW) for all orphaned rows, hiding them.
  · Symptoms: Killer (#G0CQ2PYG) showed only 5 CWL seasons (Dec 2025 – Apr 2026) despite data
    going back to August 2025. 60 orphaned war_attacks rows (4 CWL seasons × 7 rounds) were
    present but classified as regular CW.
  · Fix (get_player_war_history_sync, both actual and sentinel queries):
    COALESCE(ws.is_cwl, 0) → COALESCE(ws.is_cwl, CASE WHEN MAX(wa.max_attacks)=1 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    COALESCE(ws.cwl_season,'') → COALESCE(NULLIF(ws.cwl_season,''), CASE WHEN max_attacks=1 THEN substr(date,1,7) ELSE '' END)
    When war_summary is absent, max_attacks=1 reliably identifies CWL (1 attack per member).
    Regular CW orphaned rows (max_attacks=2) correctly remain is_cwl=0.
  · Result: Killer now shows 9 CWL seasons (Aug 2025 – Apr 2026), 62 CWL wars total.
  · Note: Nov 2025 shows 6 rounds instead of 7 — one round genuinely missing from the import.
- Fixed: /whois player_tag — avg stars formula was wrong (showed 3.01 instead of correct ~2.61)
  · Root cause: get_player_attack_summary_sync() used COUNT(*) (rows) as denominator for avg_stars
    and total_attacks. Old aggregated CW rows store BOTH attacks in 1 row (stars up to 6, max_attacks=2),
    so counting rows instead of actual attacks (MAX(max_attacks)-MAX(missed_attacks) per war) inflated the
    denominator mismatch → avg was ~3.01 for a player whose true avg is 2.61.
  · Fix (get_player_attack_summary_sync): rewrote to use a per-war CTE subquery for total_attacks,
    avg_stars and avg_destruction (naturally correct for both modern per-row and old aggregated data).
    Star distribution (zero/one/two/three_star) now computed with a second query filtered to stars<=3
    (individual attack rows only), to avoid double-counting aggregated rows. New return key: dist_attacks.
  · Fix (get_player_war_history_sync): reverted SUM(MIN(wa.stars,3)) → SUM(wa.stars).
    With max_attacks=2 on dirty rows, the per-war arithmetic SUM(stars)/2 is naturally ≤3.0 — no capping needed.
  · Fix (_player_report_logic): simplified recent_stars/cwl_stars sums to plain r["stars"] (removed
    inner min(r["stars"], r["attacks"]*3) wrappers); added dist_atk from attack_summary["dist_attacks"]
    and updated _pct() to use dist_atk as denominator (% = distribution row count, not total attack count).
    Kept min(avg, 3.0) on per-war averages as a harmless defensive safety net.

- Fixed: Nightly archive file move silently failed — moved=0, skipped=28,025 (qapbot/move_old_archive_files.py, qapbot/config.py, QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: os.replace() = os.rename() raises EXDEV (errno 18) when crossing filesystem mount
    points. After archive/ was relocated to the SSD (${PROD_DATA_DIR}) and archive_old/ remained on
    the HDD (${PROD_BOT_ROOT}), every rename failed silently inside the except OSError block, incrementing
    skipped without logging the reason. 28,025 files accumulated unprocessed.
  · Fix: moved archive_old/ to the SSD (same mount point as archive/) so os.replace() is a pure
    metadata rename again — no data copy, no SSD wear, O(1) per file.
  · Added: archive_old_dir field to BotConfig and load_config() in qapbot/config.py, derived from
    same PROD_DATA_DIR base as archive_dir .
  · Fixed: QapBot.py _archive_move_nightly() dest_dir was hardcoded "archive_old" (relative path);
    now uses CONFIG.archive_old_dir.
  · Renamed: old HDD archive_old/ → archive_compressed/ to distinguish staging (SSD) from
    compressed long-term storage (HDD).
  · Updated: ArchiveOld.bat — cd target changed to SSD; tar output
    now written to ${PROD_BOT_ROOT}/archive_compressed/ with new naming convention
    archive_compressed_<stamp>.tar.zst.
  · Updated: BackupProd.bat — mode 3 now backs up both archive_old (SSD) and archive_compressed
    (HDD); robocopy XD list and server-machine cleanup extended accordingly; all comments corrected.
  · Updated: move_old_archive_files.py docstring — example --dest now points to SSD path; explains
    wear rationale; CLI --days default updated to 7.
  · Updated: start_qapbot.sh SQLITE_TMPDIR corrected from HDD path to SSD data/ path.
  · Manually ran move to clear backlog: 30,281 files moved, 0 skipped, ~2.1 GB, in seconds.

  · Now that archive/ and archive_old/ share the same SSD mount point (pure metadata rename,

2026-04-30
- Fixed: `kill PID` (SIGTERM) hung indefinitely when bot was in maintenance mode (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: _close_bot_after_signal() returned early when cleaned_up=True (set by
    do_maintenance_shutdown), but never called bot.close(). bot.run() was stuck waiting for
    the Discord WebSocket to close — only kill -9 could terminate the process.
  · Fix: changed `return` to `break` so the fallback loop always falls through to call
    bot.close() after cleanup is confirmed. bot.close() is idempotent — safe if already closed.
  · Result: kill PID now exits cleanly in ~1-3s from maintenance mode without kill -9.

2026-04-26
- Fixed: Nightly VACUUM OOM-killed bot process on server-machine (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: maintenance connection used cache_size=2 GB + mmap_size=8 GB + temp_store=MEMORY.
    VACUUM on a 1,268 MB freelist gradually filled physical RAM over 54 minutes until the Linux
    OOM killer issued SIGKILL — process terminated instantly with no cleanup, shell closed, no
    further log entries. The Discord ClientConnectionResetError at 05:57:50 was the last message
    logged before OOM struck, not the actual cause of termination.
  · Fixed: cache_size reduced from 2 GB to 128 MB (64× SQLite default, server-machine-safe).
  · Fixed: mmap_size set to 0 (no benefit on spinning HDD, wastes virtual address space).
  · Fixed: temp_store changed from MEMORY to FILE (avoids extra RAM from SQLite temp b-trees).
  · Optimised: replaced in-place VACUUM with VACUUM INTO for regular freelist path.
    VACUUM INTO writes a fresh sequential file (10-30× faster on HDD vs in-place random I/O).
    Original DB is never modified during operation; on failure the .vacuumed partial file is
    cleaned up and the original is left intact. After success, os.replace() atomically swaps
    the files, stale WAL/SHM sidecars are removed, and conn is reopened to the new file.
    Page_size migration retains in-place VACUUM (requires DELETE journal mode, one-time only).
  · Added: /proc/meminfo RAM snapshot logged before VACUUM (available/total MB visible in logs).
  · Added: VACUUM progress-logger daemon thread (every 2 min) and large-freelist WARNING (>200 MB).
  · Added: outer finally guards conn.close() in try/except — safe when conn is reassigned after
    VACUUM INTO file swap.
  · Fixed: corrected QapBot.py comment that wrongly stated maintenance takes "~30-40s".

2026-04-25

  · Added FRIENDLY-ARCHIVED-SKIP guard in save_war_object(): when archive exists but
    war is not in history, reads archive JSON type; if 'friendly', skips saving the
    temp file (prevents infinite recreate→archive→recreate loop when CoC API keeps
    returning an ended friendly war and clan has no active new war).
  · Leaderboard already showed ongoing friendly wars via temp stats — no change needed.
  · Legacy friendly wars already in history are unaffected (only new finalizations skip).

- Optimised: Phase-3B passive-clan skip-until set (QapBot.py)
  · Problem: On cycle 1 after restart, Phase-3B iterated ALL 18519 passive clans (no
    mtime cutoff), calling manage_war_files() for 15211 in_war clans and 3310 not_ready
    clans — 31s of mostly-wasted server-machine I/O. Cycle 2+ was already fast via the mtime guard.
  · Fix: Added a three-tier filter before the mtime check:
    1. Skip-until dict lookup: {safe_tag → unix_ts}. Zero server-machine I/O. Persists across
       cycles on QBcore.bot._p3b_skip_until.
    2. Metadata pre-seed: for clans not yet in the dict, check CACHE.temp_war_metadata
       (in-memory, zero I/O). If state='in_war' and war ends >5min from now, seed
       skip_until = war_end - 4.5min. If state='preparation', seed skip_until =
       war_start + 4.5min. Skips clan immediately.
    3. Mtime check (existing, server-machine stat): only for clans that pass tiers 1 and 2.
  · After each processed clan: if manage_war_files produced no new DB appends/moves,
    record skip_until = now + sleep_interval (clan stays guarded for one cycle). If
    something was finalized, remove the entry so the clan is checked normally next cycle.
  · Pruning: expired entries removed from dict at end of every Phase-3B run.
  · Expected impact: cycle 1 skips 15211+ in_war clans via tier-2 (metadata lookup),
    reducing Phase-3B from ~31s to near the Phase-3 processing time for the ~50 clans
    that actually need finalization. Cycle 2+ already handled by mtime + tier-1 dict.
  · Renamed cycle-summary label passive_skipped_mtime → passive_skipped in
    qapbot/cache_manager.py (now covers all skip reasons).

- Optimised: Backdated timestamp dirty-check (QapBot.py)
  · Problem: On every cycle, 18519+ clans in in_war/preparation state were added to
    _timestamp_batch unconditionally, triggering a ~0.30s bulk server-machine DB write even when
    the backdated timestamp was identical to the previous cycle (same war, same end time).
  · Fix: Added `if _bd_iso != last_update:` before _timestamp_batch.append() in both
    the in_war and preparation branches. The in-memory clan_data and _dt_cache are still
    updated unconditionally (cheap). Only genuinely changed values reach the DB.
  · Expected impact: steady-state cycles write near-zero backdated timestamps (only
    clans whose war state or timing changed), reducing the DB-TS-FLUSH from 18519 rows
    to <50 in most cycles, saving ~0.30s of server-machine write time per cycle.
  · All 1312 tests passing.

  · move_old_archive_files.py: added run(source_dir, dest_dir, days) programmatic entry
    point returning a result dict (moved, skipped, total_bytes) instead of printing to
    stdout. Safe to call from a background thread. main() CLI unchanged.
  · QapBot.py: added module-level _archive_move_nightly() — thread-safe wrapper that
    calls run(), logs results via logging.info([ARCHIVE-MOVE]), and swallows all exceptions
    so a file-move failure never prevents DB maintenance from starting.
  · _nightly_maintenance_task() now runs _archive_move_nightly via asyncio.to_thread()
    before nightly_db_maintenance(). db_maintenance_idle_event is cleared for the whole

- Removed: Two redundant indexes on war_attacks to reduce INSERT overhead and REINDEX cost
  · idx_wa_clan_tag(clan_tag) dropped — strict subset of idx_wa_clan_date(clan_tag, date);
    SQLite uses a composite index's leading column for single-column equality lookups, so
    all WHERE clan_tag=? queries still use idx_wa_clan_date with no plan change.
  · idx_wa_war_id(war_id) dropped — strict subset of idx_wa_war_clan(war_id, clan_tag)
    and also covered by the UNIQUE autoindex on (war_id, player_tag, attack_order).
    No query in the codebase filters war_id alone without clan_tag in the same predicate.
  · DROP INDEX NOT run during startup: moving these drops into _create_all_schemas() caused
    a startup timeout (DROP INDEX on a 27M-row table on server-machine costs 30-60 s, shared with
    the 60-second startup_login budget that also covers CoC API authentication).
  · Fix: new WarHistoryDB.run_deferred_index_cleanup() method; fired as a fire-and-forget
    asyncio.create_task() from on_ready() Step 10, after bot.fully_initialized = True,
    completely outside any timeout gate. IF EXISTS makes it a no-op on subsequent starts.
  · Impact: Fewer B-tree updates on every INSERT into war_attacks (~27M rows); nightly
    REINDEX touches 4 explicit indexes instead of 6, reducing nightly maintenance time.
  · All 1312 tests passing.
- Fixed: Bot startup timeout caused by VACUUM running during DB schema initialization
  · Root cause: _create_all_schemas() checked PRAGMA freelist_count and ran VACUUM if
    freelist > 500 pages. With 16,168 free pages (~63 MB), VACUUM exceeded the 60-second
    CoC API authentication + DB init timeout, preventing the bot from starting.
  · Fix: Removed the startup VACUUM entirely. Nightly DB maintenance already runs VACUUM
    with the same freelist > 500 threshold — the startup check was redundant and harmful.

2026-04-23
- Fixed: Both clans at max stars now reported as certain draw (not MC-estimated ~98%)
  · Root cause: calculate_win_probability() only short-circuited to draw when
    attacks_left == 0 AND enemy_attacks_left == 0. With remaining unused attacks,
    the code fell through to MC simulation, which returned ~98% draw instead of 100%.
    When all enemy bases are already 3-starred, remaining attacks cannot gain any more
    stars — the outcome is deterministically a draw.
  · Fix: Added deterministic case after the win/loss checks: when my_stars == enemy_stars
    AND both max_possible == current_stars (no more stars achievable), return 0/0/100/100
    immediately without running MC simulation.
  · Files: QBwarsim.py
  · Tests: 1 new test in tests/unit/test_warsim_edges.py (1312 total, all passing)

- Improved: Monte Carlo tiebreaker now uses simulated destruction instead of static mid-war values
  · Root cause: _monte_carlo_chunk() used current mid-war destruction percentages as a
    static tiebreaker when simulated stars were equal. This biased predictions toward
    the clan that had used more attacks at prediction time (higher current destruction)
    even though remaining simulated attacks would also contribute destruction.
    Example: 10v10 war, clan has 20 unused attacks from TH18/16/14 players, many sims
    result in both sides hitting 30/30 stars, but opponent's higher current destruction
    (100% vs 90.6%) caused those max-star ties to count as losses → 1% win / 98% loss
    instead of a high draw probability.
  · Fix: _run_sim_prebuilt() now returns (stars_gained, destruction_pct) where destruction
    is estimated from the final per-base star state using _DEST_FOR_STARS mapping
    (0★→0%, 1★→45%, 2★→75%, 3★→100%). Applied consistently to both sides, so the
    tiebreaker dynamically reflects the simulated outcome. The max-stars-draw case
    (all bases 3-starred → 100% destruction both sides) is now a natural consequence
    rather than a special case.
  · Files: QBwarsim.py
  · Tests: 2 new tests in tests/unit/test_warsim_coverage.py (1311 total, all passing)

- Fixed: Component interactions (buttons, selects, modals) now blocked during maintenance mode
  · Root cause: bot.tree.interaction_check only guards slash commands (app commands).
    Component interactions dispatched to View/Modal callbacks bypassed it entirely.
    During maintenance, users clicking registration buttons or submitting modals triggered
    DB writes (RuntimeError: DB closed) and CoC API calls (Session is closed), producing
    misleading "player not found" errors instead of the maintenance notice.
  · Fix: Monkey-patched discord.ui.View.interaction_check and discord.ui.Modal.interaction_check
    in QBcore.py to call check_maintenance_block() before any component callback runs.
    Added check_maintenance_block() helper in ui_common.py (checks both maintenance_mode
    and db_maintenance_mode, sends ephemeral maintenance message, returns True to block).
    Updated 3 views in ui_notifications.py that override interaction_check to call
    super().interaction_check() so the maintenance guard propagates.
  · Files: QBcore.py, qapbot/ui_common.py, qapbot/ui_notifications.py
  · Tests: 4 new tests in tests/discord/test_ui_common.py (1309 total, all passing)

2026-04-19
- Fixed: FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP now checks DB state before blocking war_ended saves (qapbot/cache_manager.py, qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP fired whenever archive + history both existed, regardless of
    whether the DB war_summary.state was actually "war_ended". Wars prematurely archived by the
    Phase-3B bug had state="in_war" in the DB but a physical archive file, so the final corrective
    war_ended snapshot was silently discarded — the self-heal path was blocked.
  · Fix: Added get_war_summary_state_sync() to db_manager (queries war_summary.state by
    clan_tag + war_id). In cache_manager.save_war_object(), the skip now only fires when
    DB state == "war_ended". When DB state is "in_war" or any other non-final value, the
    snapshot is allowed through with a FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP-OVERRIDE log, letting ARCHIVE-DIFFERS
    and update_war_data_sync() correct the DB record and replace the archive.
  · Self-heal path restored for all ~70K wars prematurely archived during the Apr 12–19 window.
  · Test updated: test_finalized_war_skipped now sets db_manager.get_war_summary_state_sync
    return_value = "war_ended" to match the new gate.
- Added: scan_archive_states.py diagnostic script (qapbot/scripts/scan_archive_states.py)
  · Recursively scans one or more archive directories for *_war_data.json files with
    state != war_ended, reporting attack count and time-remaining-when-archived buckets.
  · Two-phase design: single os.scandir() pass collects (path, mtime) into memory (no
    redundant stat calls); sequential JSON reads then use the cached mtime directly.
  · Progress lines every 10 000 files include bucketed breakdown (already_over / <1h / 1–2h / >=2h).
  · CLI: python qapbot/scripts/scan_archive_states.py [--archive-dir DIR ...] [--limit N]

  · Root cause: Phase-3B processes any clan not polled in the current cycle. Tracked clans
    (track_war_updates=True) are polled every ~22h, so they appear "passive" in ~263 of 264
    cycles. Phase-3B called manage_war_files(clan, "") with no opponent → current_opponent_clean=None
    → is_orphaned=True for ALL files → in_war temp files were finalized as FINALIZE-ORPHAN-REGULAR
    with partial attack data. The next 22h poll fetched complete war_ended data, creating a new temp
    file → ARCHIVE-DIFFERS overwrite (hundreds per cycle).
  · Trigger: appeared on 2026-04-11 when tracking threshold was lowered from Master II to Master III,
    dramatically increasing tracked clans subject to this premature finalization.
  · Fix: in the FINALIZE-ORPHAN-REGULAR path, when current_opponent_clean is None (Phase-3B context),
    only finalize if the war's end_time is in the past. If end_time is unparseable, falls back to
    start_time + 25h. If neither is available, skips conservatively (waits for next poll).
  · When current_opponent_clean is set (Phase-3 context, clan in a different war), finalization
    is immediate as before — the old war must have ended.
  · Result: in_war snapshots are preserved until the war actually ends; no more premature archives
    or ARCHIVE-DIFFERS churn.

  · New CACHE.cycle_stats dict (reset each cycle), record_cycle_stat(key, n) accumulator,

  · QapBot.main() records stats at every decision branch: api_fetched, api_fail:*, temp_written,

  · Each line is logged individually so every line carries a proper timestamp and [INFO] prefix.

2026-04-18
- Optimized: Cross-cycle datetime parse cache for clan categorization loop (QapBot.py, qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · Root cause: The 12K-clan categorization loop called datetime.fromisoformat() for every inactive
    clan every cycle, even though last_war_update strings only change for the ~564 clans polled per
    cycle. On CPython, 12K fromisoformat() calls + timezone checks + timedelta arithmetic accounted
    for ~2.3s of clock time each cycle.
  · Fix: Added CACHE.clan_dt_cache (Dict[str, Tuple[str, datetime]]) — a persistent dict mapping
    clan_tag to (raw_iso_string, parsed_datetime). Each cycle, if the stored raw string matches
    clan_data["last_war_update"], the pre-parsed datetime is reused directly. Re-parse only happens
    when the string changes. Precomputed cutoff_dt (now - 22h) replaces per-clan hours_since_update
    arithmetic for the fast-path check. Backdating writes also update the cache to avoid staleness.
  · Type fix: Originally used QBcore.bot._clan_dt_cache (dynamic attribute), causing Pylance
    Unknown type propagation. Moved to CACHE.clan_dt_cache (typed at __init__) — consistent with
    Cardinal Rule #2 (CACHE as single source of truth) and eliminates all type errors.
  · Expected improvement: categorization drops from ~2.3s on cycle 1 to ~0.5s on subsequent cycles
    (cache hit rate ~96% once stable).

- Optimized: DB-BULK-UPDATE path flattened from per-war loops to batch executemany (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: The UPDATE code path in sync_batch() ran DELETE + INSERT per war inside a Python
    for-loop, issuing N×2 separate SQL statements. Each statement required a separate B-tree
    traversal on the 22.5M-row war_attacks table. On HDD, each traversal costs 3-5 seeks.
  · Fix: Collect all DELETE keys and INSERT params across the batch first, then issue a single
    executemany() for DELETEs, a single executemany() for attack INSERTs, and a single
    executemany() for summary INSERTs — followed by one commit(). Matches the already-optimized
    APPEND path pattern.
  · Expected improvement: batch of 7 wars drops from ~7 SQL round-trips to 3.
- Added: composite index idx_wa_war_clan on war_attacks(war_id, clan_tag) (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · The DELETE FROM war_attacks WHERE war_id=? AND clan_tag=? query could only use the single-
    column idx_wa_war_id index, requiring a post-filter on clan_tag. The composite index enables
    exact-match lookups in a single B-tree traversal.
- Fixed: /status command taking many seconds (qapbot/db_manager.py, QBdiscordcmds.py)
  · Root cause: get_global_db_statistics_sync() had a UNION of user_players + war_attacks to
    count unique player_tags, forcing SQLite to deduplicate all 22.5M war_attacks rows via
    hash/sort — ~10-30s on HDD regardless of indexes.
  · Fix: Replaced with COUNT(*) FROM user_players (the small table of Discord-linked accounts,
    a few thousand rows). Label updated from "Players tracked" to "Linked CoC accounts".
- Optimized: Phase 3B intermediate flush interval 1000→5000 clans (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: With ~12K passive clans and only ~20 wars to flush per batch, the 1000-clan
    interval produced ~12 tiny DB flushes (1 append + ~10 updates each), each paying
    2-4s COMMIT + WAL checkpoint overhead on HDD. Total: ~80s wasted.
  · Fix: Raise interval to 5000 clans. Add skip_checkpoint=True on intermediate flushes.
    A single background WAL checkpoint fires after the final flush.
- Fixed: bulk_update_clan_timestamps taking 4.75s for 419 rows after Phase 3 flush (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: The final flush_pending_war_writes() called PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)
    synchronously, leaving the WAL dirty/large. The immediately following timestamp flush ran
    its async executemany+commit against that dirty state, stalling for 4.75s vs 0.19s baseline.
  · Fix: Skip checkpoint on the final flush too. Fire a single background asyncio.create_task(
    run_passive_checkpoint) after all Phase 3 DB writes (war data + timestamps) complete.
- Optimized: Phase 3B skips unchanged passive clan temp files (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: Phase 3B called manage_war_files() for ALL ~12K passive clans every 5-minute
    cycle, reading each JSON file from HDD to check state=in_war → skip. Passive clans are
    only polled every 22h so their temp files can't change between polls.
    Cost: ~12K unnecessary HDD reads every 5 minutes.
  · Fix: Track _last_phase3b_time on bot. Each cycle, only process passive clans whose newest
    temp file was modified since the last Phase 3B run (mtime threshold with 60s overlap buffer).
    First cycle after restart processes everything (threshold=0). Expected: Phase 3B drops from
    ~22s to near-zero on normal cycles.
- Optimized: temp war file reads eliminated from hot path (qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: temp_war_metadata only stored state/start_time/end_time/filepath. Three hot-path
    functions still read JSON from disk to get type, is_cwl, war_tag, opponent_tag:
    - _manage_war_files_inner(): read newest file to check state/type for "current war" detection
    - _scan_orphans(): read single-file CWL temp files to check is_cwl/war_tag/state
    - get_current_war_data(): read full JSON from disk for every call
  · Fix 1: Enrich temp_war_metadata with type, is_cwl, war_tag, opponent_tag at all write points
    (save_war_object(), startup Phase 1 filename scan, startup Phase 2 JSON parse).
  · Fix 2: Add temp_war_objects dict — caches the full war payload in memory. save_war_object()
    writes to both disk and cache; get_current_war_data() reads from cache first (disk only as
    cold-start fallback). Startup Phase 2 JSON parse also populates temp_war_objects.
    set_temp_war_stats({}) clears the cache entry when a clan's war ends.
  · Fix 3: _manage_war_files_inner() uses cached metadata for newest-file state check,
    falling back to JSON read only when metadata is absent.
  · Fix 4: _scan_orphans() uses cached metadata (type/is_cwl/war_tag/end_time) for single-file
    CWL orphan detection, falling back to JSON read only when cache is cold.
- Fixed: type annotation for temp_war_metadata (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · Type was Dict[str, Dict[str, str]] but now stores non-string values (bool for is_cwl,
    None for optional fields). Updated to Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]].
  · Root cause: The UPDATE code path in sync_batch() ran DELETE + INSERT per war inside a Python
    for-loop, issuing N×2 separate SQL statements. Each statement required a separate B-tree
    traversal on the 22.5M-row war_attacks table. On HDD, each traversal costs 3-5 seeks.
  · Fix: Collect all DELETE keys and INSERT params across the batch first, then issue a single
    executemany() for DELETEs, a single executemany() for attack INSERTs, and a single
    executemany() for summary INSERTs — followed by one commit(). Matches the already-optimized

  · Expected improvement: batch of 7 wars drops from ~7 SQL round-trips to 3.
- Added: composite index idx_wa_war_clan on war_attacks(war_id, clan_tag) (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · The DELETE FROM war_attacks WHERE war_id=? AND clan_tag=? query could only use the single-
