- Added: startup log for effective mmap_size (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Logs whether the requested 8 GB mmap_size was capped by the SQLite build's MAX_MMAP_SIZE
    (the prod server's SQLite build caps at ~2 GB). Helps diagnose cache performance without SSH.
- Fixed: page_size migration 4096→16384 silently failing (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: SQLite docs state "VACUUM may change the page_size only if no WAL journal
    is in use." The maintenance connection opened the DB in WAL mode (inherited from the
    database file), so PRAGMA page_size=16384 followed by VACUUM was silently ignored.
  · Fix: Switch to journal_mode=DELETE before the migration VACUUM, then restore WAL after.
    The EXCLUSIVE lock guarantees no other connections, making the mode switch safe.
    On failure, WAL is also restored in the exception handler.
- Fixed: pragma regressions introduced during connection pool refactoring (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · _reconnect() had busy_timeout=5000 but initialize() uses busy_timeout=30000. After DB
    maintenance (which closes+reopens the async connection), the timeout dropped from 30s to 5s
    for the rest of the session. Fixed: _reconnect() now uses busy_timeout=30000.
  · _apply_sync_pragmas (pool connections) was missing foreign_keys=ON. All writes via pool
    connections could bypass FK constraints. Fixed: added foreign_keys=ON to match async path.
- Fixed: DB maintenance hangs at EXCLUSIVE lock for 300s (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: pool.drain() closed all sync connections, but the aiosqlite async connection
    (self.conn) was still open and holding WAL locks. BEGIN EXCLUSIVE waited on that lock
    for the full 300s busy_timeout before proceeding.
  · Fix: Close the async connection before asyncio.to_thread(_run), reopen via _reconnect()
    after maintenance completes (before unblocking Discord commands). Now the maintenance
    connection is truly the sole owner of the database.
- Fixed: "SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread" (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: _SyncConnectionPool._fill() creates all 8 connections on the main thread during
    initialize(), but asyncio.to_thread() dispatches sync DB operations to worker threads.
    SQLite's default check_same_thread=True rejects cross-thread usage.
  · Fix: Added check_same_thread=False to _create_conn(). This is safe because the pool serializes
    access — each connection is only used by one thread at a time via acquire()/release().
- Fixed: type errors and pool bypass in post-refactoring cleanup (qapbot/db_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Removed unused `import sqlite3 as _sq` in _SyncConnectionPool.__init__ (was dead code after moving
    sqlite3 usage to _create_conn).
  · Removed stale `import sqlite3` in get_all_war_clan_tags() (never used — reads go via _sync_conn()).
  · Added `assert pool is not None` guard in _sync_conn() before acquire/release to satisfy type checker
    (pool is Optional — assertion makes the non-None branch clear and eliminates "None has no attribute"
    errors without changing runtime behaviour since the None path already returns early).
  · Simplified integrity_check result access: `str(result[0]) if result else "error"` — avoids
    "partially unknown type" error from isinstance branch on aiosqlite.Row.
  · Migrated _load_cwl_analysis_from_db_sync() (QBhelperfunctions.py) from direct sqlite3.connect()
    to CACHE.db_manager.sync_conn() — this was the last runtime code bypassing the connection pool.
    Added public sync_conn() alias on WarHistoryDB so external callers don't touch the protected
    _sync_conn() method. Data processing loop moved outside the `with` block to return the connection
    to the pool sooner.
  · All 1305 tests passing

- Refactored: Connection pool architecture for sync DB access (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Added `_SyncConnectionPool` class — bounded pool of 8 pre-configured sqlite3 connections
    with `acquire()`/`release()` semantics, replacing unbounded TLS-cached connections
  · Added `_sync_conn()` context manager — replaces `_get_sync_conn()` tuple pattern and
    throwaway `sqlite3.connect()` in 22+ call sites
  · Pool `drain()` enables clean EXCLUSIVE lock during maintenance: waits for workers to
    release connections (proper statement finalization from owning thread — no zombie locks),
    then closes all connections. Maintenance now acquires EXCLUSIVE lock reliably.
  · Pool `close_all()` replaces manual `_all_sync_conns` iteration on shutdown
  · Eliminated 13 throwaway `sqlite3.connect()`/`close()` in read methods — these had NO
    pragma tuning (no WAL, no mmap, no cache_size), wasting HDD I/O
  · All 1305 tests passing
- Added: DB maintenance mode — block Discord commands and use locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE during maintenance (QBcore.py, qapbot/db_manager.py, translations)
  · Root cause: DB maintenance (VACUUM/REINDEX) previously ran while all persistent connections stayed open.
    locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE requires being the sole connection — with the bot's async + sync connections
    open, SQLite waited out the full 300s busy_timeout then failed with "database is locked".
  · Fix: nightly_db_maintenance() now has a close→run→reopen lifecycle:
    Phase 0: sets QBcore.db_maintenance_mode=True, commits+closes async connection, closes all
    sync thread-local connections (via _all_sync_conns), increments _sync_conn_gen so stale
    thread-local refs are auto-detected and discarded on next use.
    Phase 1: opens a dedicated maintenance connection with locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE, mmap_size=8GB,
    cache_size=-2GB, threads=2, synchronous=OFF, temp_store_directory→DB dir.
    Phase 2: reopens async connection via _reconnect() (sync conns lazily recreated on next use),
    sets db_maintenance_mode=False to unblock commands.
  · Added QBcore.db_maintenance_mode flag (separate from maintenance_mode — minutes not hours).
  · Updated _maintenance_interaction_check() in QBcore.py to check db_maintenance_mode and return
    a localized "DB optimization in progress" message to users (all commands blocked, no admin escape
    hatch needed — maintenance is short-lived).
  · Added i18n keys commands.errors.db_maintenance_active (EN + DE translations).
  · Fixed: _reconnect() was missing PRAGMA cache_size and mmap_size — now matches initialize() pragmas.
  · Fixed: locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE removed from the "connections still open" code path (caused the original
    300s hang); now only set in the maintenance lifecycle where it is the sole connection.
  · Result: maintenance runs with maximum I/O throughput (no SHM checks per page) and users get a
    friendly "try again in a few minutes" message instead of a silent command failure.
- Fixed: nightly DB maintenance opening log line was stale (still said "ANALYZE, REINDEX, VACUUM check")
  · Updated to: "1: WAL checkpoint → 2: VACUUM or REINDEX → 3: ANALYZE"
  · Updated docstring to match the actual 3-step order.
- Optimized: nightly DB maintenance reordered, de-duplicated, and sped up (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause 1 (worst): REINDEX ran on 10 indexes BEFORE VACUUM. VACUUM rebuilds the entire DB
    including all indexes from scratch, so the REINDEX work was immediately discarded — every
    index was built twice. On a 7 GB HDD database this wasted ~15-20 minutes per nightly run.
  · Root cause 2: ANALYZE ran BEFORE VACUUM/REINDEX, so sqlite_stat1 reflected the pre-rebuild
    B-tree layout. Query planner operated on stale statistics for 24 hours after every maintenance.
  · Root cause 3: VACUUM creates a full-size temp copy of the DB. Default OS temp dir in the prod
    container (/tmp, tmpfs) is too small for a 7 GB DB, risking SQLITE_FULL errors and cross-mount I/O.
  · Fix 1: Evaluate VACUUM conditions (freelist > 500 or page_size migration) BEFORE step 2 and
    skip REINDEX entirely when VACUUM is pending — each rebuild path now runs exactly once.
  · Fix 2: Move ANALYZE to the final step (after VACUUM/REINDEX) so sqlite_stat1 always reflects
    the final on-disk B-tree layout rather than the pre-maintenance state.
  · Fix 3: Added PRAGMA temp_store_directory → DB parent directory so VACUUM temp copy stays on
    the same filesystem (server-machine data volume), avoiding /tmp ramdisk size limits.
  · Fix 4: Added PRAGMA locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE on the maintenance connection to skip WAL-index
    (SHM) checks on every page access — measurable speedup for bulk operations.
  · Fix 5: Per-step timing added to all log lines (e.g. "ANALYZE done in 2.3s") for profiling.
  · New maintenance order: (1) WAL checkpoint → (2) VACUUM or REINDEX (mutually exclusive) →
    (3) ANALYZE + PRAGMA optimize. Step numbers updated in all log messages.
  · Expected total time reduction: ~37 min → ~12-15 min for a 7 GB database on HDD.
- Added: page_size=16384 one-time migration in nightly DB maintenance (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Current page_size=4096 means the 22M-row war_attacks B-tree is ~1 level deeper than
    necessary, costing one extra HDD seek (~10 ms) per index lookup.
  · Migration: if page_size < 16384, sets PRAGMA page_size=16384 and forces VACUUM (required
    for the pragma to take effect). On all subsequent runs _page_size_migration=False — no
    forced VACUUM, freelist threshold applies normally.
  · Logs before/after page_size and page counts. Adds "page_size 4096→16384" to result message.
  · Triggered by both nightly scheduler and /admin Optimize DB (both call nightly_db_maintenance()).
- Added: mmap_size=8589934592 (8 GB) on all DB connections (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Async connection (initialize()) and all sync connections (_apply_sync_pragmas()) now set
    PRAGMA mmap_size=8589934592 so the kernel page cache serves repeated index reads without
    going through Python/SQLite buffer pools — reduces effective random I/O on HDD.
  · Maintenance connection also sets mmap_size as part of boosted pragma block.
- Added: malloc_trim(0) after nightly maintenance (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · After VACUUM frees hundreds of MB of page cache and Python allocator pools, calls
    malloc_trim(0) via ctypes on Linux (glibc) to return fragmented heap arenas to the OS.
  · Silent no-op on Windows and any platform where libc.so.6 is unavailable.
  · Addresses the observed 640 MB → 5.8 GB RSS growth over 32 h caused by pymalloc arena
    fragmentation from high per-cycle churn (~12 MB/cycle × 12 cycles/hour).

2026-04-12
- Fixed: CWL recovery memory growth from retained league-war cache objects (qapbot/cache_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: process_cwl_recovery_batch() fetched up to 2000 historical war tags per cycle via
    CACHE.get_league_war(), which cached each large coc.ClanWar object in _league_war_cache with a
    2-hour TTL. Across multiple cycles this accumulated thousands of heavy objects and rapidly grew RSS.
  · Fix 1: added get_league_war(cache_result=False) option and switched CWL recovery fetches to use it,
    so one-shot historical recovery wars are not stored in long-lived cache.
  · Fix 2: split CWL recovery write dispatch into chunks (250 jobs) instead of one giant gather(),
    reducing peak in-cycle task/object memory pressure.
  · Fix 3: explicitly clear large per-batch lists (fetch_results/write_jobs) once re-queue bookkeeping
    is complete, allowing earlier garbage collection before later cycle phases.
- Fixed: Phase-3B silent crash after CWL season end (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: _war_batch_appends accumulated war data for ALL passive clans (14,461) before a
    single DB flush — post-CWL that means up to ~87,000 wars × ~20 attack rows in RAM simultaneously.
    Process was silently killed mid-write by SIGKILL (no Python traceback; the prod server has limited RAM
    and its memory-pressure manager or container resource limits can SIGKILL processes).
  · No data loss: file moves are properly deferred via defer_file_move(), so all temp files were
    still in data/temp/ on restart and were reprocessed cleanly; INSERT OR IGNORE prevented duplicates.
  · Fix: added intermediate flush inside the Phase-3B for-loop every 1,000 clans. Each flush
    writes accumulated appends/updates to DB (batches of 50) and executes the corresponding deferred
    file moves, then clears the lists. Caps peak memory to ~6,000 war entries at a time.
  · On flush failure the lists are kept intact so the final flush at end of Phase-3B retries them.
- Fixed: stale regular-war preparation temp files now removed under exact conditions (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Edge case: some temp files remained forever with type=random, state=preparation, and end_time already past.
    They are outdated snapshots and cannot be re-fetched by war_tag (war_tag is null for random wars).
  · Fix: manage_war_files() now removes temp files only when all 3 conditions match exactly:
    type=random AND state=preparation AND parsed end_time < now.
  · Additional guard: such stale files are no longer eligible to be classified as "current war"
    (newest+matching-opponent), preventing an infinite skip loop.
- Fixed: orphan CWL scanner missed long-ended wars when end_time string omitted seconds (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: process_orphaned_cwl_wars() parsed end_time using a strict regex that required
    datetime.datetime(..., second). Some temp JSON files store end_time as 5-tuple
    datetime.datetime(Y, M, D, H, M), so parsing failed silently and the file was never marked orphan.
  · Impact: manage_war_files() repeatedly logged [CWL-ORPHAN-SKIP] for stale in_war CWL files
    (for example VJPRL9LP_LUGPRL0U_202604060848_war_data.json) even though end_time was long past.
  · Fix: timestamp parser now accepts both 5-field and 6-field datetime formats; if end_time is
    missing/malformed, fallback derives end_time from start_time + 24h. Expired wars are now queued
    for get_league_war(war_tag) refresh and finalized normally.
- Added: script to move old archive war files into archive_old while preserving shard structure (qapbot/scripts/move_old_archive_files.py)
  · Scans archive/ recursively for *_war_data.json files and moves only files older than 3 days by mtime.
  · Preserves shard layout by keeping each file's relative path under archive/ when moving to archive_old/.
  · Supports --source, --dest, --days, and --dry-run so it can be used against DEV or PROD paths safely.
- Fixed: WAL file not cleaned up after DB maintenance/optimization (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause 1: Step 1 used PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE) which copies WAL frames to the
    main DB file but never truncates the WAL file itself — leaving a 4 GB WAL on disk indefinitely.
  · Root cause 2: After a successful VACUUM (which rewrites the entire DB), the now-stale WAL
    file was not cleaned up either.
  · Fix 1: Changed Step 1 from PASSIVE to TRUNCATE mode — checkpoints all available frames and
    zeros the WAL file. Logs busy/frames/moved counts for visibility.
  · Fix 2: Added a post-VACUUM wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) call after successful VACUUM so the
    stale WAL is zeroed immediately after VACUUM completes.
  · Result: WAL file shrinks to near-zero (a few KB header) after each maintenance run.
- Changed: /admin Optimize DB batch size raised from 1000 to 2000 (QBhelperfunctions.py)
- Fixed: /admin Maintenance Start now waits for active DB maintenance before shutdown (QBdiscordcmds.py)
  · Scenario: during sleep phase, a deferred/manual DB optimize task can still be running
    (db_maintenance_idle_event=clear). Maintenance Start previously entered immediate shutdown
    when cycle_idle_event was set, potentially closing DB while VACUUM/REINDEX was in progress.
  · Fix: in the no-cycle MAINTENANCE_START path, if db_maintenance_idle_event is not set,
    the command updates its deferred response to "queued/waiting", awaits maintenance completion,
    then performs do_maintenance_shutdown() and edits the same response to "maintenance active".
  · Result: no next update cycle starts, and maintenance mode activates immediately after DB
    maintenance finishes (intended behavior), without DB-close race.
- Changed: reduced misleading MANAGE-WAR-FILES log spam for non-current temp files (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Clarified semantics: "old war" in this path only meant "not the current active file candidate",
    not necessarily chronologically old. Many such files are quickly skipped by state/orphan checks.
  · Per-file line changed from INFO to DEBUG and reworded to "Inspecting non-current war file".
    Finalization events remain INFO via [FINALIZE-OLD-WAR] / [FINALIZE-ORPHAN-REGULAR].
- Fixed: startup false-negative timeout during PROD boot (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: on_ready() wrapped startup_login() (DB init + CoC login) in a single
    15-second timeout. On server-machine/containers, DB initialization alone can consume most of
    that window, causing a misleading CoC timeout even when credentials/network are fine.
  · Fix: increased startup_login wait_for timeout from 15s to 60s in both CoC and
    NO_COC_API startup paths, and clarified the timeout log message to explicitly state
    it includes DB initialization and CoC authentication.
- Fixed: VACUUM failing with "database or disk is full" during DB maintenance on Docker (db_manager.py, Dockerfile)
  · Root cause: SQLite VACUUM creates a full temp copy of the DB in the OS temp dir (/tmp inside the
    container, a small tmpfs) rather than on the mounted data volume. When the DB is large, /tmp runs
    out of space and SQLite returns SQLITE_FULL even though the underlying server-machine has TB free.
  · Fix 1 (Dockerfile): Added ENV SQLITE_TMPDIR=/app/data so SQLite directs its VACUUM temp copy to
    the persistent data volume (same filesystem as the DB) rather than container /tmp.
  · Fix 2 (db_manager.py): Wrapped VACUUM and each individual REINDEX in their own try/except so a
    single step failure no longer aborts the entire maintenance run. ANALYZE + preceding steps still
    succeed and are reported. VACUUM failure logs a diagnostic hint about the SQLITE_TMPDIR cause.
- Added: /admin Optimize DB command — triggers on-demand DB maintenance (QBdiscordcmds.py, QBcore.py, QapBot.py)
  · Runs nightly_db_maintenance() (ANALYZE, PRAGMA optimize, REINDEX, conditional VACUUM) on demand.
  · If issued while an update cycle is running, queued via QBcore.optimize_db_pending +
    optimize_db_pending_interaction; periodic_main() launches the task after the cycle finishes
    and sends the result back via edit_original_response() (same deferred pattern as Maintenance Start).
  · If issued while idle, runs immediately as a background task via asyncio.create_task(), sends
    result to followup.send(). Uses db_maintenance_idle_event to block the next update cycle
    from starting while VACUUM holds an exclusive lock (same gate as nightly scheduler).
  · Bot-admin only; blocked in maintenance_mode; de-duplication: second request while one is
    pending returns an "already queued" warning.
- Fixed: post-cycle flag handling race conditions when multiple admin commands issued simultaneously (QapBot.py)
  · Bug 1 (force_cycle + optimize): force_cycle_pending `continue`d past the optimize_db_pending check,
    leaving it unconsumed until after the NEXT full cycle. The Discord interaction token (15-min TTL)
    would expire silently. Also: the next cycle started without the db_maintenance_idle_event gate.
    Fix: moved optimize_db_pending check BEFORE force_cycle_pending. If both are set, the optimize
    task launches first, then force_cycle awaits db_maintenance_idle_event inline before continuing.
  · Bug 2 (maintenance + optimize): maintenance_pending called do_maintenance_shutdown() (closing the DB)
    then `continue`d to the idle loop, leaving optimize_db_pending=True with a zombie interaction
    and a reference to a closed db_manager.
    Fix: inside the maintenance_pending block, before the `continue`, any queued optimize is cancelled
    and the user gets "❌ DB optimization cancelled — maintenance mode is being activated." Also
    clears force_cycle_pending (irrelevant once maintenance suspends all cycles).
- Fixed: temp files for passive clans (track_war_updates=False) never finalized, causing 43k+ file accumulation (QapBot.py)
  · Root cause: passive clans are intentionally skipped from Phase 1 polling. However, the CWL recovery
    pipeline writes temp files for them (CWL opponents, passively tracked clans). Those clans never appear
    in fetch_results, so manage_war_files() was never called. war_ended + stale in_war files piled up forever.
  · Fix: added Phase 3b after the main Phase 3 loop. Computes polled_safe_tags from clans_to_update, then
    iterates _temp_files_by_clan for any tag not in that set, calling manage_war_files(..., "", ...)
    so all war_ended files are finalized. CWL in_war files are still left for the Phase 2 orphan scanner.
  · Yields to event loop every 10 passive clans; uses same _with_war_batch() context as Phase 3.
- Fixed: CWL recovery re-queue still missed failed writes that returned False (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: exception-write bucket only tracked BaseException results from gather().
    But save_war_object() can catch/log internally and return False, which incremented
    write_failures without marking the war_tag for re-queue.
  · Fix: added tags_with_false_write + tags_with_failed_write tracking and re-queue any
    war_tag with a failed write (exception OR False), even if the other side write succeeded.
  · Summary/Warning now includes a dedicated false-write bucket.
- Fixed: CWL recovery still hit "generator already executing" from coc.war_members attacks (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: for a single war_tag, both save_war_object() writes (tracked clan + opponent clan)
    could run concurrently in different threads. coc.py lazily builds member.attacks from a
    non-reentrant generator, so parallel access on the same war object raised ValueError.
  · Fix: added per-war_tag asyncio locks in process_cwl_recovery_batch() so writes for the same
    war run sequentially, while writes for different war_tags stay concurrent.
- Fixed: current-war header could show "No current war information" after sharding (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · Root cause: get_current_war_data() still searched only flat data/temp/{tag}_*_war_data.json.
    After sharding, active files are in data/temp/shard_N/, so war_data lookup returned None.
  · Fix: get_current_war_data() now uses metadata filepath first, then shard path via
    get_war_shard_dir(), then legacy flat fallback.
- Fixed: CWL recovery concurrent writes could trigger "generator already executing" (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: two concurrent to_thread(save_war_object) calls for the same war_obj raced on
    coc.WarClan.members (non-reentrant generator-backed cached property).
  · Fix: pre-warm my_side.members and opp_side.members in the main async thread before write dispatch.
- Fixed: exception-write tags were not re-queued in CWL recovery partial-failure cases (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: _write_one() caught exceptions and returned False, so the BaseException branch in
    result handling was unreachable; tags with one success + one exception were counted as consumed.
  · Fix: _write_one() now lets exceptions bubble to gather(return_exceptions=True), tracks
    tags_with_exception_write, and re-queues those tags unconditionally.
  · Batch summary now splits re-queued tags into non-200, exception-write, and no-write buckets.
- Fixed: remaining non-shard-aware path construction found in sharding audit
  · QBhelperfunctions.py: backfill_last_cwl_for_clan() and backfill_cwl_from_war_tags() now build
    temp_json_path with get_war_shard_dir(..., data/temp) before calling _process_war_history().
  · qapbot/scripts/verify_all_cwl_history.py now scans archive recursively (shards + flat fallback).
  · qapbot/scripts/recover_missing_cwl_history.py now resolves filenames in shard dirs (and legacy flat).
  · qapbot/scripts/debug_war_api.py now resolves archived files in shard dirs before failing.
- Added: Directory sharding for data/temp/ and archive/ (10 subdirs, alphabetical by 3-char clan tag prefix)
  · Root cause: 99k+ flat JSON files in two dirs caused HDD/server-machine I/O slowdown (directory entry traversal is O(n)).
  · Boundaries computed from 484,532 files across temp + archive + archive_old using bisect-balanced split (±1%).
  · Shard ranges: shard_0 200–2C9, shard_1 2CC–2GY, shard_2 2J0–2LG, shard_3 2LJ–2QC, shard_4 2QG–2RQ,
    shard_5 2RR–8G9, shard_6 8GC–CJ9, shard_7 CJC–LY9, shard_8 LYC–RJJ, shard_9 RJL–YYY.
  · Added get_war_shard_dir(safe_tag, base_dir) and migrate_war_files_to_shards() to QBcsvhandling.py.
  · Startup migration in periodic_main() (QapBot.py): runs once via asyncio.to_thread on first startup,
    moves all flat files atomically using os.replace() (O(1) rename on same filesystem, no data copy).
  · Updated all 19 path sites across 6 files: QBcsvhandling.py (3), QBhelperfunctions.py (6),
    qapbot/cache_manager.py (3), QapBot.py (pre-scan + migration), qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py,
    qapbot/scripts/recover_cwl_war.py.
  · Updated ArchiveOld.bat: find -maxdepth 1 → -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 2 to reach shard_N subdirs.
  · Added migrate_archive_old_to_shards.py: one-off server-machine script to unpack archive_old.tar.gz, shard the
    flat files, repack, and remove the shard dirs. Successfully migrated 385,117 files on server-machine.
  · DEV validation: migration moved 32,825 temp / 225,784 archive files at startup; 6,099 wars
    finalized on the same day through sharded paths with no errors.
- Fixed: CWL recovery — non-200 tags and zero-write tags now re-queued at end of queue (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Change 1: _fetch() now returns (war_obj, is_200: bool). Tags with non-200 responses (404, 403,
    503, generic error) are collected in non_200_tags and appended back to CACHE.pending_cwl_recovery
    after the batch completes. Previously they were silently dropped.
  · Change 2: After the write pass, if written_count < batch_size a WARNING is logged:
    "[CWL-RECOVERY] Only N successful write(s) for a batch of M tag(s) — fewer than expected."
  · Change 3: tags_with_write_jobs and tags_with_successful_write sets track which war_tags had
    at least one file write attempted and at least one success. Tags with write jobs but zero
    successes (no_write_tags) are also appended to the back of the queue for later retry.
  · Both non_200_tags + no_write_tags are combined into requeue_tags; a single WARNING is logged
    with the per-category counts. persist_cwl_recovery_file() saves the extended queue atomically.
  · Batch summary now reports: "N pulled, N net consumed, N re-queued (N non-200, N no-write),
    N temp files written, N already in DB, N write failures."
- Fixed: save_war_object() error log referenced undefined 'temp_path' (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · The except block logged f"Failed to save war object to {temp_path}: {ex}" but temp_path is
    only defined in a different method (get_current_war_data). Any exception during JSON write
    immediately raised NameError: name 'temp_path' is not defined, masking the real error and
    always returning False. Fixed to reference target_file (the variable holding the write path).
- Fixed: Pylance errors in QBhelperfunctions.py
  · Added return_exceptions=False to asyncio.gather call at line ~1201 (calculate_win_probability
    concurrent dispatch) so Pylance resolves the overloaded return type for tuple unpacking.
  · Wrapped asyncio.gather for fetch_results in list(...) with return_exceptions=False and
    # type: ignore[assignment] to satisfy the explicit List[Tuple[Any, bool]] annotation.
- Fixed: Discord gateway disconnects during CWL recovery batch writes (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: process_cwl_recovery_batch() called CACHE.save_war_object() synchronously in a loop
    after the parallel API fetch phase. With batch_size=1000 and ~2 writes/war, up to 2000
    server-machine file writes (~10 ms each) ran without yielding → ~20 s event-loop block → Discord
    missed heartbeat → server closed the TCP connection → ClientConnectionResetError on send.
  · Fix: all writes are now dispatched concurrently as asyncio.to_thread() tasks and awaited
    via asyncio.gather(return_exceptions=True), so the event loop stays free throughout I/O.
  · Each _write_one() wraps the call in try/except and returns False on error (logged as WARNING).
  · asyncio.gather uses return_exceptions=True so one failing write never cancels the rest.
  · Side-effect: writes also complete faster (parallel thread-pool I/O vs. sequential).
- Changed: CWL recovery batch size raised from 300 to 1000 per cycle.
  · Now safe with the threaded write fix above; drains the remaining ~200k queue ~3× faster.
- Fixed: nightly DB maintenance trigger pinned to 03:xx UTC window instead of 03:00 exact
  · Removed _now_utc.minute == 0 guard so bot busy at 03:00 still triggers on 03:05, 03:10, etc.
- Changed: nightly DB maintenance fired as asyncio.create_task() background task (QapBot.py)
  · Was: awaited directly — blocked periodic_main() for ~30-40 s; bot unresponsive during VACUUM.
  · Now: create_task() fires immediately after [CYCLE-END]; periodic_main() continues to sleep.
  · CACHE.last_db_maintenance pre-set before task launch so 03:05 cycle does not re-trigger.
  · QBcore.db_maintenance_idle_event (new asyncio.Event) cleared when task starts, set when done.
  · Sleep-end gate: periodic_main() awaits db_maintenance_idle_event before starting next cycle,
    preventing a new update cycle from running while VACUUM holds an exclusive DB lock.
  · Logged: "[CYCLE] Waiting for nightly DB maintenance..." if gate actually blocks.
- Added: QBcore.db_maintenance_idle_event (QBcore.py)
  · asyncio.Event, starts set; cleared while nightly maintenance is in flight, set on completion.
- Added: CWL recovery per-API-call logging at INFO level (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · _fetch() now logs one line per war_tag with HTTP status: e.g. "#ABC123 → 200 OK",
    "#ABC123 → 404 Not Found", "#ABC123 → 503 Maintenance", etc.
  · Removed silent logging.debug() fallback for failed fetches.
- Changed: CWL recovery batch size reduced from 3000 to 300 per cycle.
  · Reduces burst API pressure and keeps individual cycle times manageable.
- Changed: CWL recovery file writeback now happens unconditionally after every batch.
  · Removed _CWL_RECOVERY_PERSIST_EVERY throttle constant (was 500).
  · Removed deprecated shutdown persist blocks from QapBot.py and QBdiscordcmds.py.
  · Removed cwl_recovery_consumed field from cache_manager.py (no longer needed).
- Added: CWL recovery batch summary re-logged just before [CYCLE-END]
  · CACHE.last_cwl_recovery_summary stores the last "Batch done: ..." string.
  · Logged again at end of periodic_main cycle for visibility during sleep interval.
- Changed: CWL recovery clan filter — write for all known clans, not just tracked ones
  · Old: skip clan if not in clan_name_cache OR track_war_updates=False
  · New: skip clan only if not in clan_name_cache at all (unknown to our DB)
  · Rationale: a clan with track_war_updates=False may have a lapsed subscription but
    its historical CWL data is still worth recording.

  · CLI: python qapbot/scripts/filter_cwl_recovery.py [--dry-run]

- Fixed: CWL recovery Phase 1.5 caused per-write SQLite stalls (same pattern as earlier Phase 2/3 fixes)
  · Root cause: process_cwl_recovery_batch() called _process_war_history() per war per clan immediately
    after save_war_object(), triggering an individual COMMIT for each write. With ~6k writes/batch and
    WAL autocheckpoint at 1000 frames, SQLite stalled for 3-5s every ~15-20 writes → 25-minute batch.
  · Fix: Phase 1.5 now only fetches the war from the API and writes the temp JSON (save_war_object
    force_save=True). No _process_war_history() call. The existing Phase 2 orphan scanner picks up the
    temp files (filename timestamps are >25h old → pass the single-file orphan gate) and finalises them
    via war_write_batch() — batched COMMITs, deferred file moves, no stalls.

  · Root cause part 1: PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE) ran synchronously inside _flush_pending_war_writes
    after the last COMMIT, inside war_write_batch() context. No logs, no timeout. For 69 wars = ~13s;
    for thousands, scales linearly and blocks indefinitely.
    Fix: skip_checkpoint=True in war_write_batch(); run_passive_checkpoint() fired as asyncio.create_task
    background task so Phase 3 starts without waiting. Uses asyncio.create_task() (not deprecated
    asyncio.get_event_loop().create_task()).
  · Root cause part 2 (actual 11-minute stall, confirmed by log timestamps): _refresh_finalized() called
    glob.glob(f"data/temp/{tag}_*_war_data.json") for EACH clan in _phase2_finalized. 5225 file moves =
    up to 5225 glob calls. Each call scans the full temp/ directory (tens of thousands of files) on HDD/server-machine.
    5225 full-directory scans = ~11-minute stall with zero log output.
    Fix: replaced with O(n) in-memory dict pops (_temp_files_by_clan.pop(safe_tag, None)) — no filesystem
    I/O. Correct because finalized clan files were moved to archive; Phase 3 gets war_files_prescan=None →
    own glob → empty → instant no-op for those clans.
  · Fix C: Added timing log in _batch_finalize: "[ORPHANED-CWL] Deferred N file move(s) completed in X.Xs"
  · Impact: Phase 2 → Phase 3 transition is now immediate regardless of orphan batch size.
- Fixed: Phase 3 → "File processing completed" gap (9s stall after deferred file moves)
  · Root cause: bulk_update_clan_timestamps aiosqlite commit triggered WAL autocheckpoint
    (threshold=1000 frames). After Phase 3 wrote ~26K attack rows the WAL exceeded 1000 frames;
    the aiosqlite commit fired a synchronous PASSIVE checkpoint on the aiosqlite background thread,
    blocking the entire await for ~9s. The completion was logged at DEBUG level (invisible in prod).
  · Fix: Added PRAGMA wal_autocheckpoint=0 before executemany and restore to 1000 after commit.
    A PASSIVE checkpoint is already running as a background task; no second one needed here.
  · Fix: Promoted completion log from DEBUG to INFO with timing: "[DB-TS-FLUSH] Batch-persisted N
    clan timestamps in X.XXs" — the gap is now visible and measurable in prod logs.
  · Bug fix: PRAGMA wal_autocheckpoint=1000 (restore) was inside try but not in finally — if commit()
    raised (disk full, database locked), the restore was skipped, permanently disabling autocheckpoint
    on the aiosqlite connection for the entire bot session → unbounded WAL growth → disk exhaustion.
    Fixed by wrapping executemany+commit in an inner try/finally so the restore runs unconditionally.
  · Bug fix: _t0 timer was placed after executemany, only measuring commit(). Moved before executemany
    so the logged duration covers the full operation (executemany + commit).
- Fixed: [CRITICAL] save_user fails with "database is locked" during bulk war finalization bursts
  · Root cause: two independent write-lock hierarchies on the same SQLite file.
    Sync bulk writes (_flush_pending_war_writes) hold _sync_write_lock (threading.Lock) for ~10 s per
    50-war batch. Async save_user uses _write_lock (asyncio.Lock) + aiosqlite — the two paths have no
    cross-awareness. SQLite allows only one writer; the async write hits busy_timeout (5 s) and the
    _retry_on_locked window (7.5 s total) while the sync batch (10 s) is still running → all retries
    exhaust → permanent failure.
  · Fix 1 (primary): Added _sync_write_fence() — a sync method that acquire-then-releases
    _sync_write_lock — called via asyncio.to_thread() at the start of _save_user_impl. The async path
    blocks in a thread-pool worker (not the event loop) until the in-progress sync batch finishes, then
    proceeds immediately while the lock is free.
  · Fix 2 (belt-and-suspenders): Raised busy_timeout on the async connection from 5 000ms to 30 000ms
    so SQLite itself waits up to 30 s if a new sync write starts in the tiny window after the fence.
  · Test fix: added _sync_write_lock = threading.Lock() to _create_db() helper in
    test_db_manager_async.py (the helper bypasses __init__ via __new__).
- Fixed: 67k+ single-file CWL temp files (untracked/discovered clans from cwl_clan_discovery.py) stuck
  as `in_war` indefinitely due to orphan scanner skipping all single-file clans by design.
  · Root cause: `_scan_orphans` had `if len(file_paths) <= 1: continue` — designed for tracked clans
    that get a second file when next war starts. Discovered clans never get a second file so the orphan
    signal never fires.
  · Fix: replaced the hard skip with an `end_time + 1h` check gated by a 25h filename-timestamp
    pre-filter (zero file I/O for wars < 25h old). Added `_SINGLE_FILE_ORPHAN_CAP = 5000` to rate-limit
    processing to 5000 orphans/cycle, clearing ~60k backlog in ~1h without flooding.
  · Also fixed `fetch_and_update_orphan` for postponed wars: when API still shows `in_war`, rewrites
    full JSON via `save_war_object` so `end_time` stays current across maintenance postponements.
  · Fixed misleading log message: "archive had stale data; will reprocess via ARCHIVE-DIFFERS path"
    → "initial save was skipped (war ended >24h ago, stale-war guard); archive created, finalizing".
  · Impact: prod processed 28,505+ orphaned CWL wars in first cycle post-deploy; remaining backlog
    clearing at ~5000/cycle.

- Fixed: cwlinfo/cwlinfo_comp embeds failing with Discord 400 "Must be 4096 or fewer in length" for
  clans with long CWL seasons (many prediction lines, long lineup strings, long clan names, etc.).
  · Root cause: both `generate_cwlinfo_embeds` and `_generate_cwlinfo_archive_embeds`
    (QBhelperfunctions.py) joined all 7 rounds into a single `discord.Embed` with no length guard.
    Discord's embed description limit is 4096 characters; comp-mode seasons with full prediction
    tables easily exceed this.
  · Fix: added `_EMBED_DESC_LIMIT = 4096` and a new helper `_desc_lines_to_embeds()` that groups
    desc_lines into round-level blocks (split on blank "" separators) and packs as many complete
    rounds as fit per embed (≤ 4096 chars), starting a new embed for the next round when adding it
    would overflow. Hard-truncation is only used as a last resort if a single round block is itself
    > 4096 chars. The first embed carries the clan title/URL; continuation embeds have empty title
    so the series reads as one contiguous post. Both embed-creation sites now call this helper.

- Fixed: cwlinfo/cwlinfo_comp showing ~16% more Draw and ~16% less Win than currentwar for identical war state.
  · Root cause: `_build_cwl_war_proxy` (QBhelperfunctions.py) did not include `'destruction'` in the
    returned `clan_side` / `opp_side` dicts.
  · `_monte_carlo_chunk` (QBwarsim.py) reads `war_data['clan']['destruction']` and
    `war_data['opponent']['destruction']` as a tiebreaker whenever two simulated war runs end with equal
    final star totals. Without those keys, both sides default to 0.0 → every equal-star simulation
    resolves as Draw instead of Win/Lose.
  · The `currentwar` path loads a real JSON temp file that contains true destruction percentages, so its
    equal-star runs correctly resolve to Win (when our destruction > theirs). The cwlinfo/cwlinfo_comp
    paths use the proxy dict and were missing destruction → all equal-star runs → Draw.
  · Fix: added `my_destruction: float = 0.0` / `opp_destruction: float = 0.0` keyword params to
    `_build_cwl_war_proxy`; populated `'destruction'` in both side dicts. Updated the `inwar` call site
    in `generate_cwlinfo_embeds` to pass `my_dest_r` / `opp_dest_r` (already computed, just not forwarded).
    Preparation/notinwar call sites correctly default to 0.0 (no attacks = 0% destruction).

- Investigated: 492 "History missing" CWL wars reported by verify_all_cwl_history.py
  · Root cause (verify script false positive): History query used `WHERE attack_order > 0`,
    which excludes sentinel rows (attack_order = 0) written for CWL members who made 0 attacks
    in a round. These wars ARE correctly recorded in DB (all 15 sentinel rows present);
    the query just found nothing and falsely reported "History missing".
  · Confirmed: all 492 archive files have state=war_ended, our_attacks=0, opp_attacks>0 — the
    tracked clans genuinely did not attack in those CWL rounds (valid participation with 0 attacks).
  · Fix 1 — verify_all_cwl_history.py: removed `AND attack_order > 0` from the history query
    so sentinel-only wars are now correctly matched and not flagged as missing.
  · Fix 2 — QBcsvhandling.py: defensive fix for write-ordering inversion in
    _append_current_war_to_history(): file move to archive/ used immediate os.rename()/os.replace()
    while DB write was queued via deferred batch (add_war_data_sync → _tls.war_batch_appends).
    Fixed both the "new archive" and "late-attacks replace archive" branches to call
    CACHE.db_manager.defer_file_move() when is_war_batch_active() is True, so file moves are
    deferred until after the batch DB writes commit (matching the guarantee in defer_file_move).
    This prevents future data loss if the bot restarts between a file move and a batch flush.

  · All 1305 tests pass.

- Fixed: /status command (and any command using send_and_track) failed with "database is locked"
  during heavy Phase-3 CWL finalization (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: Phase-3 sync batch writes hold the SQLite WAL writer lock for several seconds
    (up to one batch of ~50 wars). At 20:19:48, user ran /status which called send_and_track →
    set_leaderboard_message → save_leaderboard_message. The aiosqlite connection's
    busy_timeout=5000ms expired because the sync batch held the lock for >5s →
    sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked → command failed with unhandled exception.
  · save_user, save_guild_config, save_subscriptions_for_channel, save_clan_family all already
    had _retry_on_locked applied (from 2026-04-04 fix). save_leaderboard_message and
    delete_leaderboard_message were missed.
  · Fix: Applied _retry_on_locked pattern to both save_leaderboard_message and
    delete_leaderboard_message. Extracted _save_leaderboard_message_impl and
    _delete_leaderboard_message_impl as inner implementations (same pattern as save_clan_family).
    On "database is locked": retries up to 4 times (0.5→1→2→4s backoff, ~7.5s total).
  · No DB corruption risk was present: WAL mode prevents corruption; the error was a clean
    lock-contention timeout with no partial writes attempted.

- Performance: Temp war file loading rewritten to avoid event-loop blocking at startup (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  · Root cause: load_all_temp_war_stats() was called synchronously from async load_all(). During
    CWL season the temp/ directory holds 76k+ JSON files (one per tracked clan). Reading all of
    them via ThreadPoolExecutor(16) still blocked the Discord heartbeat connection because the
    entire call ran on the event loop thread, causing Discord to disconnect mid-startup.
  · Fix 1 — off-thread execution: load_all() now calls
    `await asyncio.to_thread(self.load_all_temp_war_stats)` so all disk I/O happens on a worker
    thread and the event loop stays free during the 60-95s load.
  · Fix 2 — two-phase design eliminates almost all file reads:
    Phase 1: Scans directory once, derives state/start_time/end_time from the filename timestamp
             (YYYYMMDDHHMM = battle start; end = start + 24 h). Zero JSON reads for 76k files.
             Time strings are synthesised in CoC Timestamp format for compatibility with
             _parse_dt_from_raw() and _get_hours_until_war_end().
    Phase 2: Full JSON parse only for subscribed clans (has_active_subscriptions=True) + in_war
             clans with end_time within 6 h (already in the notification window). Typically
             < 50 files read at startup vs 76k before.
  · Added _STATS_LOAD_LOOKAHEAD_HOURS = 6 module-level constant (cache_manager.py).
  · All other clans get temp_war_stats = {} at startup; Phase-3 repopulates them (~5-10 min).
  · Latest file per clan is kept (lexicographic ts comparison) — correct for CWL multi-round.
  · All 1305 tests pass unchanged.

2026-04-05
- Fixed: WARNING "[DISCORD-WS] Gateway disconnected" logged on clean intentional shutdown
  · Root cause: on_disconnect() unconditionally logged at WARNING level. bot.close()
    intentionally closes the WebSocket gateway, which triggers on_disconnect. There is
    no bug — the disconnect is expected — but the WARNING created confusing log noise.
    Affected both CTRL+C (signal) shutdown and /admin Maintenance Start shutdown.
  · Fix: check QBcore.shutdown_initiated in on_disconnect(); if True, log at INFO with
    "(intentional shutdown)" suffix and return; otherwise keep WARNING for real disconnects.
    For the maintenance path, do_maintenance_shutdown() now sets QBcore.shutdown_initiated
    = True at the top before closing any resources, so the flag is always set before
    bot.close() fires the WebSocket disconnect event.
  · Files changed: QapBot.py (on_disconnect), QBdiscordcmds.py (do_maintenance_shutdown)

- Fixed: WARNING "Could not remove sidecar file" on Windows CTRL+C shutdown
  · Root cause: A Windows-specific timing issue exposed by the previous aiosqlite fix.
    Before: _close_bot_after_signal() raced with async_cleanup() → event loop closed
    before WAL removal was even attempted. After: cleanup runs cleanly to completion,
    but sqlite3.Connection.close() executes via the aiosqlite background thread. On
    Windows, the .db-shm file uses CreateFileMapping (memory-mapped shared memory); the
    OS may not release the mapping within the ~9ms between close() and os.remove().
    On Linux/server-machine (production), unlink() succeeds regardless — this only affects Windows.
  · Fix: retry os.remove() up to 3 times with 25ms sleep between attempts. If all 3
    fail (extremely unlikely), log at DEBUG instead of WARNING — the file is harmless
    and SQLite recreates it on next open.
  · File changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (close() Step 4)

- Fixed: RuntimeError "Event loop is closed" in aiosqlite background thread on CTRL+C
  · Root cause: _close_bot_after_signal() called bot.close() after only ONE asyncio
    tick (asyncio.sleep(0)). This caused discord.py's asyncio.run(runner()) to see its
    coroutine complete and start cleanup — cancelling all remaining tasks including
    periodic_main mid-way through async_cleanup(). The aiosqlite background thread
    (still alive because db_manager.close() was never reached) then tried to deliver
    a result via call_soon_threadsafe() and found the event loop already closed.
  · Fix: _close_bot_after_signal() now polls QBcore.cleaned_up (set at the END of
    async_cleanup() by periodic_main) in 0.1s steps for up to 60 seconds. It only
    calls bot.close() directly as a fallback if periodic_main hasn't handled cleanup
    within 60 s (e.g. very early startup or stuck mid-cycle).
  · Normal shutdown-during-sleep path: periodic_main wakes from shutdown_event → runs
    async_cleanup() (DB close etc.) → calls bot.close(). _close_bot_after_signal sees
    cleaned_up=True and returns. No race, no crash.
  · File changed: QapBot.py (_close_bot_after_signal)

- Enhanced: Memory profiling improvements for differential leak detection
  · Bug fix: _get_process_memory_mb() was using resource.ru_maxrss (Linux peak/high-water-mark,
    never decreases) → replaced with /proc/self/status VmRSS (current live RSS) as primary
    source, with psutil and resource as fallbacks
  · Added: Baseline snapshot stored in QBcore.memtrace_baseline (+ timestamp in
    QBcore._memtrace_baseline_time) at the moment tracemalloc.start() is called in
    handle_memory_profile(). Cleared after snapshot is saved.
  · Added: [GROWTH vs BASELINE] section in save_memtrace_snapshot() using
    snapshot.compare_to(baseline, "lineno"), showing only sites where memory grew
    (size_diff > 0), sorted by size increase. Shows "no growth detected" if stable.
  · Added: Bot uptime field in header ("uptime=Xh Ym Zs"), derived from
    QBcore.bot_start_time (Unix float set in on_ready Step 4).
  · These changes enable startup-vs-later differential profiling: run /admin Memory Profile
    shortly after startup, then again hours/days later — the GROWTH section pinpoints
    exactly what accumulated over that interval.
  · Files changed: QBcore.py (memtrace_baseline, _memtrace_baseline_time, bot_start_time),
    QapBot.py (set bot_start_time in on_ready, import time),
    qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py (_get_process_memory_mb fix,
    save_memtrace_snapshot differential section, handle_memory_profile baseline capture)

  · Root cause 1 (2nd disconnect): save_memtrace_snapshot() ran gc.collect(),

  · Fix 3: Moved gc.collect(0) + clear_expired() to asyncio.to_thread() in periodic_main
  · Fix 4: Changed _send_channel_war_notification to use get_channel() (cache) before

  · Fix 5: Added on_disconnect/on_resumed event handlers for clear log visibility
  · Fix 6: Added connection-reset exception handling (ConnectionClosed,

2026-04-05
- Fixed: WAL/SHM sidecar files not removed after maintenance mode (db_manager.py) — 2nd attempt
  · Root cause: previous fix used PRAGMA journal_mode=DELETE which requires SQLite to acquire
    an EXCLUSIVE lock on the DB file. Any connection still open (tracked or not) causes
    SQLITE_BUSY → "database is locked". Fragile on server-machine shared filesystems.
  · Fix: replaced journal_mode=DELETE approach with direct os.remove() after full closure:
    1. Close all registered sync connections (existing behaviour)
    2. PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL) — merge all committed WAL frames into main DB file
    3. commit() + close the async aiosqlite connection
    4. os.remove() the .db-wal and .db-shm files directly — no exclusive lock needed once
       all connections are gone; files are just orphaned bytes at that point
  · FULL checkpoint (not TRUNCATE) ensures all committed data is in main DB before deletion
  · next initialize() call re-enables WAL mode with a fresh pair of sidecar files as usual

2026-04-05
- Fixed: /admin Maintenance Start closed DB mid-cycle causing [DB-MAINT] errors (QBdiscordcmds.py)
  · Root cause: used asyncio.wait_for(cycle_idle_event.wait(), timeout=60s) — cycle ran 151.7s
    so the timeout fired first and resources were closed while the cycle was still active
  · Fix: replaced timeout-based wait with a flag (QBcore.maintenance_pending); when a cycle
    is active the handler sets the flag and returns immediately; periodic_main() checks the
    flag at natural cycle end and calls do_maintenance_shutdown() before sleeping
  · Resource-closing logic extracted to do_maintenance_shutdown() (QBdiscordcmds.py) so it
    can be called from both the handler (no active cycle) and periodic_main (deferred path)

- Fixed: WAL/SHM sidecar files not removed after maintenance mode shutdown (db_manager.py)
  · Root cause: SQLite does not auto-delete -wal/-shm on Linux/server-machine even with 0-byte WAL;
    also persistent thread-local sync connections held locks preventing cleanup
  · Fix: close() now (1) closes all thread-local sync conns first, (2) PRAGMA
    wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE), (3) PRAGMA journal_mode=DELETE — which is the only
    guaranteed way to make SQLite physically delete the sidecar files, (4) closes async conn
  · On next startup PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL re-enables WAL mode as normal

2026-04-04
- Performance P0+P1: Batched war DB writes + WAL checkpoint suppression
  - Root cause: PROD log (2026-04-04, 4 cycles) showed Phase-3 DB writes with P50=20ms but
    P95=5-8s. Pattern: ~12 fast writes then ~5-8s stall caused by HDD writeback flush (Linux
    dirty_writeback_centisecs 5s). 691 orphan writes took 436s; 634 Phase-3 writes took 318s.
    95-96% of Phase-3 time was spent in pauses waiting for HDD.
  - P0 — Batch collect + single transaction: add_war_data_sync() and update_war_data_sync()
    now check is_war_batch_active() and collect (clan_tag, attack_rows, summary) tuples into
    thread-local lists instead of writing immediately. On batch exit, _flush_pending_war_writes()
    writes all data in batched transactions (50 wars per COMMIT via executemany). Reduces ~691
    individual commits to ~14 batched commits.
  - P1 — WAL auto-checkpoint suppression: Each batch sets PRAGMA wal_autocheckpoint=0 before
    writing, restores to 1000 after. After all batches: PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE) merges
    WAL pages into main DB without blocking readers.
  - Deferred file moves: _archive_and_log_war_file() uses defer_file_move() instead of direct
    os.replace(). File moves happen AFTER bulk DB write succeeds, ensuring consistency.
  - Orphan finalization: process_orphaned_cwl_wars() changed from Semaphore(3) parallel
    manage_war_files() to single-thread sequential inside war_write_batch(). All DB writes
    batched, all file moves deferred.
  - Phase-3: Each asyncio.to_thread(process_clan_war_data) call wrapped with
    activate_war_batch_on_thread() pointing to shared lists. After loop: flush_pending_war_writes()
    then deferred os.replace() moves.
  - New public API: war_write_batch() context manager, activate_war_batch_on_thread(),
    deactivate_war_batch_on_thread(), flush_pending_war_writes(), defer_file_move(),
    is_war_batch_active()
  - Expected: Phase-3 from ~300s to ~20-30s (10-15x). Orphan finalization from ~436s to ~30s.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py, QapBot.py

- Reliability: User-facing DB operations survive concurrent finalization bursts
  - Root cause: PROD log showed sqlite3.OperationalError "database is locked" in save_user()
    called from UnlinkConfirmView button callback during heavy Phase-3 finalization. The async
    connection's busy_timeout=5000ms was exceeded because sync finalization threads held the
    WAL writer lock during rapid sequential writes.
  - Fix 1 — _retry_on_locked() helper: async retry wrapper with exponential backoff
    (0.5→1→2→4s, 4 retries, ~7.5s total). Catches sqlite3.OperationalError containing
    "database is locked" and re-creates the coroutine on each attempt. Typed:
    Callable[[], Awaitable[None]].
  - Fix 2 — Wrapped 4 user-facing async DB methods with retry:
    save_user() → _save_user_impl() (unlink/link player, profile edits)
    save_guild_config() → _save_guild_config_impl() (clan/family setup)
    save_subscriptions_for_channel() → _save_subscriptions_for_channel_impl() (subscription UI)
    save_clan_family() → _save_clan_family_impl() (family management)
    Each _impl has _write_lock.acquire() / finally: _write_lock.release() so on error
    the lock is released and rollback() called before retry gets a clean state.
  - Not wrapped (lower priority — periodic/background, not user-triggered):
    save_all_subscriptions(), delete_notification_state_for_war()
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (_retry_on_locked, 4 wrapper/impl splits, typing import)

- Performance: Persistent thread-local sync connections (eliminate HDD writeback stalls)
  - Root cause: PROD log showed 409 wars finalized in 220s, but 210s of that was pauses (>500ms).
    Pattern: ~13 fast writes (19ms apart) then ~6s pause. Correlated with Linux
    dirty_writeback_centisecs (5s default). sync_batch() opened a NEW sqlite3.connect() per clan.
    During HDD writeback flushes, new connect() must read DB header pages → blocks behind
    sequential writes already queued by the kernel.
  - Fix 1 — _get_sync_conn() persistently caches connections in threading.local() storage.
    Open once per thread, reuse forever. Eliminates ~1,500+ connect/close cycles per Phase-3.
  - Fix 2 — sync_batch() updated to use persistent cache instead of creating/closing connections.
  - Fix 3 — Removed sync_batch() wrapper from manage_war_files() in QBhelperfunctions.py
    (no longer needed since connections are persistent).
  - Expected impact: Phase-3 220s → ~12-20s (eliminates 6s HDD stall between each clan batch).
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (_get_sync_conn, sync_batch), QBhelperfunctions.py
    (manage_war_files)

- Performance: synchronous=NORMAL on sync connections (eliminate 300ms fsync per commit)
  - Root cause: Async connection had PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL but sync connections (used by
    Phase-3 finalization via asyncio.to_thread) were missing it. Default synchronous=FULL
    causes an fsync system call on every COMMIT — ~300ms per write on HDD.
  - Fix: Added synchronous=NORMAL to _apply_sync_pragmas() (called on every sync connection).
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (_apply_sync_pragmas)

- Fix: deferred_commit=True caused "database is locked" errors (39 errors in PROD)
  - Root cause: sync_batch(deferred_commit=True) held the WAL writer lock for entire batch
    duration (all writes for one clan in a single transaction). Other threads' busy_timeout
    expired waiting for the held lock.
  - Fix: Changed deferred_commit default to False. Each per-write commit is sub-ms with
    synchronous=NORMAL, and WAL lock is released between writes.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py (sync_batch default parameter)

2026-04-03
- Fixed: CWL orphaned war infinite loop + stale archive overwrite (0-attack data in DB)
  - Root cause: `fetch_and_update_orphan()` in `process_orphaned_cwl_wars()` ignored the
    return value of `save_war_object()`. When the war was already in both archive and history
    (FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP → returns False), the function still logged "Updated" (falsely) and
    returned (True, ...). The temp file was never actually overwritten, so `manage_war_files`
    kept seeing `state=in_war` and firing `CWL-ORPHAN-SKIP` every cycle — infinite loop.
    Additionally, if the archive had been written with stale/incomplete data (e.g. state=
    preparation with 0 attacks from a premature archive), the DB history also had 0 attacks for
    all players and was never corrected.
    Fix: capture `_saved = save_war_object(...)`. When `_saved` is False, call
    `save_war_object(..., force_save=True)` to bypass the finalized check and write the correct
    war_ended+attacks data to temp. `manage_war_files` then detects `[ARCHIVE-DIFFERS]`,
    replaces the stale archive JSON with correct data, and re-processes DB history.
    Both the infinite loop and the 0-attack archive/DB are corrected automatically on next cycle.
  - Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (fetch_and_update_orphan inside process_orphaned_cwl_wars)
- Fixed: CWL war data not retrieved for clans with private war log
  - Root cause 1 (PrivateWarLog exception): `GET /clans/{tag}/currentwar` raises 403.
    Both handlers caught it and returned None immediately.
    Fix: CWL league group fallback in the PrivateWarLog/NotFound except block.
  - Root cause 2 (API returns old war_ended instead of 403): When warlog is flipped to private
    during an active CWL war, the API can return the last ended regular war instead of raising
    PrivateWarLog. save_war_object() fires [FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP] and returns False, but that
    return value was silently ignored, so the active CWL war was never fetched.
    Fix: check save_war_object() return value; when False + state==war_ended, try CWL league
    group fallback (newest-first round iteration via get_league_war) to find an active CWL war.
    If found: replace coc_war_obj with the CWL war, re-derive metadata, save the new file,
    and mark warlog_is_public=False in cache. Both fixes use get_league_group + get_league_war
    which bypass warlog privacy entirely.
  - Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (fetch_clan_war_data)
- Performance: /admin Check Data — eliminated remaining ~25s slowdown (integrity_check + archive scan)
  - Root cause 3 (11s PRAGMA integrity_check): Full 584MB DB page scan ran unconditionally on every
    /admin Check Data invocation.  Rarely needed interactively.
    Fix: added include_integrity: bool = False param to check_database_consistency().  Default
    skips the PRAGMA; callers can opt-in with include_integrity=True for explicit health runs.
  - Root cause 4 (13.8s archive scan): check_database_consistency() read each of 32,653 archive JSON
    files via ThreadPoolExecutor(16) to extract clan_tag and war_id.  1000× slower than necessary.
    Fix: replaced _read_archive_file() + ThreadPoolExecutor with a simple os.listdir() loop that
    derives (clan_tag, war_id) purely from the filename:
      CLANTAG_OPPONENTTAG_YYYYMMDDHHMM_war_data.json  →  war_id = OPPONENTTAG_YYYYMMDDHHMM
    Verified 100% match on 10 samples.  Scan time: 13.79s → 0.014s (1000× faster).
    concurrent.futures import and _CORRUPT sentinel removed.
  - Combined result: /admin Check Data now completes in < 5s instead of ~1 minute.
  - Test updates: test_integrity_failed, test_db_query_exception (phase5 + phase9) pass
    include_integrity=True so integrity-path coverage is preserved.

- Performance+Fix: /admin Check Data — eliminated 2-minute block and Discord reconnect
  - Root cause 1 (event loop blocked): check_database_consistency() is a sync function
    called directly on the asyncio event loop with no offloading.  Blocked the loop for
    ~2 minutes → Discord heartbeats missed → bot reconnected mid-command.
    Fix: wrapped call with `await asyncio.to_thread(check_database_consistency, ...)` in
    QBdiscordcmds.py so the event loop stays free during the entire validation run.

- Investigation: DEV Phase-1 (123s) vs PROD Phase-1 (44s) — DEV-only API throttle
  - Symptom: 178 COC-API-SLOW calls (1574.7s aggregate) on DEV, 0 on PROD.
    Same code, same concurrency (Semaphore 20), same coc.py config.
  - Hypothesis: Yesterday's performance optimisations sped up request issuing rate,
    triggering silent rate limiting by CoC API servers on the faster DEV machine.
  - Fix: Added _DEV_API_THROTTLE_S (20ms) in coc_health.py coc_retry().
    When CONFIG.is_dev_mode is True, acquires a global asyncio.Lock and sleeps
    _DEV_API_THROTTLE_S before each API call, serialising all concurrent tasks
    to enforce max ~50 req/s globally.  Set to 0.0 to disable.
    Initial per-task sleep was ineffective: 20 concurrent tasks sleeping
    simultaneously doesn't reduce aggregate throughput.
    Will tune the value down once the hypothesis is confirmed.
  - Tuning results (confirmed rate limit threshold ~45–50 req/s on DEV credentials):
    * 0ms  → 178 slow calls, 1574.7s slow time, 123s cycle (rate limited badly)
    * 15ms → pauses returned (>50 req/s still hits limit)
    * 20ms → 20 slow calls (natural jitter), 44.3s slow time, 94s cycle  
    * 22ms → sweet spot: ~45 req/s, pauses gone, cycle ~80-85s  ← FINAL
    * 30ms → same 20 slow calls but 59.8s slow time, 126.5s cycle (too slow)
  - Final value: _DEV_API_THROTTLE_S = 0.022 (22ms) in coc_health.py
  - Test fix: Updated test_health_coverage.py to filter DEV-throttle sleeps when
    asserting on backoff/rate-limit sleep calls.

- Performance: War simulation (Monte Carlo) — 3 algorithmic optimisations
  - Root cause 1 (O(n_members) setup per simulation): simulate_attacks_dynamic rebuilt
    base_stars, base_th_map, attacker_info, attack_queue, tag_to_name from scratch on every
    single call. With 1000 sims × 2 clans = 2000 full dict/set reconstructions per war,
    all from identical war_data that never changes between simulations.
    Fix: added _precompute_sim_state() — called ONCE per chunk; extracts all static arrays
    (initial_base_stars, bitmask per attacker, attack order, probability table) so the hot
    inner loop only copies two small lists per simulation.
  - Root cause 2 (th_star_probabilities in the hot loop): called for every attack of every
    simulation, i.e. 1000 sims × 30 attacks = 30,000 calls per war. Each (atk_th, def_th,
    league, skill) combo is fixed for the duration of a war — should be pre-computed once.
    Fix: _precompute_sim_state builds a prob_table[atk_i][base_j] of cumulative 3-tuples
    (cum0, cum01, cum012) so _run_sim_prebuilt never calls th_star_probabilities.
  - Root cause 3 (random.choices overhead): random.choices([0,1,2,3], weights=probs, k=1)[0]
    allocates a list and runs Python's internal alias-table machinery for every single attack.
    Fix: _run_sim_prebuilt uses random.random() + direct float comparison against the
    pre-computed cumulative thresholds — zero list allocations, simple branch logic.
  - Root cause 4 (process pool spawn on server-machine — dominant wall-time issue): _sim_min_chunk=100
    activated the spawn process pool for n_sim=1000 (1000 ≥ 4×100=400). Each pool creation
    on the prod server takes 500ms–1s. With 19 wars per cycle each spawning a pool, that
    was 10–20 seconds of pure OS overhead per cycle before any computation began.
    Fix: raised _sim_min_chunk from 100 to 2000. n_sim=1000 now always runs in-process
    (1000 < 4×2000=8000). Process pool only activates for explicit large batch requests.
  - Result: 1000-sim run on dev machine: ~48 ms in-process (vs previous approach that would
    spawn a 4-worker pool). Estimated server-machine: ~150 ms per war × 19 wars = ~3 s total for
    leaderboard sim phase (was ~10–40 s with spawn overhead).
  - Files: QBwarsim.py
- Fixed: Pylance errors in QBwarsim.py introduced by Monte Carlo optimisation
  - `offset` variable in _monte_carlo_chunk unpacking was no longer used after removing
    the per-simulation sim_number tracking → renamed to `_offset`.
  - `mask` type annotation was unresolved because `prebuilt` is `Dict[str, Any]` and
    Pylance couldn't propagate a concrete type through the subscript → added
    `type: ignore[index]` on the specific assignment; also tightened `list()` calls
    and explicit `int()` cast on `n_bases` so surrounding variables have concrete types.
  - File: QBwarsim.py
- Performance: War notification check — 3 algorithmic optimizations targeting 6.94s gap
  - Root cause 1 (stale seconds_until): _get_hours_until_war_end() read the seconds_until field
    baked into the serialized coc.Timestamp string. That value is computed once at write time and
    never decreases — so wars archived hours ago could still appear "active" in memory (false
    positives). Fix: parse the actual UTC datetime via regex and compute
    (end_dt - datetime.utcnow()).total_seconds(). seconds_until kept as fallback-only when no
    datetime component exists in the string.
  - Root cause 2 (O(18917) full scan): _get_active_wars() iterated all 18,917 temp_war_metadata
    entries per notification cycle even though at most ~19 clans are ever truly in_war at once.
    Fix: added CACHE.in_war_clan_tags: Set[str] — a small pre-filtered set maintained by
    load_all_temp_war_stats(), set_temp_war_stats(), and save_war_object(). _get_active_wars()
    now iterates only this set. O(n_in_war) instead of O(n_all_temp). Includes self-heal: stale
    set entries (state != in_war in metadata) are discarded on first encounter.
  - Root cause 3 (redundant Timestamp re-parsing): _get_hours_until_war_end() was called 4× per
    active war — once in _get_active_wars(), then again in 3 downstream functions. Fix: store
    computed hours_remaining in war_data["hours_remaining"] in _get_active_wars(); downstream
    functions read war_data.get("hours_remaining") with a fallback call if absent.
  - Tests updated: TestGetHoursUntilWarEnd (coverage + deep), test_get_active_wars_filters_by_state_and_time (phase4), _make_cm factory (deep).
  - Files: qapbot/war_notifications.py, qapbot/cache_manager.py,
