    tests/unit/test_war_notifications_coverage.py

  - Root cause: _get_active_wars() in war_notifications.py called glob.glob("*_war_data.json") over
    all 18k temp files every cycle. On server-machine (SMB/NFS), the directory listing itself took ~75s even
    though the metadata pre-filter prevented most file reads. Confirmed in prod log:
    [PHASE-3] File processing completed → [ROLE-SYNC] Bootstrapping: 75s gap.
  - Fix:
    1. Added "filepath" key to temp_war_metadata in load_all_temp_war_stats() (startup backfill).
    2. Added filepath update to save_war_object() after each successful JSON write (kept current).
    3. Rewrote _get_active_wars() to iterate temp_war_metadata directly and open stored
       filepaths — eliminates glob.glob() entirely. O(in_war_clans) instead of O(all_temp_files).
  - Files: qapbot/cache_manager.py, qapbot/war_notifications.py
- Fixed: CoC API slow calls were invisible in logs (logged at DEBUG, never visible at INFO level)
  - Symptom: Periodic update cycle pauses had no log evidence, making root cause diagnosis impossible.
  - Fix: Promoted [COC-API-SLOW] threshold logging in coc_health.py coc_retry():
    - >= 2s: INFO  (visible in normal log output)
    - >= 5s: WARNING (stands out for significant stalls)
  - File: qapbot/coc_health.py
- Changed: /admin Check Data — duplicate war detection window reduced from 7 days to 48 hours
  - Root cause analysis: both previously flagged duplicate pairs and both missing JSON archives were
    direct artifacts of the old temp file naming convention (no timestamp), which caused a newer war
    against the same opponent to overwrite the temp file of the still-running older war.
  - Rationale: Wars max 24h battle + ~2h prep gap; true data-integrity duplicates (API jitter, race
    conditions) are always minutes/hours apart, never days. 7-day window was too broad.
  - Precision fix: changed from `timedelta.days <= 7` (truncates to whole days, up to 167h) to
    `total_seconds() / 3600 <= 48` (exact 48-hour window) to avoid edge cases around day boundaries.
  - UI label updated from "within 7 days" to "within 48 hours" in the Discord embed.
  - File: qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py

- Performance: Startup — eliminated double file read in load_all_temp_war_stats()
  - Root cause: _load_one() opened each temp war JSON file twice — once via
    _load_war_data_from_json() (full parse) and once explicitly to extract
    lightweight metadata (state, start_time, end_time). With 18,210 temp files
    at startup this meant ~36,420 file opens. Dev log: 7.2 seconds blocked
    (12:40:25.036 → 12:40:32.272).
  - Fix: Added preloaded_raw_data: Optional[dict] = None parameter to
    _load_war_data_from_json(). _load_one() now reads the file exactly once,
    extracts metadata inline, then passes the preloaded dict to the parser —
    skipping the second open/json.load. Expected ~50% reduction in startup I/O
    (18,210 opens instead of 36,420). No behaviour change; all 1303 tests pass.
  - Files changed: QBcsvhandling.py (_load_war_data_from_json signature + file-open
    block), qapbot/cache_manager.py (_load_one inner function).

- Performance: War notification check — eliminated 75-second event loop blockage (3 fixes)
  - Root cause: _get_active_wars() in war_notifications.py performed synchronous
    glob + json.load on all ~18,124 temp war JSON files every cycle. The 72+ seconds
    of blocking I/O starved the asyncio event loop, causing Discord interactions
    (commands, buttons, modals) to expire before the bot could respond.
    Prod evidence: /admin CHECK_LOGS issued at ~11:41:30, event loop freed at 11:41:32
    after 75s blackout, "Interaction expired" warnings at 11:41:33 (3s timeout exceeded).
  - Fix 1 — Metadata pre-filter: _get_active_wars() now checks CACHE.temp_war_metadata
    (already populated at startup and kept current by set_temp_war_stats) to identify
    in_war clans (~95) before touching the filesystem. Only matching files are opened.
    Eliminates ~18,000 unnecessary json.load() calls per cycle. 75s → <1s.
  - Fix 2 — asyncio.to_thread(): the _get_active_wars() call is wrapped in
    asyncio.to_thread() so remaining synchronous file I/O runs in a thread pool
    instead of blocking the event loop. Defense-in-depth for future regressions.
  - Fix 3 — Leaderboard phase yield: added await asyncio.sleep(0) per subscription
    iteration inside _process_channel() so the event loop can process Discord
    interactions between CPU-bound leaderboard/skill calculations.
  - Test updated: test_get_active_wars_filters_by_state_and_time now populates
    CACHE.temp_war_metadata and uses realistic filenames matching the new pre-filter.
  - Files changed: qapbot/war_notifications.py (_get_active_wars, check_wars_for_notifications),
    QapBot.py (_process_channel yield), tests/unit/test_war_notifications_phase4.py

- Fixed: War temp/archive file naming collision when two clans fight each other more than once
  - Root cause: filename {CLAN}_{OPP}_war_data.json overwrote earlier war files for the same
    opponent pair, causing data loss. Became frequent with 40K+ clans in the database.
  - New naming convention: {CLAN}_{OPP}_{YYYYMMDDHHMM}_war_data.json
    where YYYYMMDDHHMM is the war start_time (12-digit timestamp).
  - Changes:
    - cache_manager.py save_war_object(): extracts start_time timestamp at save time;
      uses it in temp_file_name and the FINALIZED-WAR-SKIP archive check.
    - QBhelperfunctions.py manage_war_files(): updated split('_', 1) → split('_', 2) so
      opponent tag (parts[1]) is extracted correctly from new 3-segment stem.
    - QBhelperfunctions.py _archive_and_log_war_file(): notification_state cleanup key is
      now derived from just the first two segments (CLAN_OPP) to match _get_war_id() format.
    - QBhelperfunctions.py backfill_last_cwl_for_clan() and backfill_cwl_from_war_tags():
      derive war timestamp from war_obj.start_time to build matching temp_json_path.
    - qapbot/scripts/recover_cwl_war.py: builds filenames with timestamp.
  - Cleanup: removed the one-time rename utility after the prod migration completed.
    The repo now only documents and uses the timestamped war-file convention.
  - Tests: all 1303 tests pass.

2026-04-02 (date)
- Performance: Phase-3 / cycle structure overhaul (5 fixes for 8-hour cycle regression)
  - Root cause analysis: After a restart, all ~21K inactive clans had their 22h timer expire
    simultaneously (thundering herd). This caused a single cycle to process 21,058 clans:
    Phase-1 API fetches took 135 min (20 concurrent), Phase-3 sequential loop ran 8+ hours.
    Additionally, the prescan dict returning None caused 17K+ fallback glob.glob() calls and
    17K+ unnecessary manage_war_files() invocations for clans with no temp files.
  - Fix 1 — Inactive clan cap (QapBot.py): Limit inactive clans per cycle to 3,000.
    Stalest clans (longest since last_war_update) get priority via sort. Deferred clans
    are picked up in subsequent cycles. Prevents runaway thundering-herd cycles.
  - Fix 2 — Prescan default to [] (QapBot.py): Changed .get(safe_tag) → .get(safe_tag, [])
    so manage_war_files() always receives a list, never None. Eliminates 17K+ glob fallbacks.
  - Fix 3 — Skip manage_war_files when no temp files (QapBot.py): Only call manage_war_files()
    on the war_data=None path when the prescan list is non-empty. Eliminates ~17K needless calls.
  - Fix 4 — Early-return fast path in process_clan_war_data (QBhelperfunctions.py): When
    war_files_prescan is empty AND state is not preparation/in_war, clear stale temp stats and
    return immediately. Skips all regex parsing, dict iteration, and manage_war_files() for
    ~90% of clans per cycle.
  - Fix 5 — Increase _FETCH_CONCURRENCY from 20 → 50 (QapBot.py): Improves Phase-1 API
    throughput ~2.5×. For 3K clans this cuts fetch time from ~30 min to ~12 min.
  - Files changed: QapBot.py (cap logic, prescan default, skip guard, concurrency),
    QBhelperfunctions.py (early-return fast path in process_clan_war_data)

- Performance: Disable tracemalloc in PROD mode (server-machine CPU overhead fix)
  - Root cause: tracemalloc.start(25) was called unconditionally at startup, recording
    a 25-frame stack trace for every memory allocation. On the server-machine Celeron J3355 (2 cores,
    2.0 GHz) this added 10-30% CPU overhead, causing Phase-1 to take 37 min (vs ~2 min on
    DEV), cache validation to take 3+ min (vs instant), and post-cycle operations to hang
    for 40+ minutes. The overhead was invisible on DEV due to faster hardware.
  - Fix: Guard tracemalloc.start(25) behind DEV mode check (DISCORD_GUILD_ID > 0).
    The /admin Memory Profile command already handles tracemalloc being off gracefully.
    Updated the warning message accordingly.
  - Files changed: QapBot.py (tracemalloc guard), QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py (message)

- Feature: On-demand memory profiling for PROD (tracemalloc-lite)
  - Problem: tracemalloc.start(25) had to be disabled in PROD due to 10-30% CPU overhead.
    But memory leak diagnosis still needed periodic profiling on the production server-machine.
  - Solution: Two-step on-demand flow via /admin Memory Profile:
    1st call (PROD, tracing off): starts tracemalloc with nframe=1 (~2-3% CPU overhead,
    ~10× cheaper than nframe=25). Sets QBcore.memtrace_pending flag.
    2nd step: periodic_main() auto-takes snapshot after the next cycle completes, saves
    the full report to data/logs/memprofile_<timestamp>.txt, then stops tracing.
    On DEV (tracing always on): immediate snapshot as before.
  - Report includes: Top 50 allocation sites, CACHE dict sizes, GC type counts, RSS/VMS.
  - Refactored into helpers: save_memtrace_snapshot(), _build_cache_summary(),
    _build_gc_type_counts(), _get_process_memory_mb().
  - Files changed: QBcore.py (memtrace_pending flag), QapBot.py (auto-snapshot in
    periodic_main), QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py (refactored handle_memory_profile)

- Bugfix: Discord WebSocket disconnect during Phase-3 + interaction responsiveness
  - Root cause: Phase-3's synchronous for-loop (3,018 clans) blocked the asyncio event
    loop for 20-30 seconds straight. Discord's gateway heartbeat could not be processed,
    causing "Cannot write to closing transport" reconnects. User interactions (slash
    commands, buttons, modals) were also unresponsive during Phase-3.
  - Fix 1: Yield to event loop every 50 clans via `await asyncio.sleep(0)`. Max blocking
    time per batch: ~500ms (50 × ~10ms/clan). Well within Discord's 3s interaction window.
  - Fix 2: Wrap `os.listdir()` on archive dir (27K files) in `asyncio.to_thread()` to
    avoid 100-500ms blocking on HDD during pre-scan.
  - Files changed: QapBot.py (Phase-3 yield interval, archive pre-scan to_thread)

- Bugfix: "Cannot operate on a closed database" errors in Phase-3 finalization
  - Root cause: add_war_attack_records_sync() and add_war_summary_sync() had a stray
    unconditional conn.close() call after the 'if owns_conn: conn.close()' guard in their
    finally blocks. This closed the shared sync connection on the very first DB write,
    causing all subsequent finalizations in the same Phase-3 run to fail.
    The other 3 sync methods (update_war_attack_records_sync, war_attacks_exist_sync,
    delete_notification_state_sync) were already correct.
  - Fix: Removed the unconditional conn.close() from both affected methods' finally blocks.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py

2026-04-01 (date)
- Performance: Phase-3 finalization micro-optimization (3 fixes)
  - Root causes: 
    1. 27,450-file archive dir → os.path.exists() is O(n) per call; ~1,500 calls = major penalty.
    2. ~1,500-2,000 sqlite3.connect()/close() cycles (3-4 per war × 506 finalizations).
    3. Same JSON file read 3× per finalization (in _process_war_history, _append_current_war_to_history x2).
  - Fix 1 — Archive dir pre-scan: build set of archive filenames once before Phase-3 loop
    (set(os.listdir(archive_dir))). Pass archive_set through manage_war_files → process_clan_war_data
    → _process_war_history → _archive_and_log_war_file → _move_to_archive → _append_current_war_to_history.
    Use 'basename in archive_set' (O(1)) instead of os.path.exists() (O(n dir size)).
    Set is mutated on write to stay current for subsequent clans in the same cycle.
  - Fix 2 — Shared sync DB connection: add _shared_sync_conn attribute to DatabaseManager.
    Phase-3 loop opens one sqlite3 connection before iteration and closes after.
    All 5 hot-path sync methods (add_war_attack_records_sync, add_war_summary_sync,
    update_war_attack_records_sync, war_attacks_exist_sync, delete_notification_state_sync)
    use _get_sync_conn() which returns the shared connection when available.
    Eliminates ~1,500+ connect/close cycles per Phase-3 run.
  - Fix 3 — Eliminate triple JSON reads: pass loaded war_obj (dict) from _process_war_history
    to _append_current_war_to_history via new war_obj parameter. Skips _load_war_data_from_json()
    and the second open() for build_per_attack_rows/build_war_summary when war_obj is pre-loaded.
    Saves 2 disk reads per new-war finalization (506 × 2 = 1,012 fewer reads per cycle).
  - Files changed: QapBot.py (archive pre-scan, shared conn open/close),
    QBhelperfunctions.py (archive_set param on manage_war_files, process_clan_war_data,
    _process_war_history, _archive_and_log_war_file, _move_to_archive; war_obj pass-through),
    QBcsvhandling.py (archive_set + war_obj params on _append_current_war_to_history),
    qapbot/db_manager.py (_shared_sync_conn, _get_sync_conn(), sync_batch() context manager,
    updated 5 hot-path sync methods),
    tests/unit/test_phase9_history_and_dbcheck.py (updated mock signature)

2026-04-01 (date)
- Performance: O(n_clans × n_subs) → O(n_clans + n_subs) in update_all_clan_subscription_statuses()
  - Root cause: _calculate_subscription_status() scanned every subscription for every clan (30k+
    clans × N subscriptions = ~60-second startup freeze). Changed clans were then persisted via
    sequential await persist_clan() calls — one DB lock + commit per clan.
  - Fix 1: Build a subscribed_tags set in one pass over subscriptions + clan_families (O(n_subs)),
    then check each clan with `clan_tag in subscribed_tags` (O(1)) rather than calling
    _calculate_subscription_status() per clan.
  - Fix 2: All status changes are persisted in a single SQL executemany + one commit via new
    db_manager.bulk_update_clan_subscription_statuses() instead of N individual transactions.

2026-04-01 (date)
- Performance: Parallel JSON loading in load_all_temp_war_stats()
  - Root cause: At startup, 4,086 temp war JSON files were read sequentially, taking ~66 seconds.
    The loop iterated all 30,095 clans (O(n_clans)), calling _load_war_data_from_json() one at a
    time for each that had a matching file.
  - Fix 1: Inverted loop — now iterates only the ~4k file entries and uses a pre-built reverse
    safe_tag→clan_tag dict, eliminating 26k wasted loop iterations.
  - Fix 2: All matching JSON reads are now submitted to a ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16) and
    executed concurrently. Pure I/O, no shared state → fully thread-safe.
  - File changed: qapbot/cache_manager.py (load_all_temp_war_stats)

2026-04-01 (date)
- Fixed: Memory leak — CoCClanCache expired entries never evicted
  - Root cause: CoCClanCache had a clear_expired() method that removes hard-TTL-expired entries
    (>600 s old) but it was never called in production. Over 12 days, 23,456+ coc.Clan objects
    (each ~10 KB incl. 50 members) accumulated in memory — visible as 214 MB just for the cache.
  - Fix: clear_expired() is now called in periodic_main after every successful update cycle
    so the cache stays bounded to recently-active clans only.
  - File changed: QapBot.py (periodic_main)

- Fixed: Memory leak — CACHE.notification_state never pruned
  - Root cause: delete_notification_state_for_war() existed in db_manager but was never called
    outside of tests. Every war involving a subscribed player added a permanent entry to the
    in-memory notification_state dict and the notification_state/channel_notification_state DB
    tables. Both grew without bound across restarts.
  - Fix: _archive_and_log_war_file() now deletes the in-memory entry and calls the new sync
    DB method delete_notification_state_sync() whenever a war JSON is moved to archive/.
  - Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py (_archive_and_log_war_file),
    qapbot/db_manager.py (added delete_notification_state_sync)

- Fixed: GC pressure from coc.py object graphs
  - Root cause: Each update cycle creates large `coc.Clan` object graphs that may form
    reference cycles. Python's generational GC was not explicitly triggered between cycles,
    causing memory to build up between automatic GC runs.
  - Fix: gc.collect(0) (youngest generation, fast) is now called after each successful cycle
    in periodic_main alongside clear_expired().
  - File changed: QapBot.py (periodic_main)

- Added: tracemalloc-based /admin Memory Profile command for runtime memory diagnosis
  - tracemalloc.start(25) is called at bot startup to record 25-frame allocation stacks.
  - New /admin action "Memory Profile" (bot admin only) takes a snapshot and writes a report
    to data/logs/memprofile_<timestamp>.txt containing:
    * CACHE structure sizes (all major dicts)
    * Top 15 GC object types by count
    * Top 50 allocation sites by cumulative size (tracemalloc lineno stats)
  - A compact summary is also sent as an ephemeral Discord reply.
  - Files changed: QapBot.py (tracemalloc.start), QBdiscordcmds.py (new admin choice + handler),
    qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper_admin_command.py (handle_memory_profile)

- Added: Automatic CWL history backfill for newly subscribed clans
  - New async function backfill_last_cwl_for_clan(clan_tag) in QBhelperfunctions.py:
    * Fetches the last available CWL league group via CACHE.get_league_group()
    * Checks DB idempotency first (war_summary WHERE is_cwl=1 AND cwl_season=?) — returns
      early if season data already present, so safe to call repeatedly
    * Fetches all war rounds in parallel via CACHE.get_league_war()
    * Filters to rounds where our clan is a participant
    * Writes each round's JSON via CACHE.save_war_object(..., force_save=True) to bypass
      the 24-hour stale guard (CWL backfill is intentionally historical)
    * Finalises each round through _process_war_history() → war_summary + war_attacks DB rows
    * Returns a human-readable summary string suitable for Discord followup
  - Wired into validate_and_add_clan_to_cache() (QBdiscocmdshelper.py): fires as a

- Fixed: War prediction used a single shared CWL league for both clans (architectural bug)
  - Root cause: monte_carlo_war_prediction() / simulate_attacks_dynamic() accepted only one
    cwl_league parameter applied symmetrically to all attackers regardless of clan. With both
    leagues defaulting to "Master League I" for newly-added clans, the symmetric simulation
    produced Win 1%, Lose 1%, Draw 98% even for a Champion I vs Master II matchup.

    and all four CWL round call sites updated to pass the correct per-clan league.
  - Bonus fix: _resolve_war_league now captures the running event loop on any async call and
    performs a synchronous blocking fetch (asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe, timeout 3 s)
    when called from a thread context (asyncio.to_thread), so the first prediction after a
    newly-added clan is accurate instead of falling back to the default.

  - Root cause: When a user clicks a button on a persistent RegistrationView while the bot's
    asyncio event loop is busy (e.g., mid-leaderboard-cycle), Discord's 3-second interaction
    window expires before the callback runs. The bot's attempt to respond then raises
    discord.NotFound (error code 10062: Unknown interaction), which discord.py catches and
    re-logs as an ERROR via its default on_error handler.
  - Fix: Added `on_error` override to RegistrationView that silently captures 10062 errors
    and logs them as INFO ("Expired interaction...") instead, re-raising all other errors to
    preserve existing error reporting.
  - File changed: qapbot/ui_registration.py

- Fixed: Bulk message deletions during leaderboard cycles hitting Discord 429 rate limits
  - Root cause: The periodic 5-minute leaderboard update concurrently deletes many old messages
    across clans. Each deletion is individually sequential per-clan, but multiple clans run in
    parallel, saturating Discord's per-resource rate limit (~5 deletes/s). The discord_retry
    backoff (1s → 2s → 4s) was insufficient during heavy concurrent deletion bursts, causing
    ~10 messages per cycle to fail permanently with "40062: Service resource is being rate limited".
  - Fix: Added `await asyncio.sleep(0.5)` after each successful message deletion in both
    `_delete_messages_by_filter` and `post_or_update_leaderboard_message`. This paces individual
    clan deletion loops to max 2 deletes/s, providing headroom when multiple clans run in parallel.
  - File changed: qapbot/QBdiscocmdshelper.py

- Fixed: PlayerSubstringModal "No matches" logged at WARNING level
  - A user searching for a name with no results (e.g., typo, nickname) logged a WARNING,
    creating false-positive noise in error analysis. Changed to INFO since it is expected
    user-input behavior, not an application error.
  - File changed: qapbot/ui_registration.py

- Fixed: avgstars Ø Stars/Atk and Ø🔥/Atk divided by made-attacks instead of total attacks
  - Root cause: The "Attacks" column displayed total_attacks (Attacks + Missed_Attacks) but
    the avg formula used attacks_made (Attacks only). For a player with 17 stars, 6 made
    attacks, 1 missed: showed 2.83 (17/6) instead of the correct 2.43 (17/7).
  - Fix: Changed avg = Stars / attacks_made → avg = Stars / total_attacks in both rendering
    paths in render_leaderboard() (grouped avgstarsbyth branch and non-grouped else branch).
    Updated all three sort_keys (avgstars, avgstarsbyth, avgstars_cwl) to use total attacks
    in the denominator. Also fixed Avg_Dest_Pct in _merge_entries() to use attacks_total so
    the Ø🔥/Atk column is consistent with the displayed Attacks count.
  - Files changed: qapbot/formatting.py, QBhelperfunctions.py

    copied from the first month only, while the Attacks denominator reflected the full multi-month
    total — same class of bug as the avgstars divide-by-wrong-denominator fix.

- Changed: /analyse leaguegroup attacker ranking — total stars + map position tiebreak
  - Old ranking: average stars on equal/higher TH attacks only (upward-attack quality metric).
  - New ranking: primary = most total stars across the whole CWL season; tiebreak = lowest sum of
    attacked opponents' map positions (map position 1 = strongest base, lower sum = harder
    opponents faced → rewards players who get top stars against the best defenders).

- Fixed: Defense leaderboard metric changed from Def/War to Stars/Def
  - Root cause: "Def/War" divided by Wars_Count (total wars participated), not by actual defenses.
    A player in an ongoing war round where they haven't been attacked yet was counted in the
    denominator, inflating Def/War scores — the discrepancy surfaced via /analyse leaguegroup.
  - New metric: Stars_per_Def = Defensive_Stars / Defs_Count, where Defs_Count = number of
    wars in which the player was actually attacked (times_defended > 0 OR defensive_stars > 0
    as fallback for pre-migration rows where times_defended defaults to 0).
  - times_defended (from CoC opponentAttacks field) was already stored in the war_attacks table
    via build_per_attack_rows — no schema migration required.

      _merge_entries: added def_war_sets tracking per player; computes Defs_Count and Stars_per_Def;
      retains Def_Stars_per_War as legacy alias. calculate_leaderboard: added "Times_Defended"
      to temp-stats per-player aggregation. Multi-month aggregation: propagates Defs_Count and
      computes Stars_per_Def at the end.

- Added: Config variables for Monte Carlo simulation parallelism
  - SIM_MULTIPROCESS_ENABLED (default: true) — set to false to force single-threaded
    simulation (useful on very low-core or shared server-machine systems)
  - SIM_MAX_WORKERS (default: 0) — 0 means use all logical cores (capped at 8);
    set to a positive integer to limit to that many worker processes
  - Values read by load_config() and stored in CONFIG.sim_multiprocess_enabled /
    CONFIG.sim_max_workers (immutable BotConfig dataclass fields)
  - init_sim_pool(enabled, max_workers) in QBwarsim.py reads the config at startup,
    creates/replaces the ProcessPoolExecutor, and logs the resolved worker count
  - get_cpu_info() returns {cpu, logical_cores, sim_enabled, sim_workers} for display
  - Files changed: qapbot/config.py, QBwarsim.py, README.md

- Added: CPU and simulation worker info to startup log and /status command
  - Startup log now emits:
      CPU: <brand> — <N> logical core(s)
      [SIM] Multiprocess: enabled — <W> worker(s) of <N> logical core(s) used
  - /status command now shows:
      CPU: <brand>
      Cores: <N> logical | Sim workers: <W> / <N>   (or "disabled")
  - Files changed: QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py

- Fixed: Monte Carlo worker deadlock causing lingering/stuck processes on server-machine
  - Root cause: ProcessPoolExecutor on Linux uses fork() by default. When
    monte_carlo_war_prediction() is called inside asyncio.to_thread(), the parent
    process already has multiple threads running (event loop, HTTP sessions, DB
    pool). fork() snapshots the parent at that instant, including any mutexes
    that are locked at that moment — most notably the logging module's internal
    lock. The child inherits the locked state but NOT the thread holding the lock,
    so the worker deadlocks the first time it tries to write a log line.
  - Observed symptoms:
    · Lingering QapBot.py worker PIDs visible in ps aux during the bot's sleep cycle
      (worker from a previous run never exited — it deadlocked at fork and orphaned)
    · /admin Maintenance Start stuck: MAINTENANCE_START waits on cycle_idle_event
      but the asyncio thread running calculate_win_probability never returned
      (pool.map() blocked on the deadlocked worker); the 60s timeout fired and
      reported "active" but the thread was still live, preventing periodic_main
      from ever finishing; only kill -9 could break the chain
  - Fix: replaced default fork context with multiprocessing.get_context('spawn').
    spawn starts a fresh Python interpreter with no inherited mutexes or thread
    state — deadlock is structurally impossible.
  - Added 30-second timeout via concurrent.futures.wait() as a belt-and-suspenders
    safety net: if a worker still hangs for any unexpected reason, we cancel
    remaining futures, log a warning, and fall back to in-process sequential
    execution rather than blocking forever.
  - Trade-off: spawn adds ~50-100 ms per call vs fork (fresh interpreter startup).
    At 584-1158 ms per round this is 5-15% overhead — acceptable.
  - Files changed: QBwarsim.py

- Performance: Parallelized Monte Carlo war simulation — ephemeral process pool
  - monte_carlo_war_prediction() previously ran all n_sim iterations sequentially
    in a single-threaded Python loop (GIL-bound, ~1s per call on server-machine)
  - Added _monte_carlo_chunk(): module-level worker function (must be picklable)
    that runs a batch of iterations and returns aggregated (win, lose, draw, stars)
  - Added _SIM_N_WORKERS = min(os.cpu_count(), 8): auto-detected from host CPU
  - Threshold logic: pool used only when n_sim >= _SIM_N_WORKERS * _SIM_MIN_CHUNK
    (default: n_workers × 100). Below threshold the simulation runs in-process,
    keeping monkeypatching in tests compatible (worker processes don't inherit
    patched module globals from the test process)
  - Pool design: EPHEMERAL with SPAWN start method — a fresh ProcessPoolExecutor
    (using multiprocessing.get_context('spawn')) is created and destroyed inside
    each monte_carlo_war_prediction() call via a 'with' statement.
    Workers exist only for the duration of the computation (~500–1500 ms) and are
    never left idling in the background. spawn avoids fork-lock deadlocks (see fix
    above). Avoids persistent-pool orphan accumulation on server-machine restarts.
  - Fallback: if pool raises or 30 s timeout fires, logs a warning and falls back
    to sequential in-process execution
  - Measured speedup on the prod server (2 logical cores, n_sim=1000):
      Before (sequential): ~4146 ms total across 3 rounds
      After (ephemeral pool + asyncio.gather): ~1746 ms total (+7 ms fork overhead vs
      persistent pool, well within run-to-run noise)
  - Files changed: QBwarsim.py

- Performance: Parallelized comp_mode prediction lines with asyncio.gather
  - _cwl_append_prediction() for comp_mode previously awaited two sequential
    asyncio.to_thread(calculate_win_probability, ...) calls (skill-adj then base)
  - Since each call dispatches work to the ProcessPoolExecutor process pool
    (bypassing the GIL), the two can genuinely run in parallel
  - Replaced sequential awaits with asyncio.gather() — both calls start at the
    same time; wall time per comp_mode round drops from ~2x to ~1x MC call time
  - Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py

- Performance: Removed full-table-scan subquery from all CWL DB query methods
  - get_cwl_attack_records_sync, get_direct_cwl_attacks_sync, and
    get_player_cwl_attacks_multi_season_sync all contained a LEFT JOIN subquery:
      SELECT player_tag, MAX(th_level) AS th_level FROM war_attacks GROUP BY player_tag
    used as a fallback for missing defender_th values. This materialized the entire
    war_attacks table on every call (~500ms each; two times in get_cwl_attack_records_sync).
  - Replaced with COALESCE(NULLIF(wa.defender_th, 0), wa.th_level): uses the attacker's
    own TH level as a neutral fallback — valid same-TH assumption for unknown defenders,
    no extra join, no full-table scan.
  - Fixed: 1.4s gap visible in logs between last [SKILL-LOAD] message and Monte Carlo
    start in cwlinfo_comp was caused by the two full-table scans in
    get_cwl_attack_records_sync running after all skill loads had completed.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py

- Performance: Added missing war_summary(war_id) index
  - JOIN between war_attacks.war_id and war_summary.war_id had no index on the
    war_summary side — SQLite was doing a full scan of war_summary for every
    matching row in war_attacks.
  - Added CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_ws_war_id ON war_summary(war_id)
    to _create_schema() and applied it manually to both PROD and DEV databases.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py

- Fixed: Event loop blocking during CWL skill factor loading

    inside the async event loop for each CWL group clan (~8 clans × ~500ms each = up
    to 4–16 seconds of event loop blocking visible as log messages every ~500ms).
  - Fixed: wrapped each call in asyncio.to_thread() so SQLite queries run in the
    thread pool, keeping the event loop free between skill loads.
  - _load_skill_factors_for_clan() stays synchronous (dual-use: also called from sync
    thread context in generate_war_info_text via db_manager thread pool).
  - Files changed: QBhelperfunctions.py

- Fixed: cwlinfo_comp debug table: predicted-only opponents (e.g. round 7 "not yet scheduled")
  were missing entirely because no war object exists in _round_war_cache for them.
  - Root cause: Steps 2 and 3 only process clans already in _dbg_opp_clan_order, which is
    populated by Step 2 (live war cache). Clans with no war object never entered the flow.
  - Step 4 (rewrite): all CWL league group clans are tracked in the DB. For any future-round
    opponent not yet in _dbg_opp_clan_order, Step 4 now queries:
    1. Roster: get_cwl_roster_sync(clan_tag, cwl_season) — new db_manager method; returns the
       full 15-member roster from the most recent war_summary row for that clan in the season
       (player_tag, player_name, th_level, map_position). Players with 0 attacks are included
       because sentinel rows (attack_order=0) appear in war_attacks for all roster members.
    2. Attack history: get_direct_cwl_attacks_sync(clan_tag, cwl_season) — existing method,
       now also returns map_position. DB-only, no group_clan_map dependency.
    Clan display name still falls back to group_clan_map.name if available, then the tag.
  - Also fixed: map_position was missing from get_direct_cwl_attacks_sync SELECT and return dict;
    added so the field is available to all callers.
  - Files changed: qapbot/db_manager.py, QBhelperfunctions.py

- Added: Extended war_attacks and war_summary schema with new columns for richer attack data
  - war_attacks new columns (all idempotent ALTER TABLE migrations + CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS):
    - defender_th INTEGER DEFAULT 0 — defender's TH level, stored at write time; eliminates live join
    - defender_map_position INTEGER DEFAULT 0 — defender's map position in the war
    - duration INTEGER DEFAULT 0 — attack duration in seconds (future ML signal)

    - best_def_destruction REAL DEFAULT 0.0 — % destruction of best opponent attack received

    📊 standard TH-diff + league-calibrated model (unchanged current algorithm)
    🧠 Skill-adj.: player-skill-weighted model using each player's actual CWL performance
  - Algorithm: Per-player skill factor = actual_avg_stars / expected_avg_stars at equal-TH
    matchup in the clan's CWL league.  Applied by scaling p_3★ proportionally and
    redistributing the probability delta across p_0★–p_2★ (renormalized).
    Skill factors clamped to [0.2, 3.0] to prevent extreme small-sample outliers.
    Only OUR clan's players receive skill adjustment; opponent uses the standard model.

- Added: /status command now includes Database Statistics section

- Fixed: CTRL+C during sleep cycle had a multi-second delay (up to ~7 s) before shutdown
  on Windows instead of responding immediately.
  - Root cause: WindowsSelectorEventLoopPolicy uses select.select() for I/O polling, but
    never calls signal.set_wakeup_fd() (only done by loop.add_signal_handler(), which raises
    NotImplementedError on Windows). Without a wakeup fd, select.select() is unaware of
    pending signals and only returns on the next real I/O event (e.g. Discord websocket
    traffic), causing the observed delay.
  - Fix: In _setup_hook() (first code running inside the live event loop), call
    signal.set_wakeup_fd(loop._csock.fileno()). Python's C-level signal handler then writes
    a byte to _csock on CTRL+C, making select() return immediately and delivering SIGINT
    to _signal_shutdown_handler without delay.
  - File: QapBot.py (_setup_hook)

- Fixed: Windows BSOD (0x00000139 KERNEL_SECURITY_CHECK_FAILURE) during startup caused by
  Windows Defender minifilter kernel driver overload from excessive glob.glob() calls
  - Root cause: load_all_temp_war_stats() in cache_manager.py called glob.glob() once per
    tracked clan (1569 calls at time of crash) on the same data/temp/ directory (341 files).
    This generated ~535,000 file-system callbacks into Windows Defender's kernel minifilter
    driver (WdFilter.sys) in a tight synchronous loop during startup. WdFilter.sys corrupted
    a kernel data structure under this load → 0x00000139 BSOD.
  - Why it started with CWL: clan count grew from ~1385 to 1569 with CWL tracking expansion,
    crossing the threshold where Defender's minifilter can no longer keep up with the callback
    rate without kernel data structure corruption.
  - Why NIC driver changes did not help: crash is purely file-system / kernel callback load,
    completely unrelated to network stack. BSOD codes (0x00000050, 0x00000139) varied because
    WdFilter.sys hit different kernel structures depending on timing.
  - Fix: Replaced the per-clan glob.glob() loop with a single directory scan at startup.
    data/temp/ is now listed exactly once via glob.glob("data/temp/*_war_data.json"), building
    a {clan_tag: filepath} lookup map. Each clan then passes its filepath directly to
    _load_war_data_from_json(json_file_path=...), skipping all further directory access.
    Result: 1569 glob calls → 1 glob call (~1500× reduction in filesystem operations).
  - File: qapbot/cache_manager.py (load_all_temp_war_stats)

  - Row 1: Channel dropdown (ChannelSelect); disabled in clan_link mode

  - File: qapbot/db_manager.py

- Fixed: CTRL+C / SIGINT shutdown delay (up to 300 seconds instead of immediate)
  - Root cause: asyncio.Event.set() called directly from a signal handler uses loop.call_soon()
    which queues the wakeup but does NOT write to the loop's self-pipe. The sleeping selector
    stays blocked until the next natural I/O event or the 300 s timeout fires.
  - Fix: use loop.call_soon_threadsafe(QBcore.shutdown_event.set) which writes to the self-pipe
    and wakes the selector within milliseconds. Fallback for unavailable loop retained.
  - File: QapBot.py (_signal_shutdown_handler)

- Fixed: Background tasks (fire-and-forget) accessing closed CoC client / DB during maintenance
  - Root cause: asyncio.ensure_future(_harvest_cwl_group_clans(...)) and
    asyncio.create_task(_do_refresh()) tasks spawned during the leaderboard posting phase
    run independently and outlive the main update cycle. When maintenance START closes the
    CoC client and DB, these tasks continue running in the event loop:
    (a) Tasks already past their CoC API call tried to write to the closed DB — the DB
        auto-reconnect fired and re-opened the database DURING maintenance, violating the
        "safe to copy" guarantee.
    (b) Tasks that had not yet made their CoC call got RuntimeError: Session is closed —
        went through 3 retries with backoff, generating 3 WARNINGs + 1 ERROR per clan.
  - Note: In this specific incident the errors appeared as normal retries (no reconnect
    callback attempted) because the tasks were started by the old code instance (startup 3,
    before the new coc_health.py with reconnect callback was deployed). With new code,
    the reconnect callback would have fired and tried to re-authenticate during maintenance,
    which would be equally wrong.
  - Fix 1 (critical): db_manager._ensure_connected() — refuse auto-reconnect if
    maintenance_mode is True. Raises RuntimeError immediately instead of re-opening the
    DB, preserving the "safe to copy" guarantee for the data/ directory.
  - Fix 2: coc_retry (coc_health.py) — if "Session is closed" AND maintenance_mode is True,
    log at DEBUG level and raise immediately (no retries, no reconnect callback). Session
    was intentionally closed; no point retrying.
  - Fix 3: _harvest_cwl_group_clans (QBhelperfunctions.py) — bail out at the top of the
    function if maintenance_mode. Clean DEBUG log, no CoC/DB access attempted.
  - Fix 4: _do_refresh (qapbot/coc_cache.py) — bail out at the top of the coroutine if
    maintenance_mode. Same pattern as fix 3.
  - Files: qapbot/db_manager.py, qapbot/coc_health.py, QBhelperfunctions.py,
           qapbot/coc_cache.py

- Performance: Offloaded calculate_win_probability() to thread pool via asyncio.to_thread()
  - Root cause of slowness: calculate_win_probability() is a CPU-bound synchronous Monte
    Carlo simulation (0.35–1.1 s per call). With 16+ calls per cycle it was blocking the
    asyncio event loop for ~12 s total, making the bot unresponsive to Discord during
    leaderboard posting.
  - Decision: asyncio.to_thread() (default ThreadPoolExecutor) chosen — no lifecycle mgmt
    needed; yields the event loop; one-liner per call site.
  - Call sites fixed:
    (a) 4 call sites in async generate_cwlinfo_embeds() (lines ~1127, ~1287, ~1327, ~1368
        in QBhelperfunctions.py): each wrapped with await asyncio.to_thread(...)
    (b) 1 call site in sync generate_war_info_text() — can't await inside sync; instead
        wrapped the whole generate_leaderboard_text() call at the async caller level:
        • QapBot.py post_leaderboards_to_subscribed_channels()
        • QBdiscordcmds.py /leaderboard handler
  - Impact: ~12 s event loop blocking eliminated per cycle.
  - Files: QBhelperfunctions.py, QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py

- Performance: Parallelized CoC role-sync bootstrap (guild_role_manager.py)
  - Root cause of slowness: get_clan() sequential for each bootstrap clan (~4.8 s for 8 clans).
  - Fix: Replaced sequential for-loop with asyncio.gather() over all bootstrap clans.
  - Impact: N sequential CoC API calls → N concurrent. ~4.8 s → ~1 s for 8 clans.
  - File: qapbot/guild_role_manager.py

- Performance: Parallelized leaderboard posting across channels (QapBot.py)
  - Root cause of slowness: post_leaderboards_to_subscribed_channels() processed channels
    sequentially — dominated the 39.3 s cycle (29.5 s in leaderboard posting phase).
  - Fix: Pre-filter channels into work list; process concurrently via asyncio.gather() with
    Semaphore(5). Subscriptions within each channel stay sequential (Discord rate limits).
    generate_leaderboard_text() also offloaded via asyncio.to_thread() (see above).
  - Impact: Text generation and Discord posting for different channels now overlap.
  - File: QapBot.py (post_leaderboards_to_subscribed_channels)
  - All 1307 tests passing.

2026-03-06 (Date)
- Windows stability improvements added (defensive; not the BSOD root cause fix):
  NOTE: The true root cause of the BSODs was the M×N glob.glob() file-read condition —
  see the "Windows BSOD (0x00000139 KERNEL_SECURITY_CHECK_FAILURE)" entry above for the fix.
  The Intel I225-V NIC driver was investigated as a hypothesis during this date but was
  incorrect — NIC driver changes did not stop the crashes.

  OBSERVATIONS that led to the investigation:
    Crash 1 @ 18:18:30 — 0x00000050 (PAGE_FAULT_IN_NONPAGED_AREA) during on_ready CoC auth
    Crash 2 @ 19:36:00 — 0x00000050 — 18 s after first sleep ended
    Crash 3 @ 19:59:07 — 0x00000139 (KERNEL_SECURITY_CHECK_FAILURE) during on_ready startup
  All three crashes were ultimately caused by the M×N glob.glob() load at startup, not the NIC.

  DEFENSIVE CODE IMPROVEMENTS added regardless (good practices on Windows):
    1. asyncio.WindowsSelectorEventLoopPolicy() set before bot.run() on Windows.
       ProactorEventLoop (IOCP) has known edge-case issues with discord.py and aiohttp on
       Windows; SelectorEventLoop is more stable for I/O-bound bots.
    2. asyncio.Semaphore(20) wraps fetch_single_clan() in Phase-1.
       Caps concurrent CoC API fetches at 20 regardless of clan count; prevents API overload.
    3. coc_client.close() before sleep + startup_login() after wake — added then REMOVED 2026-03-07.
       Was a speculative mitigation for stale-pool reconnect burst. Caused "Session is closed"
       errors on slash commands during the sleep window. Removed; reconnect callback in
       coc_health.py handles any accidental session-close gracefully.
    Files: QapBot.py, qapbot/coc_health.py

2026-03-05 (Date)
- Fixed: /status crash on early startup with AttributeError 'NoneType' has no attribute 'save_leaderboard_message'
  - Root cause: /status was issued 3 s after setup_hook completed but before on_ready finished
    initialising the DB. set_leaderboard_message() called db_manager.save_leaderboard_message()
    while db_manager was still None.
  - Fix 1 (primary): Added startup guard to bot.tree.interaction_check in QBcore.py. Any slash
    command fired before bot.fully_initialized=True receives an ephemeral message
    "⏳ The bot is still starting up. Please try again in a few seconds." and returns False —
    the command never reaches business logic. (i18n key: commands.errors.startup_in_progress)
  - Fix 2 (safety net): set_leaderboard_message() in cache_manager.py now checks
    db_manager is not None before attempting the DB write; logs WARNING and returns early
    instead of raising AttributeError. In-memory cache is still updated.
  - Files: QBcore.py, qapbot/cache_manager.py, qapbot/translations/en.json,
    qapbot/translations/de.json

- Fixed: All /admin subcommands still worked during maintenance (only MAINTENANCE_END should pass)
  - Root cause: interaction_check allowed the entire /admin command through;
    only the action option value distinguishes MAINTENANCE_END from all other admin actions.
  - Fix: interaction_check now inspects interaction.data['options'] to read the 'action' value;
    only 'MAINTENANCE_END' passes — all other /admin choices are blocked with the maintenance
    message. str() cast added to satisfy type checker (option values can be int/float/bool).
  - File: QBcore.py

- Fixed: Maintenance start could close DB while main() was still writing (data integrity risk)
  - Root cause: the MAINTENANCE_START handler set maintenance_mode=True and immediately called
    coc_client.close() and db_manager.close() without checking whether an update cycle was
    active. db_manager has auto-reconnect so a mid-cycle persist_clan() call after the close
    would silently reopen the DB, invalidating the "safe to copy" guarantee.
  - Fix: handler now polls in_main_cycle (1 s intervals, 5 min max) after defer() before
    touching shared resources. Only closes coc_client and db_manager once in_main_cycle is False
    (cycle fully complete). Discord followup window is 15 min so the wait is well within bounds.
  - File: QBdiscordcmds.py

- Added: BOT_VERSION global and version display in /status + startup log
  - BOT_VERSION = "1.0.0" defined in QBcore.py as single source of truth.
  - /status embed shows "Version: 1.0.0" as first line after the header.
  - Bot startup log now prints "[INFO] Version: 1.0.0" immediately after "QapBot started."
  - Files: QBcore.py, QBdiscordcmds.py, QapBot.py

- Added: Maintenance mode for safe deployments and data operations
  - New commands (bot admin only):
    · /admin Maintenance Start — suspends all periodic update cycles, closes CoC API client
      and database connection so data/ can be safely copied or edited
    · /admin Maintenance End   — triggers a graceful shutdown with exit code 42, which the
      new start.sh script detects and uses to restart the bot immediately
  - During maintenance:
    · All slash commands are blocked with an ephemeral "bot is in maintenance mode" message
      (i18n key commands.errors.maintenance_mode_active, EN + DE)
    · Only /admin with action=MAINTENANCE_END is allowed through
    · Periodic update cycles are skipped (maintenance_mode flag checked at top of the loop)
    · Maintenance start waits for any running main() cycle to finish (in_main_cycle guard)
      before closing DB/CoC client (max 5 min)
  - Shell script (start.sh): wraps python QapBot.py in a while-true loop that restarts
    only on exit code 42; all other exit codes stop the loop and propagate to parent
  - Exit code propagation: QBcore.exit_code set to 42; os._exit() used (not sys.exit())
    because sys.exit() inside an asyncio task only terminates the task, leaving bot.run()
    hanging forever
  - New globals in QBcore.py: maintenance_mode, in_main_cycle, exit_code, EXIT_CODE_MAINTENANCE
  - Files modified: QBcore.py, QapBot.py, QBdiscordcmds.py,
    qapbot/translations/en.json, qapbot/translations/de.json, start.sh (new)

- Fixed: ClanManagementView mode-select dropdown raised [ERROR] when interaction expired (10062)
  - Root cause: during the parallel 28-clan API fetch cycle the event loop was saturated; a user
    clicking the mode dropdown had their interaction token expire before _on_mode_select() could
    call defer(), resulting in discord.errors.NotFound 10062 logged at ERROR level
  - Fix: Added on_error() override to ClanManagementView that downgrades 10062 to [WARNING] and
    returns gracefully; all other exceptions continue to propagate to the default handler
    (qapbot/ui_clan_management.py)

2026-03-03 (Date)
- Fixed: Family tag (#28D925AF14) being polled as a real CoC clan every 5 min → constant NotFound 404 errors
  - Root cause: subscriptions.clan_tag is intentionally allowed to hold family tags (for family-level
    war notifications). _ensure_clan_exists() in db_manager.py, called by save_subscriptions_for_channel(),
    did not know this distinction and auto-created a placeholder row in the clans table for #28D925AF14.
    On the next restart, load_clan_name_cache() loaded the row, update_all_clan_subscription_statuses()
    set has_active_subscriptions=True (direct subscription match), and PHASE-1 started polling it via
    the CoC API every cycle — producing ERROR [PHASE-1] NotFound 404 approximately every 5 minutes.
  - Fix 1: _ensure_clan_exists() now queries clan_families before inserting; if the tag is a family
    tag it logs a debug message and returns early (qapbot/db_manager.py)
  - Fix 2: validate_clan_cache_consistency() now runs an eviction step (step 0) before its existing
    member_clans check: any tag in clan_name_cache that also appears as a key in clan_families is
    removed from the in-memory cache and deleted from the clans table (qapbot/cache_manager.py)
  - Data fix: SQL script data/fix_family_tag_in_clans.sql — run once against prod to delete the
    #28D925AF14 row from the clans table (the 4 family subscriptions remain correct)
  - Impact: Eliminates the 404 error flood; family-level subscription logic unaffected (individual
    member clans still get has_active_subscriptions=True via the family subscription lookup)

  - Root cause: a misleading comment claimed get_league_war() was "cached" but no cache existed;
    both the pre-scan loop and the main render loop each called get_league_war() sequentially for
    every round, resulting in 2× the necessary API calls (up to 57 total in a full 7-round CWL)
  - Fix: added _round_war_cache dict; pre-scan stores the fetched war object per round_idx; render
    loop checks _round_war_cache first and only falls back to API if the round was not pre-scanned
    (e.g. round 0 edge cases) — eliminating all duplicate fetches
  - API calls reduced from up to 57 to up to 29 (best case 8 → 15 → 8)
  - Superseded the same day by the deeper fix below

- Fixed: cwlinfo leaderboard fetched warended rounds from API when DB already has the data
  - Root cause: the pre-scan looped over ALL assigned_rounds calling get_league_war() for each,
    including past (warended) rounds whose data already lives in the war_summary DB table
  - Key insight: assigned_rounds[-1] is always the live round (CWL assigns rounds sequentially);
    all prior elements are warended with their data already persisted in war_summary
  - Fix:
    · Load _db_season_rows from war_summary filtered to current CWL season (zero API calls)
    · Seed real_war_opponents + real_team_size directly from DB rows (no API)
    · Pre-scan now fetches only assigned_rounds[-1] (the live round): 1–4 calls max
    · Render loop: for past rounds, check _db_past_row from _db_season_rows first;
      if found, renders directly from DB and continues (skip API entirely for that round);
      falls back to API only if DB row is missing (e.g. war not yet archived)
    · Added _lineup_from_json_r() helper to render TH arrays from DB JSON fields
  - API calls reduced from up to 57 → 2–5 (1 get_league_group + 1–4 for live round)
  - Location: QBhelperfunctions.py generate_cwlinfo_embeds()

- Fixed: cwlinfo in_war win probability ignored current star scores (Monte Carlo started from 0–0)
  - Root cause: proxy_r dict built in generate_cwlinfo_embeds() omitted 'stars' key from clan/opponent
    subdicts; monte_carlo_war_prediction() reads stars from war_data (my_clan.get('stars', 0)) not from
    the explicit parameters → both sides started at 0 stars regardless of actual scores
  - currentwar mode was correct because it passes the real cached war_data dict which has 'stars'
  - Fix: added 'stars': my_stars_r and 'stars': opp_stars_r to the respective clan/opponent dicts in
    proxy_r so monte_carlo_war_prediction() sees the true current score as its baseline
  - Affected state: in_war only; preparation/notinwar/predicted states unaffected (stars=0 there by design)
  - Location: QBhelperfunctions.py generate_cwlinfo_embeds() inwar block

2026-03-02 (Date)
- Added: CWL round predictions for not-yet-scheduled rounds in /cwlinfo embed
  - Before CWL starts (league group available, no war tags yet), all 7 rounds show predicted opponents
    instead of "Not yet scheduled – eligible roster only"
  - Predicted opponents: remaining 6 group clans shuffled deterministically (seed = hash(clan_tag + season))
    so the order is stable across re-renders but unique per clan
  - Our roster: actual eligible members from league_group.clans sorted top-N by TH level
  - Opponent roster: their eligible members from league_group.clans sorted top-N by TH level
  - N = war size inferred from first real war in the group, or from eligible member count (CWL sizes: 5/10/15/30)
  - Runs the full calculate_win_probability() for each predicted round (same as preparation/inwar states)
  - When real war tags become available (CWL progresses), the normal code path takes over automatically
    — predictions are replaced by real data without any special-casing
  - Pre-scan phase fetches all already-assigned rounds via get_league_war() (cached) to exclude
    their real opponents from the prediction pool
  - Also added win probability to the existing "Scheduled" (notinwar) state which previously showed
    lineup but no win prediction
  - Location: QBhelperfunctions.py generate_cwlinfo_embeds()

- Fixed: CWL war prediction showing Draw 98% / Confidence 0% for all wars (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: proxy_r (inwar) and proxy_p (preparation) member dicts only contained {'townhall': N} —
    no 'tag' key. assign_attacks_to_bases_with_stars() builds base_stars = {m.get('tag',''): 0 ...}
    which collapsed all 5 members to a single '' key → only 1 base in the simulation.
    calculate_max_possible_stars() returned 3 (1 base × 3 stars) instead of 15 (5 × 3).
    The same collapse occurred on the attacker side, so both sides scored near-zero → Draw dominates.
    Additionally, 'attacks_per_member' was missing from the proxy so the function defaulted to 2
    instead of 1 for CWL.
  · Fix: added 'tag': getattr(m, 'tag', str(i)) via enumerate() to all four member lists across both
    proxy dicts; added 'attacks': [...] (real attack data for inwar, [] for preparation);
    added 'attacks_per_member': apm_r / apm_p to both proxy dicts.

- Fixed: cwlinfo embed not showing all 7 future CWL rounds when matchups are already known (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause: generate_cwlinfo_embeds() handled 3 war states (warended, inwar, preparation) but not
    `notInWar`, which is the state the CoC API returns for future CWL rounds that are scheduled but
    haven't entered their prep window yet. These rounds fell through to the `else` branch which only
    printed the raw state name with no roster lineup.
  · Fix: added `elif state_str_e == 'notinwar':` branch (mirrors `preparation` logic) — shows the
    opponent name/tag/league, builds my roster from my_war_mems → our_eligible_members fallback, and
    builds opp roster from opp_war_mems → group_clan_map fallback. Label: "📅 Scheduled".
  · Result: all 7 rounds are now fully displayed from the moment CWL matchups go live.

- Fixed: cwlinfo Round 1 showing "⏳ Not yet scheduled" despite war already being live (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause 1: coc.py's ClanWarLeagueGroup._from_data() filters out all rounds whose first war
    tag is "#0" before storing them in .rounds. For a fresh CWL where only Round 1 has real tags,
    .rounds contains only 1 list; rounds 2-7 are dropped entirely. The code iterated .rounds
    (1 entry) instead of range(1, number_of_rounds+1) (7 entries).
  · Root cause 2: each element of .rounds is already a List[str] of war tags. The code used
    getattr(round_obj, 'war_tags', []) on a plain list, which always returns the default [] —
    so even round 1's real war tags were silently ignored and the "Not yet scheduled" path triggered.
  · Fix: replaced `for round_idx, round_obj in enumerate(rounds_data)` with
    `for round_idx in range(1, total_rounds+1)` using `number_of_rounds` for the total count and
    `assigned_rounds[round_idx-1]` (the pre-filtered List[str]) for war tags when available.
  · Also added `import asyncio` at top-level (was only locally imported in some functions).

- Fixed: CWL group clans (7 opponents) not added to tracking DB when the regular war endpoint
  returns notInWar (QBhelperfunctions.py — generate_cwlinfo_embeds, _harvest_cwl_group_clans)
  · Root cause: _harvest_cwl_group_clans is called from _upsert_enemy_clan_on_war_start, which is
    only triggered when fetch_clan_war_data saves a war object. During CWL, that function returns
    None early (regular war endpoint → notInWar), so the harvest never ran for active CWL season.
  · Fix: generate_cwlinfo_embeds now fires `asyncio.ensure_future(_harvest_cwl_group_clans(...))`
    after fetching the league group, passing the already-fetched group object directly.
  · _harvest_cwl_group_clans refactored: added optional `league_group=None` keyword arg. When a
    pre-fetched group is provided, the internal get_league_group() API call is skipped.
  · Guard conditions unchanged: harvest only runs for subscribed clans in top-5 CWL leagues.

- Fixed: Spurious WARNING "Failed to update war info" during CWL for /leaderboard command (QBdiscordcmds.py, QBhelperfunctions.py)
  · Root cause 1: /leaderboard command called update_clan_war_info_and_stats() for cwlinfo-mode clans.
    Fix: skip clans whose per_tag_mode is 'cwlinfo' when building clans_needing_updates.
  · Root cause 2 (proper fix): update_clan_war_info_and_stats() returned False when fetch_clan_war_data()
    returned None. None means "no active regular war" (notInWar, CWL prep day, private warlog) — all
    expected, correct states. True failures raise WarDataFetchError (exceptions), never return None.
    Fix: changed return False → return True when war_data is None. False now only comes from
    process_clan_war_data() returning False (a genuine data-processing failure worth logging).
  · Caller log updated: "No active war to update" INFO → "War data processing failed" WARNING
    (only fires for the real failure case, which is now much rarer).

    vs. [OppName](CoC-profile-url)  ‎`#TAG`  ·  League
    `MyClan`  🏚×1 🏰×4 🏯×10　vs　🏯×11 🏛×4  `OppClan`

  Key design decisions and fixes:
  · Embed title + vs. opponent name = blue hyperlinks to CoC clan profile URLs
  · Roster label names in the lineup line = plain text (not links), to avoid crowding

  · Opponent TH roster remains descending (high→low)

    `37⭐ – 42⭐ · 87.3% – 98.4%`

- Fixed: TH lineup emojis not rendering in currentwar leaderboard (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  Root cause: the entire leaderboard text was wrapped in ``` code blocks before posting;
  Discord does not render custom emojis (<:NAME:ID>) inside code blocks — they show as raw text.
  Fix: introduced _PLAIN_SENTINEL_START / _PLAIN_SENTINEL_END byte-sequence markers that
  are embedded around the roster emoji lines inside generate_war_info_text(). In
  _split_and_post_leaderboard_helper() these sentinel-bounded segments are extracted and
  posted as plain (non-code-block) Discord messages, while the rest of the leaderboard
  continues to be posted in ``` code blocks for aligned monospace rendering.
  Result: TH emojis now render visually in Discord; all 1414 tests pass.

- Improved: War prediction model — league-as-base with TH-diff delta
  Replaces the previous 50/50 blend with a cleaner two-stage model:
  1. clashspot.net league distribution is used as the BASE probability
     (represents the actual observed 3★/2★/1★/0★ rates for that CWL tier).
  2. A per-bucket TH-diff delta (th_probs[diff] − th_probs[0]) is added on top,
     shifting the distribution toward better or worse outcomes while the league
     data anchors the overall skill level.  At diff=0 the delta is zero and the
     league distribution is used unchanged.
  Result: the model correctly weights empirical data over a mechanical 50/50 blend,
  while still capturing the direction and magnitude of TH advantage/disadvantage.
  Falls back to pure TH-diff table when no league is known (unchanged behaviour).
- Added: _ensure_clan_war_league() async helper (QBhelperfunctions.py)
  Resolves the tracked clan's CWL war league by trying, in order:
    1. clan_name_cache (DB-backed — fast, no API call)
    2. Live API via CACHE.coc_clan_cache.get_clan() if missing
    3. Opponent's league as last resort (very rare edge case)
  Called inside fetch_clan_war_data after saving the war object, ensuring that
  generate_war_info_text always has a league available for the prediction model.
- Updated: generate_war_info_text — opponent-league sync fallback
  Adds a third sync fallback in the cwl_league resolution block: reads the
  opponent's war_league from clan_name_cache when the tracked clan's league is
  still unavailable after the async cycle.  All 1414 tests pass.

- Improved: War prediction system — league-calibrated attack probabilities
  - Added CWL_LEAGUE_STAR_DISTRIBUTION constant in QBwarsim.py with empirical
    star-distribution data for all 18 CWL leagues (source: clashspot.net, 2026-02).
    Columns: [p_0★+missed, p_1★, p_2★, p_3★]. Ranges from ~78% 3-star (Champion I)
    down to ~14% 3-star (Bronze III), reflecting the real skill gap between tiers.
  - Extracted _th_star_probs_by_diff() internal helper; th_star_probabilities() now
    accepts optional cwl_league parameter and returns a 50/50 blend of the TH-diff
    model and the empirical league baseline when a recognised league name is provided.
    Falls back to pure TH-diff model (unchanged behaviour) when cwl_league is None.
  - Threaded cwl_league through simulate_attacks_dynamic, monte_carlo_war_prediction,
    and calculate_win_probability (all as optional kwargs, fully backward-compatible).
  - QBhelperfunctions.generate_war_info_text now reads the clan's CWL war league from
    the in-memory clan cache (synchronous, no extra API call) and passes it through
    to calculate_win_probability, enabling league-aware predictions at runtime.
  - Updated two test mocks to accept the new cwl_league kwarg. 1414 tests pass.

- Added: Persistent clan tracking system — war_league storage + track_war_updates flag
  Background: warLeague (CWL tier) is returned ONLY by GET /clans/{tag}, not by any
  war endpoint, so it must be persisted in the DB to survive restarts and be available
  during war-result display without an extra API round-trip.
  - DB (db_manager.py): Added war_league TEXT and track_war_updates BOOLEAN (DEFAULT 1)
    to the clans table schema. Idempotent ALTER TABLE migrations added (try/except).
    ON CONFLICT uses COALESCE(excluded.war_league, war_league) to keep existing value
    when the new upsert omits it; MAX(...) ensures track_war_updates can only go
    True→True or False→True, never True→False (prevents active clans from becoming
    silent when re-inserted as a war enemy).
  - coc_cache._update_clan_metadata: dirty-tracks war_league changes; writes to
    clan_name_cache so it is picked up by the next persist_clan call.
  - cache_manager.persist_clan: now passes war_league and track_war_updates to
    db_manager.save_clan. get_league_group() async wrapper added.
  - QBhelperfunctions: Added _CWL_HARVEST_LEAGUES constant (top 5 leagues),
    _upsert_enemy_clan_on_war_start() (fetches enemy clan info, upserts with
    track_war_updates=False), and _harvest_cwl_group_clans() (fetches full CWL group
    for high-league tracked clans and upserts all group members). Both called after
    each new war JSON is saved, guarded by "if enemy not already in clan_name_cache".
  - generate_war_info_text: war_league now read from clan_name_cache (primary, DB-
    backed) with fallback to in-memory coc.Clan object — more reliable than live
    coc_clan_cache access.
  - QapBot.py polling loop: enemy-only clans (track_war_updates=False) are now
    skipped entirely in the categorization loop, preventing unnecessary API polling
    of ~40,000 passively-harvested clans.
  - All 1414 tests pass.

- Added: Member role SIMPLE/STRICT mode toggle + cyclic member role sync
  - New config key "member_role_strict" (bool, default False) per guild.
  - SIMPLE mode (default): member role is assigned as soon as a player account is
    linked to any member clan/family — no API verification required.
  - STRICT mode: member role requires API verification (or admin_override), same as
    the previous hard-coded behaviour.
  - UI: RoleConfigurationView (Configure Roles) has a new mode toggle button (row 3).
    Button label reflects current mode (🔒 Switch to Strict / 🔓 Switch to Simple).

  - Mode-aware embed: _format_clan_management_roles shows "Mode: Simple / Strict"

  - Cyclic sync (sync_roles_for_user / sync_all_roles_for_guild): now also checks

- Fixed: coc_cache._do_role_sync() lacked DEV mode guild filter
  - Root cause: _schedule_role_sync_for_clan() fires a background asyncio task on
    every real CoC API fetch. The task iterated ALL guilds in server_config and called
    sync_roles_for_clan_members() for each one — no DEV mode guard was present.
    In DEV mode the bot is connected to the dev guild only, but role changes were being
    triggered for every production guild whose config happened to reference the same clan.
  - Fix (coc_cache.py): Added `if CONFIG.is_dev_mode and int(guild_id_str) != CONFIG.discord_guild_id: continue`
    guard inside _do_role_sync(), mirroring the identical guard in the QapBot.py periodic loop.
  - Files changed: qapbot/coc_cache.py

- Fixed: config.py load_dotenv(override=True) broke test isolation and blocked DEV startup
  - Root cause 1 (test isolation): load_dotenv(override=True) at module top re-injected .env
    values (including SLEEP_INTERVAL=30) when config.py was freshly imported inside tests
    that used `patch.dict(os.environ, ..., clear=True)`, overriding the test's own patched
    values and causing 3 TestLoadConfigProd / TestLoadConfigDev tests to fail.
  - Root cause 2 (DEV startup): SLEEP_INTERVAL minimum was unconditionally >= 60 s, blocking
    bot startup when .env has SLEEP_INTERVAL=30 for faster DEV cycles.
  - Fix 1 (config.py): Changed load_dotenv(override=True) → load_dotenv(override=False).
    OS/patched env vars now take priority over .env values; production behaviour is unchanged
    because production has no conflicting OS-level env vars.
  - Fix 2 (config.py): SLEEP_INTERVAL minimum is now 10 s in DEV mode, 60 s in PROD mode.
    Error message updated to reflect mode-specific minimum.
  - Files changed: qapbot/config.py


  - Root cause: _update_warlog_status() (called after get_clan) trusted
    clan.is_war_log_public=True and updated cache False→True. But some clans return
    True in the clan endpoint while get_current_war() raises PrivateWarLog in the
    same cycle, causing an immediate flip back to False by the PRIVATE-WARLOG handler.
  - Fix (coc_cache.py): _update_warlog_status() now returns early when is_public=True.
    Only the False (private) direction is handled here. The True (public) direction is
    exclusively owned by the war fetch success path in QBhelperfunctions, which is the
    authoritative signal for marking a clan public.
  - New test: test_skips_public_update_even_when_cache_is_false covers the flip-flop scenario.

- Fixed: Redundant double [DB-WRITE] WARNING for same event

    ("saved placeholder" + "using placeholder") in the same else-block for a single event.
  - Fix (db_manager.py): Collapsed to a single descriptive WARNING message.

2026-02-22 (Date)
- Fixed: CoC in-game role wrongly assigned to users not in any guild-tracked clan
  - Root cause: _get_highest_coc_role_for_user() iterated ALL of the user's player
    accounts globally (across every clan), so a user who is e.g. Leader in an
    unrelated clan would receive the @CoC Leader role in the Stay server too.
  - Fix (guild_role_manager.py): added guild_clans: Optional[Set[str]] parameter to
    _get_highest_coc_role_for_user(). Players whose current_clan_tag is not in the
    guild's tracked clan set (member_clans + all family clans) are now skipped → the
    function returns None → all four CoC roles are removed for that user.
  - sync_roles_for_user() now computes guild_member_clans once (before both the CoC
    role and clan role blocks), removing the duplicate computation that was only in
    the clan_role block. The set is passed into _get_highest_coc_role_for_user().
  - Tests: 3 new cases in TestGetHighestCocRole covering filter-excludes-unrelated,
    filter-includes-matching, and filter-picks-highest-among-multiple-tracked clans.

- Added: /admin "Refresh Data" subcommand (bot admin only) to force-refresh all tracked clans

    with value REFRESH_DATA added to the /admin command in QBdiscordcmds.py.
  - Restricted to BOT admin via check_bot_admin_only(interaction, CONFIG.server_admin).
  - Collects every unique clan tag across ALL guilds by iterating CACHE.server_config and
    calling get_guild_clans_including_member_config() per guild (covers subscribed, family,
    and member_clans).
  - Invalidates each tag in coc_clan_cache, then parallel-fetches all via asyncio.gather
    with return_exceptions=True so a single failing clan doesn't abort the whole operation.
  - Reports success/failure count back to the invoking admin as an ephemeral message.

- Added: 🔄 Refresh button in "My Accounts" view for non-admin users
  - Users can now force-refresh their own player data from the CoC API without waiting for
    the 30-min/12-h periodic background sync cycle.

- Fixed: Refresh button in registrations/notifications mode could show data up to 280 s stale
  - Root cause: ClanManagementView._on_refresh() called format_clan_management_message() directly
    without evicting the in-memory clan cache first.  The CoCClanCache stale-while-revalidate
    strategy returns cached data within soft_ttl (280 s) without touching the API, so an admin
    who clicked Refresh could still see almost 5-minute-old TH levels, coc_role values, and
    current_clan_tag data.
  - Fix 1 (coc_cache.py): CoCClanCache.invalidate(clan_tag) already existed; improved docstring
    to document the admin-refresh use case.
  - Fix 2 (ui_clan_management.py _on_refresh): Invalidate all guild clans (subscribed +
    member_families + member_clans) and parallel-fetch them via asyncio.gather for every
    mode, so both coc_clan_cache and clan_name_cache are fully up-to-date before the embed
    is rendered.  return_exceptions=True prevents a single failing clan from aborting the
    whole refresh.
  - Result: Clicking Refresh in any mode always shows fully up-to-date data for all clans
    connected to the server.

- Fixed: Auto-role staleness — promotions, demotions and clan changes now reflected within 30 min
  - Root cause 1: get_clan() gate was a flat 12 h for all clans; a player promoted to co-leader
    would keep their old Discord role for up to 12 h + 5 min.
  - Fix 1 (QBhelperfunctions.py fetch_clan_war_data): gate is now dynamic — 30 min (1800 s) for
    any clan that is a member_clan or inside a member_family of a guild with coc_role_enabled or
    clan_role_enabled; 12 h for all other clans. Adds one CACHE.server_config scan per fetch call
    (in-memory dict walk, negligible cost); no extra CoC API calls for non-role clans.
  - Root cause 2: update_player_info_in_user_accounts() only updated players found IN the fetched
    member list; departed players kept their old current_clan_tag forever, so stale clan roles
    were never removed (scenario C — silent permanent staleness).
  - Fix 2 (coc_cache.py update_player_info_in_user_accounts): after processing present members,
    a second pass finds every registered player whose current_clan_tag == the fetched clan but
    who is absent from the member list, and sets current_clan_tag = None. This immediately
    triggers removal of the old clan role on the next role sync (within 5 min of the clan refresh).
  - Combined worst-case staleness: 30 min (gate) + 5 min (periodic sync) = ~35 min for any
    role-relevant clan, down from ~12 h 5 min (promotions/departures) / indefinite (departures
    to untracked clans).

- Fixed: Returning members did not get CoC/clan roles restored immediately on rejoin
  - Root cause: on_member_join only called assign_newbie_role(); sync_roles_for_user() was never
    called, so roles were only restored on the next periodic cycle (~5 min later).
  - Fix (QapBot.py on_member_join): After newbie role, check if coc_role_enabled or
    clan_role_enabled AND the user already has registered accounts; if so call sync_roles_for_user()
    immediately with the pre-fetched member object (no extra Discord API call needed).
  - Result: Returning members with linked accounts have roles restored instantly.

- Added: Family management view now triggers clan-role sync on clan add/remove and family delete
  - Root cause: EditFamilyView._on_save and _execute_delete_family had no clan-role handling at
    all; adding/removing clans from a family or deleting a family never updated Discord roles.
  - Fix 1 (ui_clan_management.py): Extracted shared helper _handle_clan_role_changes_for_guild()
    that computes added/removed coverage, creates roles for new clans, calls sync_all_roles_for_guild,
    and returns removable_tags (lost clans that have a stored Discord role).
  - Fix 2 (EditFamilyView._on_save): Defer at top; capture old_family_clans before CACHE write;
    after subscription update compute old/new guild coverage, call helper, show
    ConfirmDeleteClanRolesView followup if any roles can be deleted.
  - Fix 3 (_execute_delete_family): Capture pre-deletion guild coverage before any CACHE mutation;
    after all cleanup steps compute post-deletion coverage, call helper, show ConfirmDeleteClanRolesView
    followup if any roles can be deleted.
  - Note: CreateFamilyModal not changed — new families start empty, so no role changes needed.
  - Result: All three family management paths are now consistent with MemberClansConfigurationView.

- Fixed: New clan/family roles not assigned to existing members until next background sync cycle
  - Root cause: MemberClansConfigurationView._on_apply() created the Discord role for a newly
    added individual clan but never called sync_all_roles_for_guild(), so users already in that
    clan only got the role on the next periodic main-loop cycle.
    Newly added families had no role-creation step at all (only individual clans were handled).
  - Fix (ui_clan_management.py _on_apply): capture old_member_families alongside old_member_clans;
    after creating roles for new individual clans, also create roles for all clans in any newly
    added family; then call sync_all_roles_for_guild() whenever the clan or family set changed.
  - Result: Roles are assigned to all affected guild members immediately when Apply is pressed.

- Fixed: War reminder loop spammed 23 ERROR + 23 WARNING for unlinked player "Conan2502"

    with discord_id = "Userver-machineSIGNED", then passed "Userver-machineSIGNED" to cache_manager.fetch_user() →
    int("Userver-machineSIGNED") raised ValueError every ~5 minutes for the full war duration (16:17–17:56).

    to skip unlinked players (Userver-machineSIGNED or any non-numeric id) before grouping, with a DEBUG log.

  - Root cause: fetch_clan_war_data() in QBhelperfunctions.py skipped the get_clan() API call
    if last_checked_via_api age was under 172800 s (2 days). This means coc_role memory values
    (and thus role sync) were only refreshed every 2 days.

- Added: Automatic role sync on every CoC clan API fetch

    fire-and-forget asyncio task after every real API fetch in _update_clan_metadata().
    For each guild that has this clan as a member clan and has coc_role_enabled or
    clan_role_enabled, calls guild_role_manager.sync_roles_for_clan_members() so role
    assignments stay current without waiting for the next periodic main-loop sync.

- Fixed: CoC in-game role never assigned because str(coc.Role) returns title-cased strings
  - Root cause: coc_cache.py used str(member.role) to get the role string. But:
      str(coc.Role.leader)   == "Leader"   (≠ our key "leader")
      str(coc.Role.member)   == "Member"   (≠ our key "member")
      coc.Role.elder.value   == "admin"    (≠ our key "elder")
      coc.Role.co_leader.value == "coLeader" (✅ matches, but only this one)
    None of the str() values matched COC_ROLE_PRIORITY keys → coc_role was always set
    to an unrecognised string → _get_highest_coc_role_for_user() returned None → no role.
  - Fix (coc_cache.py update_player_info_in_user_accounts):
    Use role.name instead: "member", "elder", "co_leader", "leader".
    Map "co_leader" → "coLeader" so it matches the COC_ROLE_PRIORITY/CONFIG_KEY dicts.
    new_coc_role = ("coLeader" if raw_name == "co_leader" else raw_name) if raw_name else None

- Fixed: CoC in-game roles not assigned by periodic main loop after restart
  - Root cause: coc_role is memory-only (never persisted); cleared on each restart.
    fetch_clan_war_data() only calls coc_clan_cache.get_clan() every 2 days (throttle),
    so on most cycles after restart coc_role stays None → role sync assigns nothing.
  - Fix: Added module-level _coc_role_refreshed_clans: set[str] in guild_role_manager.py.
    sync_all_roles_for_guild() now force-calls coc_clan_cache.get_clan() for each member
    clan not yet in the set (i.e. not yet refreshed since startup), then adds it.
    Each clan is bootstrapped at most once per process lifetime — subsequent cycles
    read coc_role from the in-memory CACHE, which is kept current by normal 2-day fetches.
  - Result: On first role sync after restart, all member clans' roles are populated
    before iterating users → CoC in-game roles are assigned correctly.
  - Log line: [ROLE-SYNC] Bootstrapping CoC role data for N clan(s) (first sync after startup)
